# Character Art Spec — Cartoon Animator 5 (CA5) → activity characters

**The standing contract for every activity character you render in CA5.** Hand me a folder of transparent PNG sequences per the rules below; I run one build command and the character animates in the activity with no further art work. Until you deliver a character, the activity plays on a hand-drawn SVG placeholder, so nothing is blocked.

> Dual-asset doctrine (2026-06-23): **you** make the **characters** (this doc). **I** pull the **countable props** (food, animals, objects) straight from the 3,000-image library. Bowls/trays/UI stay code-drawn.

---

## The universal contract (every character)

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| **Frame canvas** | **512 × 512 px**, square |
| **Background** | **Transparent** (PNG with alpha — no white/solid backdrop) |
| **Format** | PNG sequence, one set of files per animation |
| **Naming** | `<pose>_0001.png`, `<pose>_0002.png`, … (zero-padded 4 digits) |
| **fps** | **12** |
| **Centering** | Keep the character at a **consistent position + scale across every pose** so it doesn't jump between animations (the build auto-trims/recenters, but a steady anchor keeps it calm). |
| **Delivery** | All the PNG sequences in **one folder**. Tell me the folder path. |

Each **animation** is its own PNG sequence sharing a pose name. A "loop" animation should be drawn so the **last frame flows back into the first** (seamless). A "play-once" animation plays a single time on its trigger, then the character returns to idle.

After you deliver, I run:
```
node scripts/games/build-sprites.js --in <your-folder> --sheet <character> --out "mini tools/sprites" --fps 12
```
…which packs the frames into one WebP sprite-sheet + a manifest + CSS, and wires it into the activity.

---

## Character #1 — **Mochi** (for "Mochi's Counting Feast")

A soft, round, squishy dumpling creature (think a plump mochi rice-cake with a little face). Warm, cute, K-3 friendly. Teal body, coral cheeks — but render Mochi however reads as most lovable; the activity adapts.

**Animations — the LEAN set (you confirmed this scope):**

| pose name | what Mochi does | loops? | frames @ 12fps | ≈ length |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **idle** | gentle breathe + an occasional blink | **LOOPS** | **12** | ~1.0 s |
| **nom** | one happy chew/bite | plays once | **6** | ~0.5 s |
| **happy** | delighted wiggle, eyes happy-closed (^ ^) | plays once | **10** | ~0.8 s |

- **idle** plays whenever Mochi is just sitting there waiting.
- **nom** fires **once each time a child drops a treat into the bowl** (so it should read as a quick single bite, then settle).
- **happy** fires **once when the child feeds the exactly-right number** and the feast is complete.

**Total: ~28 frames.** Files like `idle_0001.png … idle_0012.png`, `nom_0001.png … nom_0006.png`, `happy_0001.png … happy_0010.png`, all 512×512 transparent, in one folder.

That's everything — over-feeding just reuses the idle pose, so no extra animation is needed.

## Character #2 — **Pim** (for "The Echo Grove")

A small, round, friendly OWL who tends a magical grove. Warm, K-3 friendly — a curious grove-keeper, NOT a stern wise owl (round ringed eyes, soft ear-tufts, never slits/horns). Teal body, coral cheeks + beak, big friendly eyes.

**Animations — the LEAN set (same shape as Mochi):**

| pose name | what Pim does | loops? | frames @ 12fps | ≈ length |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **idle** | gentle breathe + an occasional blink | **LOOPS** | **12** | ~1.0 s |
| **look** | glances down toward the groves (a small head-tilt / pupils drop) — "read these" | plays once, then holds | **8** | ~0.7 s |
| **cheer** | delighted — wings up, eyes happy-closed (^ ^), a little hop | plays once | **10** | ~0.8 s |

- **idle** while the child is reading/choosing.
- **look** fires when the child taps a wrong grove (Pim looks toward the groves to gently re-direct — no scolding).
- **cheer** fires when the child picks the correct grove.

**Total: ~30 frames.** Files `idle_0001…0012.png`, `look_0001…0008.png`, `cheer_0001…0010.png`, all 512×512 transparent, one folder. Same build command (`build-sprites.js --sheet pim`).

## Characters #4–#6 — **Grandpa Pip · Wee Olen · Doot** (for "Grandpa Pip's Nesting Pots")

A hand-carving workshop. **Grandpa Pip** = a kindly old maker (round body, soft cap/hair, white beard) who names the numbers + gives warm hints. **Wee Olen** = the baby Tuckling who peeks out of the open pot-mouth (the live target). **Doot** = a cheerful round duck who waddles + gives a gentle "hmm?" on a wrong tuck (never scolding). Same warm K-3 register as Mochi/Pim/Tessa. Until delivered, all three play on hand-drawn SVG placeholders (the `_setPose` seam is wired).

| character | pose | what it does | loops? | frames @12fps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Grandpa Pip** | **idle** | gentle breathe + blink | LOOPS | 12 |
| | **talk** | names a number / gives a hint (mouth moves) | plays once | 8 |
| **Wee Olen** | **peek** | peeks up out of the open pot, blinks | LOOPS | 10 |
| | **wave** | happy wave when the family is all tucked in | plays once | 8 |
| **Doot** | **idle** | gentle bob | LOOPS | 8 |
| | **wobble** | a comic head-tilt/"hmm?" on a wrong tuck | plays once | 6 |

**Pots (props, NOT characters — static art, no animation needed):** a **pot body ×5 sizes** (`pot-1` smallest … `pot-5` biggest), each 512×512 transparent, terracotta, with an EMPTY belly (the number is drawn by the app as DOM over the belly — never bake a number into the art). One **lid** (optional, for the round-complete "tuck-in"). Deliver the 5 pot bodies + lid as static PNGs in the same folder. Build: `build-sprites.js --sheet pip` (characters) — the pots are referenced as static images.

## Character #9 — **Captain Quill** (for "Comparison Creek")

A small, over-confident but lovable DUCKLING river-captain who steers a little raft down a forking creek. Warm, plucky, eager (NOT a stern sea-captain) — round yellow body, soft round head, a teal captain's cap with a coral button, a coral beak, big friendly eyes. He rides the trunk current at the bottom of the fork and reacts as the child picks a channel. Yellow body, teal cap, coral accents. Until delivered he plays on a hand-drawn SVG placeholder (the `_setPose` seam is wired: idle/happy).

| pose | what Captain Quill does | loops? | frames @12fps |
|---|---|---|---|
| **idle** | gentle bob on the current + an occasional blink, cap settles | LOOPS | 12 |
| **peer** | leans / shades his eyes to read the two buoys ("which way…?") at the start of a bend | plays once | 8 |
| **steer** | a determined little lean as he commits to a channel (on tap) | plays once | 6 |
| **cheer** | delighted — eyes happy-closed (^ ^), a happy wing-flap — on a correct steer | plays once | 10 |
| **re-read** | a soft "oops, let me look again" head-tilt on a wrong steer (no shame — paired with the buoy position-flip) | plays once | 8 |

**Total ~44 frames.** Files `idle_0001…0012.png` etc., 512×512 transparent, one folder. Build: `build-sprites.js --sheet quill`. (Props — the water Y, buoys, reed island, raft, `=` beacon, MORE/LESS buttons, dashed current — are all code-drawn CSS/SVG; no art needed. A friendly **Buckley** beaver was considered for the wrong-steer beat but is NOT needed — Captain Quill's own **re-read** pose carries it.)

## Character #10 — **Fixit** (for "Fixit's Fix-It Corner")

A near-sighted, lovable REPAIR-MOLE who runs a cozy basement gadget-repair bench — the inverted power dynamic IS the heart: Fixit can't read the tiny number, so the CHILD is his trusted sharp-eyed apprentice (never a tested student). Warm, eager, slightly bumbling (NOT a stern fixer) — round brown body, soft snout, a coral nose, big round slipping SPECTACLES (his defining feature — he holds every gadget close to peer at it), little arms that cradle things to his chest, a small coral worker's cap. Brown/teal palette + coral accents. Until delivered he plays on a hand-drawn SVG placeholder (the `_setPose` seam is wired: idle / squint / happy).

| pose | what Fixit does | loops? | frames @12fps |
|---|---|---|---|
| **idle** | gentle breathe + blink behind his spectacles, holding a gadget close | LOOPS | 12 |
| **squint** | peers hard over his slipping glasses at the part ("I can't quite read it…") on a wrong tap | plays once | 8 |
| **model** | counts it out on his fingers / the rail as he models the computation ("8 plus 4 is 12…") | plays once | 10 |
| **delight** | beams — eyes happy-closed (^ ^), holds the fixed gadget up — when it whirs to life | plays once | 10 |

**Total ~40 frames.** Files `idle_0001…0012.png` etc., 512×512 transparent, one folder. Build: `build-sprites.js --sheet fixit`. (Props — the equation cells, the labeled-vessel whole, the ten-frame, the count-on number-line + marker, the fact-family triangle, the recombine dot-piles, the balance-beam, all juice [strain/catch/POP/whir, over-rev, half-turn-stall, room-brighten] — are all code-drawn CSS/SVG/WebAudio; no art needed. **Gadgets** = 8–10 little contraptions, each as a broken/repaired PAIR [dim+sputtering → bright + doing a SPECIFIC silly job]; deliver as static PNGs, the app draws the numbers as DOM over them.)

## Character #12 — **Judge Tess + the witnesses + court cat** (for "Numbers Court")

A cosy candlelit miniature courtroom where the child is the **Judge**. **Tess** = an ancient, kind TORTOISE in an oversized powdered judge's WIG (the visual comedy) — warm, patient, twinkly; she reads each math claim *relationally* ("is this the same amount as…?") and turns to the child. Green tortoise, cream/teal shell, the big white wig her defining feature. The **witnesses** make the claims: **Mr. Hopscotch** (earnest rabbit, off-by-one mistakes), **Crow** (boastful, sneaky-false), **Pip** (an over-cautious mouse who calls TRUE things false → the child DEFENDS the truth + overrules him), **Mr. Tilt** (a beaver — the misconception incarnate, his wrong sum literally piles onto one pan). The **court cat** = the monotonic vessel (sleeps → stirs → stretches → curls up awake on the settled-case scrolls as cases are judged). All placeholders now (Tess wired to `_setPose` idle/happy; witnesses are emoji stand-ins; the cat is the scroll-bench progress).

| character | pose | what it does | loops? | frames @12fps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Tess** | **idle** | gentle breathe + blink under the wig | LOOPS | 12 |
| | **turn-to-camera** | turns to the child — "What do you say, Judge?" | plays once | 8 |
| | **pleased** | warm nod + eyes happy-closed (^ ^) on a settled case | plays once | 10 |
| **Hopscotch** | step-up / **sheepish double-take** (caught) | plays once | 8 |
| **Crow** | boast / **caught-grin** | plays once | 8 |
| **Pip** | anxious / doubting / **beaming** (truth defended) | plays once | 10 |
| **Mr. Tilt** | confident / **piles-his-sum** (his sum slams a pan) / blink-then-grin | plays once | 10 |
| **court cat** | **sleep → stir → stretch → awake** (the 4-state vessel) | staged | 12 |

**Total ~80 frames across the cast.** Files `idle_0001…0012.png` etc., 512×512 transparent, one folder per character. Build: `build-sprites.js --sheet tess` (+ `--sheet hopscotch`/`crow`/`pip`/`tilt`/`courtcat`). (Props — the brass balance beam + fulcrum + pans, the equation tokens, the deposit counters, the repair "?" slot + tile tray, the candles/lanterns, the scroll bench, all juice [beam settle/tip, gavel] — are all code-drawn CSS/SVG/WebAudio; no art needed.)

## Character #13 — **Sock + Shadow + the customers** (for "Sock & Shadow")

The Lost-and-Found Puppet Theater. **SOCK** works the front counter and CAN see the bin — a warm, eager coral sock-puppet (the child is Sock's voice). **SHADOW** works BEHIND THE CURTAIN and CANNOT see — a gentle teal puppet who only HEARS the words, then emerges to fetch what they described. The physical can't-see asymmetry IS the pedagogy: a vague turn → Shadow honestly holds BOTH cups, worried ("which one? I can't tell from back here!"). Shadow's confusion is ALWAYS empathetic/apologetic, never a buzzer — the miss is the message's fault. The **customers** are lost kids (happy when reunited, upset when their thing is missing). The **Lost-and-Found shelf** fills with reunited objects (the vessel). All placeholders now (Sock/Shadow are SVG sock-puppets; the curtain + bin objects + shelf are CSS/SVG).

| character | pose | what it does | loops? | frames @12fps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Sock** | **idle** | gentle sway at the counter | LOOPS | 12 |
| | **mouth-flap** | mouth opens/closes while the turn is voiced (gated to TTS start/end) | LOOPS while speaking | 6 |
| | **bow** | a little bow on a reunion | plays once | 8 |
| **Shadow** | **duck-behind** | ducks behind the curtain, the bin greys on Shadow's side (the ocular asymmetry) | plays once | 8 |
| | **emerge** | parts the curtain + steps out to act | plays once | 8 |
| | **holding-one** | holds the single fetched object up, pleased | plays once | 8 |
| | **holding-two (worried)** | holds BOTH matching objects, head-swivel, apologetic "which one?" | plays once | 10 |
| | **overwhelmed** | hands full, "too many words — I forgot half!" | plays once | 8 |
| **lost kid** | **upset → reunited-beam** | the customer lights up when their object comes back | staged | 10 |

**Total ~80 frames across the cast.** Files `idle_0001…0012.png` etc., 512×512 transparent, one folder per character. Build: `build-sprites.js --sheet sock` (+ `--sheet shadow`/`lostkid`). (Props — the proscenium + the teal **CURTAIN** [closed / parting / open + a grey-overlay state on Shadow's side], the bin + its objects [cups/balls/hats/teddies/cars/blocks in colour × size variants — drawn now as tinted+scaled SVG stubs], the "Sock's words" tray, the chip rail, the Lost-and-Found shelf, all juice — are code-drawn CSS/SVG/WebAudio. The curtain art especially: a real fabric curtain Shadow visibly ducks behind will land the "can't see" metaphor the CSS stub only hints at.)

## Future characters

Same universal contract (512², transparent PNG sequences, 12fps, one folder). Only the **pose list** changes to fit the activity. Typical shape: one **idle (loop, ~12 frames)** + one or two **reaction one-shots (~6–10 frames each)**. When we get to the next activity I'll give you its character's exact pose list the same way.

## Character #15 — Pip the sleepy friend (Game #10 "Wake Up, Pip!", RL.K.2 retell)

**Pip** — a soft round creature (currently a lavender CSS/SVG blob with little ear-nubs; stub for CA5). The eyewitness's audience: naps through every story, wakes desperate to be told what happened. **6 mood frames + idle blink:** `asleep` (closed eyes, "z z" floaties), `open`/`awake` (neutral, listening), `hmm` (curious head-tilt — the warm diffuse-confusion state, NEVER disappointed), `happy`, `delighted` (leaning in, gasp — the "I was THERE!" win), + a blink idle. ~512², transparent, 12fps, one folder.

**Per-story cast (8 EN stories) — silhouette stubs (emoji placeholders now):** sleepy Pip + sun; Mira + kite + tree; the thirsty crow + jug; Duke the puppy + Sam + tub; Lina + the red mitten (snow); Theo + Mom + cake; Gus + seed→sprout→flower; Ada + the blue paint→sky. Each story = **4 diorama PANELS** (a silhouette cast on a setting-tint scene card) that DOUBLE as the faded wordless retell stubs. Culture-light silhouettes (shared across locales); the STORIES are natively authored per locale.

**Props / juice (code-drawn CSS/SVG/WebAudio, NOT CA5):** the **stub-mirror bubble** over Pip (a row of small silhouette pips that resolve on a clean retell / tangle on a break — leak-safe, never per-slot); the **storybook vessel** (empty → gaining one glowing illustrated page per round); the numbered retell ladder + the emoji option tiles. The panels are the only real CA5 art ask (8 stories × 4 panels + a handful of distractor panels).

## Character #18 — The Lantern-Keeper firefly (Game #13 "Count the Stars Awake", K.CC.A.2 draw-trace)

**The firefly** — a soft glowing teardrop (currently a coral SVG glow-circle; stub for CA5) who is *stuck* and can only fly where the child has already counted. She perches trembling on the last lit star, looks into the dark, and waits. **Moods/poses:** `perched-trembling` (idle on the lit star), `fly-forward` (a hop to the next star as the count climbs), `nestle` (the win — she settles onto the woken creature: rides the deer's antler, sits in the owl's ear). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** the **deep-teal night panel** (`#0E4A40` inset); the **stars** (dim `<circle>` when dark/un-counted — carrying NO number; gold glow + the numeral as `<text>` once counted); the **coral thread** (`<polyline>` through the woken stars — drawn in an **anti-proximity zigzag** so the path never follows nearest-neighbor and the reveal is a mild surprise) + a dashed gold **live segment** trailing the firefly; the climbing **numeral** (real DOM, TTS each step); the coral **"Count on"** beat + the teal **"Wake it!"** stop; the overshoot **glide-back**. CA5 asks: the firefly moods (perched/fly-forward/**nestle-onto-the-creature**), the **star-wake twinkle**, the **thread-draws-itself + color-flood reveal** sequence, the **per-creature constellation outlines** (fox/owl/whale/deer/bear/cat/bird — authored to the ≤9-dot budget + anti-proximity geometry), and the **night-sky scrapbook** vessel (empty → filling → dawn; currently a thin ✦ row, the GameCollection `night-sky` reward is the keepsake). **Fast-follow:** the **alphabet A→B→C sibling** (its own L.K.1.a/RF.K.1.d activity) + the drag-the-firefly input + the wordless worked-example intro animation.

## Character #19 — The two foxes & the necklace (Game #14 "The Necklace That Won't Hold Still", K.CC.B.4 mimic-copy)

**The two foxes** — a little fox (the child's stand-in) makes a bead necklace to match its **friend fox's** — not the same colors, the same *how-many*. Currently a single warm-orange SVG fox head (idle / happy) as the speech-bubble avatar; stub for CA5. **Moods/poses:** `idle` (watching, ears up), `tuck-it-away` (the friend playfully hides the just-counted necklace — the count-then-hide spine), `shake-it` (the friend gives the necklace a mischievous shake so the beads bunch — narrated BEFORE it happens, never a silent loss), `nose-touch` (the win — the two foxes touch noses, both necklaces glowing the same warm pulse: *"we're singing the same number"*). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** the **beads** (glossy `<circle>` discs with a gloss ellipse + inner shadow; the model in one warm color, the child's in another — **color is NOISE**, count-only grading); the **cord** (a wrapped row of bead slots; the next empty slot is the **gold "ready" bead** that pulses — tap it to thread one, +1 count + a spoken number word; tap a bead to remove one); the **clasp** 🔗 (dim until ≥1 bead, the coral clasp-shut + unison-chime on count-equality); the **scatter pile** (a lineless disordered count for the hard one-to-one); the **counted-tick** ✓ + the climbing readout (real DOM, TTS each tap). CA5 asks: the fox moods (idle/**tuck-away**/**shake**/**nose-touch**), the **bead-arcs-onto-the-cord** thread animation + the "fly to the counted tray" for the scatter round, the **clasp-shut + unison-chime** sequence, and the **friendship-branch** vessel (the GameCollection `friendship-branch` reward — finished necklaces hang on a branch, empty → filling, each tap replays its count-chime). **Fast-follow:** per-locale fox lines + number-word TTS (`[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi); the drag-onto-cord desktop enhancement; the K.NBT.A.1 ten-and-ones (11–20 two-bar Rekenrek) future extension (the only thing justifying a longer cord).

## Character #20 — The wondering owl & the jar (Game #15 "The Wondering Jar", K.CC.B.5 estimate-jar)

**The wondering owl** — a soft purple sweet-shop owl who loves to guess jars ("Hoooo many, do you think?"). Currently an SVG owl head (idle / happy / **wow** big-eyed at the reveal); stub for CA5. **Moods/poses:** `idle` (curious, ear-tufts up), `lets-count` (leaning toward the spread sweets), `wow` (eyes wide at the reveal — the 5-year-old "HOW MANY?!" beat, the most art budget per the spec). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** the **jar** — at the estimate stage it's CLUMPED/frosted (a charming glass vessel with overlapping drifting sweets + a soft magic-glow; genuinely uncountable, NOT a grey blur — the clump-then-spread leak-guard); at the count stage it has SPREAD all its sweets into the round's arrangement. The **sweets** = glossy `<circle>` discs in 5 warm hues (dim + a white ✓ check when counted). The **0–20 number line** (teal axis + 0/5/10/15/20 labels + coral benchmark ticks at 5/10/20) with a **⭐ star "wish" thumb** (drag via the shared `LCS.drag.linear` helper) + a coral value pill. The four **count arrangements** — line (wrap row) · array (grid) · circle (a real ring + a coral ▶ "start here" marker) · scatter (a grid-jitter pile). The **reveal/compare** — the persisted wish-pin (⭐ above the line) + the actual-star (teal number pill below) meeting, with a DIRECTION-only verdict ("More than you thought — jars surprise us!", never a distance number). CA5 asks: the owl moods (idle/lets-count/**wow**), the **jar clumped↔spread + the SPREAD animation** (the signature reveal-prep beat), the sweet sprites (+ dim/check), the wish-pin + actual-star + the reveal burst, and the **Wish-Wall shelf** of shelved jars (the `wish-wall` GameCollection keepsake — empty → filling; NO accuracy/gap encoding). **Fast-follow:** per-locale owl lines + number-word TTS (`[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi); the estimate-length/weight/capacity siblings on the same predict-then-check engine; a stacked desktop layout for the relational round (the two-jars+slider row is the busiest frame); high-N (15–20) challenge LINE rounds; the literal animated spread.

CA5 asks: Big & Bit's moods (idle / match-us-up / **bust-whoa** / we-are-the-same / **you-forgot-me**); the fuzzball-friend sprite (+ the buddy-clasp + rung-settle); **THE MISLEADING-BUST sequence (the most art budget — the long line collapsing into rungs while the lonely friend pops out on the short-looking side, the misconception dying on screen)**; the see-saw beam locked→tips→settles; the Friendship Garland filling. **Fast-follow:** per-locale relation lexicon (MORE/FEWER/SAME, the "fewer" register — native-confirmed, a fixed small lexicon NOT a calque) + Big/Bit lines + number-word TTS (`[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi); the drag-yoke enhancement on the same pair path (>360px); the context-variety theme-wave over the fuzzball friends.

CA5 asks: Rivet's moods (idle / **count-it** / **lever-clunk** / wheeze / card-of-nothing); the treasure tokens (+ the count-tap ✓ + **the draw-into-the-forming-numeral** — the load-bearing aha, the most art budget); the forge press + the foil-mint; the binder pages filling → gilding (the `rivets-album` GameCollection keepsake — a shelf of completed-hoard spines); the Card of Nothing; the parade (transient treasure marching past). **The count-into-the-numeral + the mint get the most art budget** (the "3 built from the three things"). **Fast-follow:** the full 11-locale fan-out (Rivet's lines + the §20.10 framework NAME + number-word TTS, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi); the SELECT 0-9 accessibility-floor toggle (never the premium default); the context-variety treasure theme-wave.

## Character #26 — Mama Duck & the ducklings (Game #21 "Mama's Roll Call", K.CC.B.5 count-out-that-many)

**Mama Duck** — a warm matron duck on the far bank of Quack Creek who calls the morning roll; the child sends her exactly the number she asks for. Currently a small SVG duck (idle / happy) — the ducklings are mini geometry ducks; stub for CA5. **Moods/poses:** `idle` (at the bank, calling), `the-call` (a spoken "I need SIX for the lily-pad swim!" — the target, never a counter), `gather-cheer` (**the signature** — the warm double-quack when the roll seals: Mama gathers her whole brood and the formed mud-numeral presses into the bank), `re-do-nudge` (the NON-directional "let's call that roll again" when the set ≠ the call — the ducklings paddle back to a fresh raft; NEVER a high/low/by-how-much signal, never a buzzer), `let-them-sleep` (the 0-call delight — "none this morning, let them sleep in"; a single ripple crosses the still water). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

CA5 asks: Mama's moods (idle / **the-call** / **gather-cheer** / re-do-nudge / let-them-sleep); the duckling (raft-idle + **the paddle-across** [the count-out made visible — the most art budget] + the settle-by-Mama); the creek + the **mud-writing channel** (the ink-under-finger, the formed numeral — the commit, the other art budget); the roll-book bank filling → gilding (the `rollbook` GameCollection keepsake — the child's OWN hand-formed numerals, the wobble kept never "corrected"); the still-water 0-ripple. **Fast-follow:** the full 11-locale fan-out (Mama's lines + the §20.10 framework NAME + number-word TTS, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi) + the **per-locale numeral stroke-path variants (1/4/7/9 — native K-handwriting-validated, amortized program-wide)**; the teens (two-digit formation, tens-frame raft); the richer freeform-trace scoring; the context-variety duckling theme-wave.

## Character #27 — Pim & Bo + the moon-beads (Game #22 "The Sharing Jar", 1.OA.D.8 make-it-fair) — the social-fair FAMILY HEAD

**Pim** (small, hopeful, orange) **& Bo** (tall, gentle, blue) — two friends who scoop a spilled jar of moon-beads and, with no ill intent, one ends up with more. The **emotional arc IS the payload** (this game is the social-fair family head, so the art bar sets the family tone). Currently small SVG friends with pose-swaps; stub for CA5. **Moods/poses:** `idle`, `quiet-dim` (**the felt-wrong** — the friend with fewer doesn't cry, they just go quiet, **ears down**, the jar dim), `reconcile` (**the signature — the math heals the friendship** — the quiet jar brightens to match, the "=" bridge glows, the friends **lean their heads together**), `patient` (the warm "look again" when the child's number is off — never sad-AT-the-child, never a scold), `already-fair` (the 0-case warmth — both content, nobody short). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** the **moon-beads** (`<circle>`+glow geometry, NO image-library → no 404); the **two bead-jars** (the count `<text>` AUTHORITATIVE, beads decorative); **THE PRODUCE-THE-NUMERAL-FIRST SPINE (the family's signature anti-cheat):** the child determines the gap and **taps ONE number-tile (0-10)** — there is **NO basket to fill one-bead-at-a-time against a visible target** (the increment-and-watch perceptual cheat structurally gone); **THE GIVE IS THE CELEBRATION, not the input** — only on a correct numeral do exactly m beads **float from the neutral floor-pile into the quiet jar** (a give from a SHARED source, never robbing the other friend — the social tone, art-enforced), the jar brightens, the **"=" bridge** glows; the **5 schema stagings** (equalize-add level-up / compare-diff a STATIC gap named with a "+N" + gold-highlighted extra beads, NO transfer / restore the faded "ghost" beads + "had N" / reduce-to-target "fair = T" put-back / start-unknown "gave k away" reason-backward); the **ceiling tier** (the bigger jar's numeral hidden "?", beads countable — the true missing-addend); the **friendship-string** garland (each fair moment a 💛). CA5 asks: Pim & Bo's moods (idle / **quiet-dim ears-down** / **reconcile heads-lean** / patient / already-fair); the moon-beads (floor-pile idle + **the float-into-the-jar** + the brighten); the jars dim→full; the "=" bridge; the friendship-string filling→hung. **The float-into-the-jar + the brighten + the heads-lean get the most art budget** — the "I said the kind number and made it fair" aha. **Fast-follow:** the full 11-locale fan-out (the friends' lines + the §20.10 framework NAME + number-word TTS, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi) + the **per-locale EQUALLY-MANY social register** (native-ensemble §A.13.48 — "now they have the same number", NOT a "fair" string-swap; the social-fair family-level inheritance); the within-20 ceiling depth; the give-each-bead-along tap-to-count joy enhancement.

## Character #28 — Clunk the snack-robot & the snack-characters (Game #23 "Clunk's Lost Lunch", K.OA.A.3 decompose-a-target-many-ways + 1.OA.D.8 unknown-addend) — the 3rd collect-trade family member

## Character #29 — Bo the bunny & the berry pantry (Game #24 "Bo's Berry Pantry", 1.NBT.B.2 unitize-two-digit-tens-and-ones) — the 2nd physical-launch sibling

**Bo** — a soft cream bunny stocking his winter pantry up in the lavender dusk; he packs wild berries into crates, and a crate holds exactly ten (sealed, latched, un-countable — "a crate just IS a ten"). The child is his helper: read Bo's berries, then sling them to the matching shelf before the snow. Currently a small SVG bunny head (idle / happy); a SMALL corner sprite, never big-central; stub for CA5. **Moods/poses:** `pack-idle` (ears soft, the patient "pack me a pantry" wait), `the-read` (a spoken "Pack Bo's berries!" — the prompt, never a counter), `thwang-cheer` (**the signature** — the band pulls, the berry-bundle arcs to the LOCKED shelf, and on a correct read the crates crack open and COUNT BY TENS — "ten… twenty… thirty…" — the loose tumble, Bo names the whole number), `count-again` (**the ONE non-leaking negative** — a gentle "that shelf holds {k} crates — count again for Bo!" that names the WRONG shelf's crate-count, NEVER the pebble's value, never high/low; the pebble floats back, positions reshuffle, re-decode not re-aim — no buzzer/red-X), `curl-up-warm` (the soft pantry-full close — Bo curled cozy as the snow falls). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** **THE SEALED UN-COUNTABLE TEN-CRATE (signature, load-bearing — the §9 one-move count-match-killer):** a latched wooden crate stamped with a ten-leaf cluster + a "10" — *obviously ONE ten, you can't count berries inside it*; this kills count-the-berries-inside (a count-non-unitizer thinks 4 crates = 4 things), solves 280px legibility (1 element, not 10 dots), and locks the Game-16 separation (a sealed un-counted unit ≠ a counted ten-frame); the **loose berries** (`<circle>` ones in a 5-frame so 7 reads "five-and-two", subitizable; pure geometry, NO image-library → no 404); the **prompt card** (a loose PILE of berries [no digits] / a value-question word ["twenty-five — A CRATE IS TEN"] / a numeral pebble ["63"] / a hoard [encode]); **THE PANTRY SHELVES** (the lockable hoards — crates + loose, **NO numerals on any hoard shelf** [the digit-match-killer], a coral lock-ring on the chosen one); **THE SLINGSHOT + BAND** (a Y-stub + the coral "Sling it! 🪃" — the ONLY commit; **commit-then-deliver**: the launch reads ONLY the locked shelf, never aim/pull/pointer, so a wrong answer is always a wrong READING, never a wrong aim); **THE LAUNCH ARC** (a CSS-keyframe parabola, no physics, reduced-motion-instant); **THE CRATES-COUNT-BY-TENS PAYOFF** (the opened hoard + the whole-number caption — the place-value celebration; per-locale: count by tens [universal] then the whole word, SKIP the ones-counting interstitial for ones-first de/nl/da/no); the **5 lane stagings** (pack-the-pile decode / decade-zero-ones / unitize-count same-value-most-bundled / value-compare ten-dominates / encode hoard→numeral with a 805 three-digit drift-guard); the **filling PANTRY** (🧺 cubbies — first-attempt-correct stocks a cubby, blind-cycling does NOT). CA5 asks: Bo's moods (pack-idle / **the-read** / **thwang-cheer** / count-again / curl-up-warm); the sealed ten-crate (idle + **the crack-open-count-by-tens** [the unitizing aha — the most art budget]); the loose-berry tumble; the band-THWANG + the CSS arc; the pantry stocking → full → snow-falls; each lane's framing (the pile, the value-question, the hoard) its own small staging. **The crack-open-count-by-tens gets the most art budget** (the "a crate is ten, and I packed it the smart way" aha). **Fast-follow:** the full 11-locale fan-out (Bo's lines + the §20.10 framework NAME + the **per-locale count-aloud** [the de/nl/da/no ones-first variant — count by tens then the whole word, native-confirmed §A.13.48/§A.13.56] + number-word TTS via `PlaceValueCore._numberWord`, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi); the renderable crate-max depth; the berry-skin theme-wave.

## Character #30 — Bolt the apprentice bundle-robot (Game #25 "Bundle Bot", 1.NBT.B.2.a child-authored-bundling) — the transform-machine FAMILY HEAD

**Bolt** — an eager little apprentice factory-robot (mint-green body, antenna, a face-panel of googly eyes) who LOVES tidying loose ones into neat tens — but is **FORBIDDEN to bundle without you.** The child is the foreman; Bolt is the apprentice who *wants* to bundle, gets antsy ("mm? mm?!") as the loose ones pile toward ten, and needs YOU to judge "that's ten — bundle it!" Currently a small SVG robot head (idle / sheepish / happy poses); a SMALL corner sprite; stub for CA5. **THE FAMILY-HEAD DOCTRINE (this game establishes it):** the machine executes a delightful TRANSFORM, but the CHILD must supply the defining numerical JUDGMENT that GATES the transform — a wrong trigger is REFUSED, not corrected ("trigger ≠ decide"); future siblings (split/double/sort machines) inherit it. **Moods/poses:** `pack-idle` (at the workbench, ready), `antsy` (**the eager "mm?"** as the scatter nears ten — wanting it, forbidden), `clunk-shimmy` (**the signature** — the child counts ten + pulls the lever → the ten loose cubes CSS-collapse into ONE banded ten-bar with a deep CLUNK, the bar slides to the tens column, Bolt does a joyful full-body shimmy + speaks "Ten ones make one ten!"), `sheepish` (**the ONE non-shame negative** — a sub-ten or impostor [looks-~10-but-9] pull → the lever refuses, Bolt sheepish "not ten yet — keep counting!" / over-fill "whoa, more than ten — bundle a ten first!"; rooting for you, NEVER "wrong", no buzzer/red-X), `thumbs-up` (the tidy-shelf-full close). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** **THE EMBODIED SCATTER-TRAY (the §9 fix, load-bearing — the child AUTHORS the ten):** loose `<rect>` cubes land in a LOOSE, slightly-jittered scatter (NOT a pre-slotted clean ten-frame — that lets the child read "full" as a gestalt without ever holding "ten"); to bundle, the child must COUNT ten in the scatter themselves; **the IMPOSTOR** (a 9-that-looks-~10 scatter — the only state where "full"≠"ten", forcing the count); an **optional "Tidy ⊞" tool** (organizes the scatter into a frame to verify — the child's STRATEGY-tool, never the default answer-layout); **the SUBITIZABLE CLUMP-FEEDER** ("Drop ones" with a die-face of 1-3 pips — every feed a "will this overflow ten?" judgment, no blind tap-trance); **THE BANDED TEN-BARS** (the tens column — each a striped `<rect>` BAR that reads "one ten" at a glance, NOT a countable 10-cube stack [Game 16's affordance] + NOT a clean ten-frame [the gestalt-enabler]; tap a bar to **un-bundle** → it explodes back into 10 scatter cubes, the inverse/rote-killer); **THE BUNDLE LEVER** (coral "Bundle ten! 🔧", always present, **NEVER glows** — recognition, not a told-you-so; the child's judged trigger; ones≥10 bundles, ones<10 REFUSES — no `if(ones===10)bundle()` auto-path); **the over-fill recovery** (11+ → the tray border turns coral, "bundle a ten first"); the **TIDY SHELF** vessel (finished bundles lining up — the workshop getting pleasingly tidy; first-attempt-correct stocks a 🧰). CA5 asks: Bolt's moods (pack-idle / **antsy** / **clunk-shimmy** / sheepish / thumbs-up); the cubes (scatter idle + **the count-into-the-optional-tidy-frame** + **the 10-collapse-to-one-bar** — the composite-unit aha, the most art budget); the un-bundle (bar → scatter); the impostor (a near-ten scatter); the bundle CLUNK + the bar-slide; the tidy shelf filling. **The 10-cubes-collapse-to-one-bar gets the most art budget** ("ten ones, and I made them one ten"). **The CLUNK is the signature sound** (record first). **Fast-follow:** the full 11-locale fan-out (Bolt's ~10 personality lines + the **"bundle" verb** [de "bündeln" — NOT a literal calque, the K-1 composing term per locale] + the §20.10 framework NAME + number-word TTS via `PlaceValueCore._numberWord` [the per-bundle "ten ones make one ten" has NO count-up-order landmine], native-ensemble per §A.13.48, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi); the 50-99 depth (still ≤9 bars); the cube-skin theme-wave; the transform-machine family's NEXT sibling (split/double/sort — the child specifies, the machine refuses an invalid one).

## Character #31 — Pip the postal mouse & the mouse-town (Game #26 "Pip's Round", K.CC.A.3 recognition-face numeral-reading) — the 3rd navigate-spatial sibling

**Pip** — a warm round postal mouse (soft tan, a little coral satchel) working her morning round in a cozy, scattered mouse-town of mismatched houses — each with a **number plate by the door** and a drawn curtain, a resident waiting behind it. The hook: *someone is waiting for their letter and doesn't know it's coming yet* — when the right letter lands, the curtains fly open and a neighbor waves. **Delivery is a small act of kindness, decoded.** Currently a small SVG mouse head (idle / happy); a SMALL corner sprite; stub for CA5. **Moods/poses:** `round-idle` (satchel ready, on her route), `the-read` (Pip presents the envelope — read it, or "a letter for number fourteen!" spoken), `delivery-joy` (**the signature** — the child reads the number + taps the right house → the curtains fly open, the resident waves + clutches the letter, a tiny note drifts up, the route extends [Pip's scooter rides it], Pip re-voices "Fourteen — that's Mrs. Snail! She's been waiting"), `patient-recheck` (**the non-leaking miss** — a wrong tap leaves the wrong curtain CLOSED [no neighbor disturbed], "that's house eight — let's find fourteen"; names what they READ, never high/low/spatial, never highlights the answer), `end-yawn` (the soft close — a full waving street, satchel emptied). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** **THE READING-FORCED TARGET (the shape-match fix — the heart):** the envelope numeral is **CROSS-FONT** — a SERIF glyph (`targetFont` Georgia, coral) deliberately ≠ the rounded-sans house plates (`plateFont` Baloo 2) → the pixels don't match even when the number does → the child must read the number ABSTRACTLY, not pixel-match a squiggle; **AUDIO** rounds add a spoken "a letter for number fourteen!" (`_NUMBER_WORD_HELPERS` TTS) + a 🔊 replay (muted-safe: the cross-font glyph is the fallback) — **same-font glyph targets are CUT**; **THE SCATTERED MAP** (4-5 mismatched houses — styled `<g>`/`<button>` with a roof + body + a curtained window + an SVG-`<text>` number plate; positions↔numerals **decorrelated** so the correct house is NEVER the nearest/leftmost/in-order one; a **confusable distractor** every round — a teen-reversal 13/31, a same-tens 14/13/15 — so a navigation / first-digit glance lands WRONG; **NO house is highlighted**); **THE DELIVERY** (the correct tap opens the curtain → a resident [a colored blob with eyes, distinct per house → CA5 named neighbors] waves; the route polyline extends — `pointer-events:none`, render-only, the child never draws it); **THE SATCHEL** vessel (📬 stamps filling a strip — the bag empties as the street's curtains all open; first-attempt-correct fills it). CA5 asks: Pip's moods (round-idle / **the-read** / **delivery-joy** / patient-recheck / end-yawn); the houses (drawn-curtain idle + **the curtains-fly-open-resident-wave** — the read-made-kind, the most art budget, a distinct resident per number); the mailbox-flag flip; the route-draw + scooter; the satchel emptying. **The curtains-open-resident-wave gets the most art budget** ("I read fourteen, and Mrs. Snail is so happy"). **Fast-follow:** the full 11-locale fan-out (Pip's ~10 character/resident lines + the §20.10 framework NAME + the audio number-word target + number-word TTS via `_NUMBER_WORD_HELPERS`, native-ensemble per §A.13.48 OR number-only voicing, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi); the 1-3-deliveries-per-round depth (order-free); the resident/house theme-wave. **Cross-game guard:** the read side (recognition) is owned here; Game 20 Rivet's Forge owns the PRODUCE side (count→mint, never pre-shows the target numeral) — §22.1 same-code-different-mechanic.

## Character #32 — Mim the forgetful cloud-creature & the Memory Baskets (Game #27 "Mim's Memory Baskets", L.K.5.A category-sort) — the first real-picture game

**Mim** — a soft, sleepy, half-forgetful cloud-creature (a pale lavender rounded blob with gentle eyes) who keeps a row of woven **Memory Baskets**, one for each kind of thing Mim loves. Mim's basket of treasures tipped over in a sneeze, and Mim — being a bit forgetful — can't quite remember what goes where. The child helps Mim put each thing back where it BELONGS, and as each object lands, Mim's eyes light up: *"Oh YES — a dog is an ANIMAL! I remember now!"* The hook: **you're helping a gentle, forgetful friend remember what things ARE** — and every time you do, Mim remembers a little more of the world. Currently a small SVG cloud-blob (sleepy / happy-eyes-lit / "hmm" poses); a SMALL corner sprite; stub for CA5. **The FIRST game in the run to use REAL image-library pictures** (not geometry stubs). **Moods/poses:** `sleepy-idle` (drowsy, half-awake), `eyes-light-remember` (**the signature** — a correct drop → Mim's eyes brighten + Mim speaks the superordinate CONCEPT "Dog is an ANIMAL!"; the object nestles into its basket), `hmm` (**the ONE non-leaking negative** — a wrong drop → Mim tilts, ears-down "Hmm — that doesn't belong there"; the object floats back to the spill, the right basket is NEVER revealed/dimmed; Mim is forgetful about ITSELF, never disappointed in the child → shame impossible), `remember-shimmy` (the full-sort joy), `brighter-more-awake` (the arc — Mim grows more awake/vivid as it remembers more across the round). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn DOM/CSS/WebAudio + REAL image-library pictures, NOT CA5 yet):** **THE SPILL TRAY** of real picture-objects (`/image-library-webp/themes/<theme>/<noun>@2x.webp` — a red apple, a furry dog, a yellow duck…; tap an object to pick it up [it lifts + a coral ring], tap its basket to place it); **THE CATEGORY BASKETS** (3+ labeled bins — each a **concept-icon + a TEXT label** [🐾 Animals · 🍴 Foods · 🚗 Vehicles · 👕 Clothes · 🧸 Toys · 🔷 Shapes], **NEVER a bare color** — the anti-color-sort; the placed objects nestle inside); **THE CONCEPT-LOAD-BEARING DESIGN (the heart, art-enforced + verified against the real assets):** WITHIN-category visual diversity (a furry dog + a scaly fish + a feathery duck all ANIMALS → can't group by appearance) + CROSS-category visual confound (a RED apple [Food] + a RED fire-truck [Vehicle]; a YELLOW banana [Food] + a YELLOW duck [Animal] → sorting by color MISLEADS, the child must KNOW the concept) — *the confound pairs are chosen so the real pictures genuinely share a color* (a lesson from the build: the asset's actual rendered color is ground-truth, not the assumed color — the bus is blue, not yellow); **THE REMEMBER PAYOFF** (the nestle + the spoken concept + the basket-glow); **THE MEMORY GARDEN** vessel (the sorted things drift into a growing keepsake row + a 🌸 bloom per first-attempt round). CA5 asks: Mim's moods (sleepy-idle / **eyes-light-remember** / hmm / remember-shimmy / brighter-more-awake); the baskets (idle + **the object-nestles-in** — the concept aha, the most art budget + the "complete!" bounce); the concept-icons; the Memory Garden filling. **The object-nestles-and-Mim-remembers gets the most art budget** ("a fish is an ANIMAL — Mim remembered, because I knew"). **Fast-follow (MODERATE-HIGH, the first non-cheap fan-out):** re-VALIDATE the `OBJECT_CATEGORY` map + the confound/diversity sets per locale (the category boundaries are CULTURE-BOUND — a rabbit = pet in en/de, livestock elsewhere; native-ensemble §A.13.48, a content-validity task NOT machine-translation) + the basket labels + spoken concepts; more category-concept sets (Farm/Wild animals, Things-you-wear); a richer odd-one-out staging; the object theme-wave. §20.10 framework NAME, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi.

## Character #33 — Pippa the pond-juice frog & the frosted beaker (Game #28 "Pippa's Pond-Juice Lab", 3.MD.A.2 read-liquid-volume) — the first Grade-3 game

**Pippa** — a small round pond-green frog (rosy cheeks, big friendly eyes) who runs a cozy roadside pond-juice stand for thirsty pond creatures. A customer hops up and orders an ESTIMATE ("A dragonfly would like about SIX cups, please!"). Pippa can't read the fancy numbers on the beaker — **only the child can.** The hook: **you're the lab-scientist running the stand — you pour by feel, then read exactly how much you made.** Currently a small SVG frog (idle / happy); a SMALL corner sprite; stub for CA5. **The FIRST Grade-3 game in the run + the 2nd experiment-measure (sibling Game 15 The Wondering Jar — discrete estimate-then-COUNT; this = continuous estimate-then-READ).** **Moods/poses:** `stand-idle` (the cozy keeper, waiting), `the-pour` (the child pours; Pippa tips the watering-can pitcher), `reveal-shimmer` (**the signature** — on release the FROSTED beaker turns CLEAR with a soft shimmer, revealing where the juice landed against the scale), `taste-the-sample` (the cozy lab-ritual — Pippa sips a tiny flavor-sample; the juice recolors next round, pure flavor), `customer-served` (the customer drinks happily + says the number back "Five cups! Just right!"), `patient-recheck` (**the non-leaking negative** — a wrong reading → "hmm, what does it say where the juice reaches?" + a soft finger-glow trace to the liquid's TOP, showing WHERE to look NEVER which number; the pour precision is never judged → no-shame). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / juice (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio, NOT CA5 yet):** **THE FROSTED MEASURING-BEAKER (signature, load-bearing — the perceptual-cheat-killer + the family identity):** an SVG beaker with a numbered graduated scale (`<line>` ticks + `<text>` 0-10 labels, always visible) + a clip-path juice fill; a **frosted overlay** (`<rect>` at full opacity covering the interior) hides the level DURING the pour (nothing to chase → perceptual-line-matching structurally impossible) → CLEARS on release (opacity→0), revealing the juice at its landed level against the scale; **THE POUR** (the child taps "Pour 🫗" — the calibration gesture; the level is HIDDEN until reveal); **THE READ** (the child reads the revealed level + reports it on a horizontal **number-strip** of ≥44px buttons — the answer is the REVEALED level, NEVER the order: "about six" but the juice landed at 5 → matching "6" is wrong); **THE CONSERVATION (read the scale, not the height):** compare two cups (which has MORE?) + **different-graduation** cups (a 0-10 beaker at 7 has a TALLER column than a 0-20 beaker at 8, but 8>7 — the SHORTER column reads MORE → reading the column-height is wrong, read the NUMBERS); the **served-glass tray** (🥤 filling — happy customers, NOT a count). **Numerals ALWAYS real `<text>`** (the scale + the report-strip; 11-language-ready). **NO drawn target line/band** ever (only the juice-surface dashed line on reveal). CA5 asks: Pippa's moods (stand-idle / the-pour / **reveal-shimmer** / taste-the-sample / customer-served / patient-recheck); the beaker (frosted-pour → clear-reveal → the level against the scale — the read aha "I poured by feel and it's FIVE!", the most art budget); the pond-customers (order + drink-happily + say-the-number); the served-glass tray filling; the compare + the different-graduation their own stagings. **The frosted→clear REVEAL gets the most art budget.** **Fast-follow (CHEAP-MODERATE — lighter than #27):** the **per-locale UNIT** (do NOT hard-code "liter" — the EN pilot uses generic "cups"; the national conventional unit or a generic cups/glasses framing, native-confirmed §A.13.49) + Pippa's ~10 lines + the compare-language (more/less/most) + the §20.10 framework NAME + number-word TTS, `[NSR-FLAG]` sv/da/no/fi; the 0-20 by-2s + nearest-half + read-between-marks depth; the juice-flavor theme-wave.

**A build lesson recorded:** a "conservation" between two containers is only genuine when they have DIFFERENT scales — if both share one scale, the taller juice column always reads the higher number (no trap, a height-reader wins). The per-cup `scaleMax` (a 0-10 vs a 0-20 beaker) is what makes the shorter-column-is-more conservation real; verify it on the rendered cups, not just the gate.

## Character #34 — Nila the listening otter & the idea-fish / umbrella / lily-pad pond (Game #30 "Nila's Idea Pond", RI.K.2 main-topic + key-details of a HEARD text) — the run's FIRST informational-comprehension game

**Nila** — a calm river-otter (warm brown fur, cream belly, big gentle eyes, small round ears) who floats on her back in a still **reading-pond** and listens to little informational stories the pond tells. A short paragraph is **read aloud** (TTS, replayable, sentence-highlighted); the child hears it. Four **idea-fish** drift up; tapping one makes it **speak a short same-grain phrase about the story** (audio only — NO printed generality, NO content-distinguishing picture). The hook: **you're the one who hears the whole story and finds the BIG IDEA all the little ideas belong to.** Currently a soft SVG otter (listen / happy / hmm poses); stub for CA5. **The run's first reading-comprehension game + the hide-reveal family at its most literal** (the hidden thing = the topic/detail HIERARCHY, revealed as an umbrella the details school beneath). **NOTE for CA5: Nila is an OTTER, not a bear** — at stub size the brown critter can read bear-ish; the hand-art must clearly distinguish the otter mascot from the bear that stars in several of the stories. **Moods/poses:** `float-listen` (on her back, ear up, the patient "listening to the pond" idle — the signature calm), `name-the-idea` (**the payoff** — a happy barrel-roll + she names the idea in a full sentence "So it was all about how a bear gets ready for winter!", modeling the RI.K.2 retell), `lets-listen-again` (**the non-leaking negative** — a gentle "hmm, let's listen again" as she replays the paragraph; the redirect is ALWAYS to the text, never the answer), `nap-on-the-pad` (full-pass keepsake — she naps on the fullest bloomed lily-pad). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / pond (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio/TTS, NOT CA5 yet):** **THE FOUR IDEA-FISH (load-bearing — the comprehension-is-the-only-signal anti-cheat):** identical plain fish bodies, distinguished ONLY by a positional color tint (orange/green/gold/blue — re-shuffled per mount, so the color is NOT a content cue) + the spoken/written phrase; **there is NO per-fish content picture** (the picture→scene-match leak is removed). Poses: `idle-drift` / `held-in-net` (lifted, no gold — the pre-commit hold) / `gold-reveal` (POST-COMMIT only — the netted topic rises, glows GOLD) / `school-home` (the true details settle beneath the umbrella) / `wriggle-free` (**IDENTICAL for every wrong fish** → leaks nothing) / `flick-away` (the non-belonger flicks its tail at the pond's edge — "not this story!"). **THE UMBRELLA (signature reveal — the hierarchy made visible):** a teal canopy that **unfurls** over the gold topic fish on a CORRECT commit (the part-whole relation made spatial — the details school beneath the idea they belong to); appears POST-COMMIT ONLY, never a pre-answer cue. **THE NET + the "That's the big idea!" COMMIT** (the single deliberate act — reveal fires only on commit → no guess-and-check; a WRONG commit replays the story AND regenerates a different paragraph from the swap-group so the target MOVES → brute-force structurally dies). **THE NEST-SLOTS + the "Back to the pond" RELEASE** (drag/tap the true details into slots; send the NON-BELONGER — a plausible-but-absent fact whose noun is NOT in the heard text — back to the pond). **THE LILY-PAD VESSEL** (🪷 pads bloom across the pond as rounds are solved — NOT a count). **Text ALWAYS real DOM/SVG `<text>`** (the paragraph + the fish phrases-as-text; 11-language-ready; never bitmaps). CA5 asks: Nila's moods (float-listen / **name-the-idea** / lets-listen-again / nap-on-the-pad); the idea-fish (idle-drift / held-in-net / **gold-reveal** / school-home / wriggle-free / flick-away); **the umbrella unfurl/open (the comprehension aha — "THIS is what it's mostly about, and look, they came home" — the MOST art budget)**; the lily-pad bloom (3-4-frame vessel); the paragraph-card scene (decorative — illustrates but must NOT distinguish the topic fish). **Per-locale = the catalog's MOST EXPENSIVE content unit (FLAGGED):** real informational paragraphs + register-uniform reuse-groups authored per locale by a native K-3 ensemble (NOT machine-translated; the per-label-swap-invariant multiplies the cost) — a build-phase funding decision (EN flagship now / fund top-3 / defer the heard-comprehension family). **EN-only pilot.**

**A build lesson recorded:** a comprehension game's build-gate "can't read," so the proof is structural — the bank is authored so the answer is `f(authored topicId)` while NO surface cue (length / first-mention / latest / position / overlap / odd-one-out / phrase-frame / register) correlates with the topic above chance. The construction that passes: same-grain register-uniform declarative phrases all sharing the subject noun (kills head-noun-generality); each in-text key appears EXACTLY once (overlap tied → non-informative); the topic's position among the in-text mentions balanced 1st/middle/last across the bank (kills earliest AND latest); the gist carried by FRAME words that map to the topic semantically but are no fish's key. The armed surface-solver then scores ≤chance while the comprehension oracle scores 100% — gate-measured, the comprehension-game analog of the "measured adversarial solver must fail" signature.

## Character #35 — the Word-Doctor & the sick word-patient / medicine tray / thermometer fever-face (Game #31 "Word Doctor's Clinic — heal by ear", L.1.2.e SPELL untaught words phonetically) — the catalog's FIRST spelling-ENCODE game + the 4th repair-restore sibling

**The Word-Doctor** — the child's caring-clinician avatar (a friendly stethoscope-wearing healer; a small SVG/emoji stub for now). A **sick word-patient** (the gated COLOR image-library creature — a fish, a frog, a boat) shuffles onto the exam cot, feverish + grey. The hook: **you heal by EAR — you can't see the spelling, you HEAR the word and spell its one sick sound.** The run's 4th operator fork (the catalog's "fix one wrong letter" was error-DETECTION; operator re-mechanic'd to GENERATIVE spelling). **The catalog's FIRST spelling-ENCODE game** (vs the live RF.1.3 DECODE word-builders — this is L.1.2.e, the spelling shown NOWHERE, the atomic sick-sound + voice-back loop). **Moods/poses:** `listen` (the doctor leans in with the stethoscope — "listen, then heal by ear"), `voice-back-try` (**the signature** — the patient "tries to say" the resulting word; a wrong grapheme voices a DIFFERENT word so the child HEARS the mismatch + self-corrects — anti-brute-force AS learning), `heal-hop-say-name` (**the payoff** — the correct grapheme → the patient voices the RIGHT word, the fever cools 🤒→😄, the grey creature **pinks up to full color**, hops off the cot + says its NAME "Fish!"), `patient-recheck` (**the non-leaking negative** — a wrong tile springs back ATOMICALLY [only the sick grapheme; the frame untouched, no per-slot green], the fever HOLDS, a soft "achoo"; after ~3 wrongs a "nurse's hint" gently dims ONE clearly-impossible competitor — narrows, never reveals). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / clinic (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio/TTS, the picture = gated COLOR WebP):** **THE SICK WORD-PATIENT (the picture-creature, load-bearing — the recognition anchor):** the gated image-library noun, rendered **DESATURATED/grey while sick → FULL COLOR on heal** (the sick→well visual, paired with the fever-FACE so it is not color-only). **THE X-RAY SOUND-SKELETON (the sick word as letter-tiles):** one tile per phoneme — most **healthy** (scaffolded letters, the stable frame, e.g. `f _ sh`) + ONE **sick** (an empty dashed slot; 1 letter for a vowel, 2 for a digraph/team/blend). **The complete correct spelling appears NOWHERE while the task is live** — the slot fills (green) ONLY post-heal (the reward). **THE LISTEN LAMP** (taps → the patient says its WHOLE word, TTS, replayable; tapping the sick slot re-voices SLOWER — **whole-word only, NEVER an isolated phoneme**, the RF.K.2.c safety constraint). **THE MEDICINE TRAY** (the correct grapheme + DIFFERENT-sound confusable competitors from the gated `en-phoneme-confusables.json` table — the EAR decides; a wrong pick = a wrong sound = a different word voiced back). **THE THERMOMETER FEVER-FACE** (a binary 🤒/😄 FACE, derived `fever = healed ? 'well':'sick'`, holds NO number — un-mistakable for a score; operator no-competition lock). **The waiting-room BENCH** (🙂 healed friends — a growing cozy crowd, NEVER a count). **Letters + words ALWAYS real DOM/SVG `<text>`.** CA5 asks: the Word-Doctor (listen / re-voice / heal-flourish); the patient-creature (sick-grey-sniffle → **voice-back-try** → healthy-pink-hop-say-name); the thermometer fever-face (🤒→😄); the bench filling. **The voice-back-try + the heal-hop-say-name (the encode aha — "I heard it, I spelled it, it's ALIVE") get the most art budget.** **PER-LOCALE = EN/DEEP-ORTHOGRAPHY-BOUND (FLAGGED LOUD):** the cognitions exist BECAUSE of English orthographic depth — shallow-orthography locales (Finnish/Italian, spell-as-you-hear) have NO spelling-confusables → the mechanic is INAPPLICABLE there (structurally absent); deep-orthography (en/de/fr/da/nl) could carry a RE-AUTHORED version (NOT translation). **EN-only pilot.**

**A build lesson recorded:** a spell-by-ear gate is "blind" too, so the proof is a SOLVER SET against the REAL core — an EAR-using oracle (knows the sick sound's grapheme) scores 100% while a no-spelling (random), a constraint (frame+orthotactics, no sound), a picture-recall (sight-spell a memorized word), and a brute-force solver all score ≤chance. The construction that passes: a NEW gated `phoneme→{DIFFERENT-sound confusable graphemes}` table (the core embeds it; the gate asserts it byte-equals the JSON SoT); per-word grapheme skeletons where **`graphemes.join("")===the real approved word`**; the pool ⊆ approved-words ∩ image-vocab ∩ a COLOR WebP, **untaught-skewed** (so picture-recall fails); ≥2 plausible completions per round (so the scaffold doesn't over-determine); the correct grapheme/complete spelling absent from the snapshot. The voice-back of a wrong assembly ("fesh" for a wrong vowel in "fish") is the net-new mechanic — TTS the resulting string + a mute-safe visual spring-back.

## Character #36 — Owl & the cuckoo / the cottage cross-section / the wall clock / the sunrise→moonrise day-arc (Game #32 "Owl's Cuckoo Cottage — read and set the clock", 1.MD.B.3 tell time to the hour + half-hour) — the run's 5th operator fork; the READ-the-clock cell the free E10 omits

**Owl** — a sleepy, cozy resident of a cuckoo cottage whose WHOLE DAY (dawn tea → breakfast → garden → supper → bedtime) unfolds across a session; the child is the cottage's **timekeeper-and-time-READER**. The **cuckoo** is the star: it bursts from its door + ACTS OUT each moment. The hook: **read the wall clock to find out what Owl is doing now, and set the hands so the cuckoo can pop out and act it out.** The run's 5th operator fork — the pedagogy read the SHIPPED free E10 clock code + ruled the catalog's "Set the Clock Hands" a bit-for-bit DUPLICATE (E10 is SET-only); the operator re-framed onto the genuinely-uncovered **READ** cognition (read→name-event), with SET as the honest on-ramp. **Moods/poses:** Owl (sleepy / blink / happy / the day-walk dawn→night); the **cuckoo** (hidden → POP → coo → **act-out-each-event** — porridge steams at half past seven, lamp on at supper, one star + yawn at bedtime — the signature payoff). **Moods/poses for the cottage:** the cross-section (kitchen / window-sky / bed / garden) whose **FROZEN states advance ONLY on a correct commit** (dawn → day → dusk → night) — never reactive mid-solve (the no-proximity-cheat rule). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / cottage (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio/TTS; pure geometry — no image-library 404):** **THE WALL CLOCK (load-bearing — modeled on the proven E10 clock-core math, RE-IMPLEMENTED 0-lines-to-the-protected-core):** an SVG face (1–12 numerals at sin/-cos radius, the two hands as rotating `<g>` groups) with the **COUPLED hour hand** (`30H+0.5M` → the short hand sits BETWEEN numbers at half-past, a real clock; the "is it 3:30 or 4:30?" coupling-interpretation is the load-bearing READ skill) + a fat invisible ≥44px hit-line along each hand for the radial drag (atan2 → snap). **THE READ EVENT-CARDS** (the child READS the analog face → taps the moment whose worded time matches; NO digital target on the clock). **THE "WAKE THE CUCKOO!" COMMIT** (the deliberate set-action, not a Check-drill) + a digital **readout that mirrors the child's CURRENT hands** (a tell-time mirror, never a target). **THE WORLD-CUE** (a sky/sundial cue — dawn-pink / sun-high — decoded → set, no worded time). **THE 3-CLOCK ORDER** (read three set clocks → tap the one showing the asked event). **THE SUNRISE→MOONRISE DAY-ARC** (the non-numeric vessel — a sun bead crossing to a moon; advances per round reached, **monotonic, NOT a streak**, so a wrong try never stalls it). **Numerals + hands ALWAYS real DOM/SVG `<text>`/lines** (11-language-ready). **NO digital target ever; the cottage is FROZEN during the solve; the clock is NEVER a countdown** (a passive tick as a hand passes a numeral; no `setInterval`). **Wrong is SILENT** (the cuckoo peeks-and-tucks; no red/buzzer/arc-stall). CA5 asks: Owl (sleepy/blink/happy/day-walk); the cuckoo (hidden→pop→coo→**act-out-each-event**); the cottage frozen-states (dawn→day→dusk→night, advancing only on correct commit); the day-arc (sun→moon). **The cuckoo bursting out + acting out the moment (the read/set aha — "I read the clock and Owl's day happened") gets the most art budget.** **PER-LOCALE = MODEST:** the half-past/5-min time-idiom REUSES E10's already-authored + ensemble-verified 11-locale `timeExpr` (Germanic/Nordic toward-next-hour "halb 8"=7:30; Romance hour+half; the harder 5-min quarter-past/quarter-to); the NEW per-locale authoring = the cottage/event nouns (breakfast/lunch/bedtime) + the world-cue prompts. **EN-only pilot.**

**A build lesson recorded:** when a catalog entry duplicates a LIVE free activity, the rescue is to find the cognition the live one OMITS (E10 is SET-only → READ is the premium-distinct cell) and DEMOTE the engagement frame (Owl's day) from a claimed cognition to a wrapper — a told-target solver passes a named-target SET round but FAILS a READ round, so the day-SEQUENCE proves no cognition. The gate codifies this: a TOLD-TARGET solver must pass every SET round (honest — they name a target) but fail every READ/order/world-cue round (no named target → the child must read the face/decode the cue); a COTTAGE-ONLY solver must not converge (the cottage is frozen — no proximity cheat). These two adversaries catch the decorative-sequence + the proximity cheats that the live E10's copy-readout/wrong-coupling gate cannot. The clock math is RE-IMPLEMENTED (modeled), never imported, so 0 lines touch the protected clock-core.js.

## Character #37 — the singing engine & Whistle Valley / the number-line track / the anchor ties / the trolley / the clearing-sky day-route (Game #34 "Whistle Valley — Number-Line Track Repair", 1.NBT.A.1 count to 120 on the number line) — the 5th repair-restore sibling; the number-SEQUENCE cell, no live equivalent

**The singing engine** — a friendly, never-impatient toy train who runs Whistle Valley, where **the track IS a number line.** A storm tore out sections of track between two intact **anchor ties** (labeled with their numerals, e.g. 47 … 53); the child is the track-repair crew. The hook: **read each numbered tie and lay it at the exact spot its number belongs — count on from the anchors — then send the singing engine chugging across the repaired track.** The pedagogy ruled BUILD (1.NBT.A.1 genuinely uncovered — the live number-line is a free manipulative; the operator confirmed BUILD over the K.CC.A.2 star-stitcher fill-gap overlap). **Moods/poses:** the engine (idle / **waiting-at-station** [the no-shame negative — a wrong commit and the engine just WAITS, patient, never racing] / chug-across / **sing-the-restored-sequence** — the signature payoff, the count-on made audible "47, 48, 49, …!"). **The cottage-analog here = the valley:** the storm-torn gaps → the repaired-track sparkle; the **clearing-sky / day-route** brightens cloud-by-cloud as segments are repaired. ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / valley (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio/TTS; pure geometry — no image-library 404):** **THE NUMBER-LINE TRACK (load-bearing — MODELS number-line.js's `valueToPct(v)=((v-min)/(max-min))*100`, RE-IMPLEMENTED 0-lines-to-the-public-manipulative):** a teal rail with a faint full-scale unit-tick scaffold (a reference, NOT discrete slots) + the two **PINNED anchor ties** (labeled, at the window edges) — **CONTINUOUS place-at-distance** (position = magnitude; graded by a tolerance ε, NO discrete slots to sort into — the critic's anti-SORT resolution). **THE TROLLEY OF NUMBERED TIES** (over-provided + **in-range distractors** — the "is this a station?" check; a sorter who places the smallest-N fails). **The ties are AUTO-WIDTH pills with the numeral IN FLOW** (the fix for the 3-digit "102" anchor that clipped when the numeral was white-on-a-fixed-box — the box grows to fit any digit count; the text-containment gate now catches the class). **THE "SEND THE TRAIN!" COMMIT** (evaluate-at-commit-ONLY — NO on-drop feedback, so a wrong tie never leaks "wrong gap"; a wrong commit reshuffles the trolley, anti-brute-force). **THE CLEARING-SKY / DAY-ROUTE** (the non-numeric vessel — brightness/track, NEVER a count). **Numerals ALWAYS real DOM/SVG `<text>`** (11-language-ready). **The train is NEVER a countdown/race; NO sequential audio while solving** (singing 48…[gap]…50 would hand over 49 — per-tie numeral only; the SONG is the post-commit reward). CA5 asks: the engine (idle / wait-at-station / chug-across / **sing-the-restored-sequence**); the railway ties; the storm-debris → repaired-track sparkle; the clearing sky. **The engine singing the restored sequence + chugging across (the count-on aha — "I rebuilt the count and the train sang it whole") gets the most art budget.** **PER-LOCALE = CHEAP:** the numerals are universal; the count-on song = digit-words via TTS; ~few strings. **EN-only pilot.**

**A build lesson recorded:** the critic's SORT-ASCENDING cheat (on discrete number-line gaps, ordinal-rank-by-numeral === ordinal-rank-by-position → a child sorts ascending WITHOUT magnitude; UNBEATABLE on contiguous by-1) is killed by CONTINUOUS place-at-distance (no slot-set) + IN-RANGE distractors. The gate codifies it BANK-WIDE: a count-on/magnitude oracle heals 100% while SORT_ASCENDING / SPACING / SUCCESSOR_ONLY / BRUTE all score <= chance — and the honest nuance is that a FEW dense-by-1 rounds are individually sort-passable (even-spacing ≈ true positions, and in-range distractors are mathematically impossible on a contiguous gap), so the bank is SKEWED so the distractor-laden skip/interval rounds (where the in-range distractors defeat the cheat) are the MAJORITY → the bank-wide cheat accuracy stays ≤ chance (SORT 43%) even though count-on is the pedagogical headline. A SECOND lesson: the text-containment gate must measure the numeral-bearing element with the numeral IN FLOW — an absolutely-positioned white numeral over a fixed box passes a `scrollWidth ≤ clientWidth` check while visibly clipping (white-on-cream past the box); auto-width pills + in-flow text both fix the render AND make the gate honest.

## Character #38 — the warm woodland Vet (fox) & the shy poorly-animal patient / the x-ray window / the 3 diagram metaphors / the gratitude-sticker wall (Game #35 "Vet's Diagnosis Window — the x-ray shows the STORY", 1.OA.A.1 word problems within 20, unknowns in all positions) — the run's 6th repair-restore sibling (the WORD-PROBLEM / situation-modeling cell) + the 2nd comprehension-class game after Nila

**The Vet (a gentle fox in a woodland clinic)** and a rotating cast of **shy, poorly little animal patients** (curled-up-sick → un-curl-meet-eyes-nuzzle when healed). The symptom is a number STORY. The hook: **the x-ray window first ENACTS the situation action (friends join / some leave / two animals stand side-by-side — with the UNKNOWN quantity FOGGED so the answer can't be counted off the scene), the story is read aloud, then the child MODELS it** — taps a quantity tile, taps an iconic role-slot to place it, taps the empty slot to mark the "?", and LEAVES the magnitude-decoy tile in the tray — then a 0-9 dial appears, the child dials the DERIVED number, and "Diagnose 🩺" commits → the frosted x-ray CLEARS and the patient heals. The pedagogy ruled BUILD (1.OA.A.1 genuinely uncovered as a *situation-modeling* surface — the live story games read-then-COMPUTE; this grades the BINDING; operator confirmed "build as specced" over the K.OA.A.2 ten-frame + 2.OA.A.1 number-bond story siblings). **Moods/poses:** the patient (sick-curled / **un-curl-meet-eyes-nuzzle** — the heal aha) + the Vet (welcoming / reassuring on a wrong — "let's look again," NEVER a red-X; a shy animal can't be failed, only gently understood). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / clinic (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio/TTS; pure geometry + emoji stubs — NO image-library 404):** **THE X-RAY WINDOW** (enact → frosted → **magic-eye clear**; the enactment HIDES the unknown — no perceptual-count cheat). **THE 3 DISTINCT DIAGRAM METAPHORS (the critic's F — distinct TO THE CHILD even when EMPTY, the rework lesson below):** add-to/take-from = a **horizontal change-STRIP** (`[ ] + [ ] = [ ]`, the operator glyph the only mark); put-together/take-apart = a **vertical part-part-whole BOND** (a whole box on TOP → a drawn brace/stem → two part boxes BELOW); compare = **two stacked horizontal length-BARS** (the coral fill grows with the placed value so the longer/shorter bars convey magnitude) + a difference slot set off by a dashed divider. **THE ICONIC/UNLABELED ROLE-SLOTS** (the critic's B — NO "start/whole/bigger" words; position + glyph carry the role; the story carries no role token either → the label-match cheat has no signal). **THE QUANTITY TILES + THE MAGNITUDE-DECOY** (a 4th tile larger than the true container that must be REJECTED — the critic's C, the headline anti-magnitude device). **THE 0-9 DIAL** (appears only AFTER the model is fully bound; the number is DERIVED from the binding — no `answer` field → number-grab structurally impossible). **THE EQUATION RECAP** (the dial phase shows the child's bound model as one clean line, e.g. "5 + 7 = ?"). **THE GRATITUDE-STICKER WALL + the un-curling animal** (the non-numeric vessel — warmth/trust, NEVER a count). **Numbers + text ALWAYS real DOM/SVG `<text>`** (11-language-ready). **No timer/score/streak; wrong → the x-ray stays gently CLOUDY (no red) + re-read/re-enact + the tiles reshuffle (anti-brute-force).** CA5 asks: the Vet fox; the sick→healed animal pairs; the enactment mini-scenes (join / leave / compare); the x-ray frost→clear; the gratitude stickers. **The enactment + the x-ray-clear + the un-curling animal (the modeling aha — "I understood the patient + it trusts me") gets the most art budget.** **PER-LOCALE = HIGH (comprehension-class, FLAGGED for the operator's funding call — the stories + the keyword-traps + the slot-non-echo are RE-AUTHORED per language, not translated; the magnitude-balance is language-independent). EN-only pilot.**

**A build lesson recorded:** the gate's headline is the **BIND-BY-MAGNITUDE cheat** (a non-reader binds biggest-known → the container slot, ~6/8 CGI cells, no comprehension) — killed by a **MAGNITUDE-BALANCED deck**: over-weight the magnitude-BREAKING cells (take-from-change, compare-bigger, where the biggest known is NOT the container) + inject a magnitude-DECOY tile (larger than the true container) on every magnitude-FRIENDLY cell so the magnitude solver binds the decoy into the container → wrong. The measured gate proves a situation-modeler oracle heals 100% while MAGNITUDE scores **0%**, and KEYWORD / NUMBER-GRAB stay ≤ chance EVEN WITH THE BINDING GRANTED (the reversal cells — start-unknown + inconsistent-compare — and the derived number defeat them), LABEL-MATCH is 0% (iconic slots + a literal no-role-token-in-story grep), BRUTE ≤ chance. A SECOND lesson (the critic's catch, fixed): the 3 diagram metaphors must be distinct **at empty first-paint** — three sets of plain dashed boxes read identically; the fix was making the *structure* distinct empty (a drawn brace for the bond; visible horizontal bar-tracks for compare), not relying on the fill. A THIRD (layout): against a fixed ~600px shell chrome, the activity had to drop to ≤~290px root — solved by a full-width 2-line story + a 2-col (iconic-diagram | tile-tray) MODEL phase, then a compact equation-RECAP + a 0-9 digit-accumulator keypad for the DIAL phase (the diagram's tall compare-bars / bond would not co-fit a 21-key 0-20 pad at 320×640).

## Character #39 — Pip the otter & the seaside market stall / the driftwood counter / the kelp basket vessel / the true-diameter US coin tokens / the off-coin legend strip (Game #38 "Pip's Market Stall — Exact-Change Market Stall", 2.MD.C.8 money word problems within $1) — the run's first MONEY game + the first genuine collect-trade (value-trade) game; the catalog's FIRST paid-product overlap surfaced + operator-ruled "build as specced"

**Pip the otter** runs a sun-warmed seaside market stall (driftwood counter, kelp basket, swinging chalkboard price) and sells snacks + toys; the child pays EXACT change from a coin purse. The hook: **the coin's VALUE is INVISIBLE on the coin** — coins are recognized by SIZE + metal-tint + a denomination monogram (P/N/D/Q), and the value (penny=1, nickel=5, dime=10, quarter=25) must be RETRIEVED from memory (an off-coin legend strip scaffolds a MINORITY of rounds; ≥60% are legend-FREE). **Build context:** money 2.MD.C.8 is *partially OWNED* by a live FREE activity (`money-core.js` count-out + make-change) AND a later corrected design (Game 59 "Coraline's Coin Counter") — I surfaced the collision via AskUserQuestion; the operator ruled "build Game 38 as-specced" (full 7 cogs), accepting the overlap. **The 3 distinctness DEPARTURES from the live engine:** value-invisible-on-coin (the live engine prints "25¢" on the face) + NO live running total (commit-then-check, the sum stays in the child's head) + TRUE-relative diameters (the live engine is uniform — Pip's dime is the SMALLEST coin yet worth more than the bigger nickel). **Moods/poses:** Pip (sell / wait-patient / **cha-ching-clap** / hand-change / **celebrate-the-dime** — "the tiny dime is worth MORE than the big nickel!"). ~512², transparent, 12fps.

**Props / stall (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS/WebAudio/TTS; pure geometry — NO image-library 404):** **THE COIN TOKENS** (a ≥44px tap button wrapping a sized disc at its TRUE relative diameter [dime smallest → penny → nickel → quarter largest], metal-tint + a denomination MONOGRAM — **NO value numeral / no countable pips on the face**; the value lives only in the off-coin legend + the aria-label). **THE OFF-COIN LEGEND STRIP** (per legendTier — full = monogram↔¢ pairs / value = a values-only reminder / none = legend-FREE recall; ≥60% legend-free, where the standard is genuinely exercised). **THE TRAY (no live total)** + **THE "PAY PIP" COMMIT** (commit-then-check — the sum stays in the child's head; over → **ADJUST-DON'T-WIPE** [Pip "Take one back!"; the child removes a coin of their choosing, the careful set is NOT wiped, the system never says which/how-much]; wrong → reshuffle the purse, anti-brute-force). **THE 7 COGS over 2 primitives:** COMPOSE (make-amount / fewest [the dime-value-over-size act] / change / two-ways / trade-up) + CHOOSE (count-set [tap the total] / enough [short/just-enough/too-much]). **THE KELP BASKET** (the non-numeric vessel — abundance, NEVER a count; fills on the cha-ching). **Numbers + ¢/$ ALWAYS real DOM `<text>`** (the price / legend / options; 11-locale-ready). CA5 worklist (per-currency): Pip (sell/wait/cha-ching/hand-change/celebrate-dime); the US coin FACES (penny/nickel/dime/quarter — no value on the face, the dime visibly smallest); the kelp basket (filling); the legend strip. **The cha-ching + the snack-into-the-basket (the value aha — "I knew what each coin was worth + paid exactly") gets the most art budget.** **PER-LOCALE = DEEP CURRENCY, a PER-CURRENCY FAMILY (operator-ruled): US/EN pilot; EUR/GBP/Nordic are SEPARATE future commissions (the EUR has no dime/quarter, the dime-is-smaller misconception is US-specific, Nordic öre is withdrawn) — NOT an auto-fan.**

**A build lesson recorded:** the gate's headline is the **LEGEND-MATCH cheat the gate was blind to** — an off-coin image↔value legend lets a non-knower read-the-label without retrieving value; killed by **legend-free ≥60% + withhold-one-channel tiers + a LEGEND-MATCH gate-solver that must PASS the scaffolded full-legend rounds (proving the cheat exists) and FAIL every legend-free round**. The measured gate proves a value-composer oracle wins 100% while COUNT-PIPS (no pips on the coin) / ALL-PENNIES (penny ≤4 < target) / NUMBER-CHASE (no live gauge to hill-climb) / BRUTE (reshuffle-on-wrong) / SIZE-GREEDY (the dime is the smallest coin → diameter-greedy fails the fewest round) all score ≤ chance, and LEGEND-MATCH passes 0 legend-free rounds. A SECOND lesson (the critic's catch, fixed): **TRUE-relative coin diameters are pedagogically honest but VISUALLY too subtle** (real US coins are 18–24mm — a 1.3× range) → the dime-is-smaller confront was imperceptible; the fix was EXAGGERATING the size spread (steepen to a ~1.57× quarter:dime ratio) while preserving the true ORDER, AND capping the disc at the 44px tap floor so the bigger coins don't grow the row height past the 320×640 fold. A THIRD: a finite PURSE (not the live engine's infinite palette) is what makes penny≤4 / ≥2-denomination load-bearing — tapping a purse coin MOVES it to the tray (and back), so the limit actually bites.

## Character #40 — Mim the glow-smith & the workbench / the translucent glowing glass shards / the blank dark lantern silhouette / the Lantern Shelf + the ways-gallery (Game #39 "Shape Forge — Mim's Glow Workshop", 1.G.A.2 + K.G.B.6 compose 2D shapes into a composite shape, and back) — the run's first GEOMETRY-COMPOSITION game (the construct-build family's first shape-tiling); the integer triangular-lattice game

**Mim the round glow-smith** forges LIGHT at a cream workbench: the child fuses translucent glowing glass shards (pattern-block primitives — triangles + rhombi) to fill a blank glowing **lantern** shape, forge the SAME shape a NEW way, and re-forge a finished lantern into a new shape (the deep "compose new shapes FROM the composite" clause).
