Answer DERIVED from {target, place}, never stored. Marina: 2 poses (idle/happy). Direction-A (cream `#FBF3E4` / teal `#146B5E` harbors + jars / coral `#F2784B` Marina's roof + the lit glow). NOTE — this is the #81 design; the catalog also held #57 "Rolo's Lantern Path" (nearness/number-line) for the SAME unowned 3.NBT.A.1 — gate-check fork, operator chose #81 (#57's own sibling-critique showed nearness designs are digit-equivalent); #57 marked SUPERSEDED. EN-ONLY pilot (numeric-light per-locale; the place + midpoint vocabulary the care point).

**A build lesson recorded — the SMART-ADVERSARY FALSE-PASS GUARD + grade-the-inverse (program-wide) + the desktop scale-up (again):** (1) when a standard's cheap procedure IS the legitimate forward algorithm (the standard endorses "use place value understanding to round"), you CANNOT make the forward task digit-rule-proof by dressing it up — the governing digit is a SUFFICIENT STATISTIC for every forward fact. **The fix = grade the INVERSE/BOUNDARY the forward procedure can't run backward to produce (band edges 45 & 54, the midpoint) + the meaning-traps + place-governance; demote the forward act to the MODEL.** (2) NEW gate-design doctrine — **arm the verify gate with the STRONGEST correct-but-empty procedure (the SMART place-digit reader "the digit right of the TOLD place"), NOT the naive ones-digit miseducation; the pass-condition is "the SMART solver fails the GAME" with a `SMART_FAIL_REQUIRED` assertion (a NAIVE-only-fail is a FALSE PASS).** Implemented: all spine cogs are SMART-proof (band/midpoint/place-undeclared → SMART has no forward move); catch-false-flag is the bounded SMART-passable minority so SMART overall ≤ chance. (3) The desktop SPARSE/TINY recurrence: the automated visual-qa sparse gate measures per-ANSWER-CARD fill, so it PASSES text-only candidate buttons even when the whole cluster is phone-sized in a big desktop card — the **visual-critic on the desktop sweep is the load-bearing catch** (it flagged sparse candidates + too-small harbor icons/glow + a microscopic "@D" + an empty-vessel-as-stray-dash). Fix = clamp the content (candidates/prompt/numeral/icons) UP on desktop while keeping the phone layout + the small-vs-big harbor distinction, and give the empty vessel a recognizable shape (a lighthouse on the coast). (Grepped `halfway-harbors`/`marina`/`_marina` clear; appended via a temp-file `cat`.)

**REBUILD NOTE (Character #55 / Game #81, 2026-06-26 — CLARITY-FIRST):** the operator played the as-built Halfway Harbors and said "I cannot understand the activity — the graphics don't make sense and I don't understand what the question is." Root cause: the #81 design (for anti-cheat rigor) REMOVED the number-line and graded the abstract INVERSE (band edges / which-place / catch-false-flag) with only number buttons + tiny decorative icons. **Rebuilt around a CLEAR, VISIBLE to-scale NUMBER-LINE** — the standard way rounding is taught. New cogs: **nearest** (the boat sits on a labeled line of lantern-harbors with a dashed halfway-divide buoy → tap the nearest), **big-trail** (same on tall striped LIGHTHOUSES = hundreds, visibly distinct from short lanterns = tens), **halfway** (the line + a single coral "?" buoy at the visual middle → tap the number that goes there). The #81 SMART-solver anti-cheat suite (grade-the-inverse, band edges, place-undeclared, catch-false-flag) is deliberately RETIRED — the right trade for a free practice activity. Marina + the harbors/boats/buoy theme are unchanged (more accurate now); the abundance vessel was DROPPED (it read as a stray icon competing with the line). **DOCTRINE (program-wide): the §A.13.62 gates verify layout + anti-cheat but NOT comprehension — a design optimized purely for anti-cheat rigor can ship gate-clean yet be unusable by a child; the operator-eyeball comprehension check is load-bearing, and for free K-3 practice CLARITY > anti-cheat purity. The visual-critic rubric now includes "would a 7-year-old immediately understand what to tap?".** Verified: comprehension-critic CLEAN after labeling the ticks, adding the halfway-divide, distinct lighthouse-vs-lantern, single-"?" halfway, removing the stray vessel + duplicate prompt.

## Character #56 — Maple the mouse & the bakery / the chocolate-chip cookies / the plates / the open cookie-boxes (Game #61 "Maple's Bakery", 3.OA.A.2 interpret whole-number quotients — division) — the division companion to Game 52 (multiplication), built CLARITY-FIRST as a lcs-shell FREE activity
**Maple the mouse** is a cozy baker who needs help dividing a fresh batch of cookies. The child SEES the division happen and taps the number: **share** (partitive) — the cookies are shared equally onto a row of plates and the child taps how many are on EACH plate (answer = dividend ÷ divisor = the share-size); **pack** (quotitive) — the cookies are packed into boxes that each hold the same number and the child taps how many BOXES there are (answer = dividend ÷ divisor = the group-count). In both the number is dividend ÷ divisor; the MEANING differs (per-plate vs #boxes) — that duality IS 3.OA.A.2. **Moods/poses (for the eventual CA5 art):** Maple (idle / lean-in / happy-wiggle on a correct number / gentle nudge). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the **chocolate-chip cookies** (a tan disc + 4 brown chips), drawn in a SINGLE wide row per vessel so the count is legible + the scene fills the card width while staying short; the **plates** (wide teal ovals, cookies resting on top — the "share" vessel); the **open cookie-boxes** (tan trapezoid crates with the cookies peeking over the rim, the front wall drawn AFTER the cookies = an opaque containment wall — the "pack" vessel). The QUESTION (with the numbers) flows through the SHELL prompt banner (one prompt, no duplicate); the three answer choices are big DOM number cards (`.mb-cand`). A correct tap → the vessels glow + Maple wiggles; a wrong tap → a warm nudge ("count one plate" / "count the full boxes"), no advance, no buzzer, no score. Numbers ALWAYS real DOM/SVG `<text>` (EN pilot; 11-locale-ready).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST — operator-ruled):** the walk reached #59 "Coraline's Coin Counter" (2.MD.C.8 money) — found ALREADY built as Game #38 "Pip's Market Stall" (coin-stall), and #60 RL.K.2 retell already built (Wake-Up-Pip), so the next genuinely-UNOWNED entry was #61 division. The `divide-01.md` spec is an ABSTRACT anti-cheat design (grade the divisor's "structural placement" into a known-#groups vs known-group-size slot under grammar-neutralized prose). On an AskUserQuestion the operator chose **Division, CLARITY-FIRST** — the SAME clarity > anti-cheat-purity trade taken on the #81 rounding rebuild. So this game grades the visible, concrete share/group (deal-and-count is allowed — seeing + reading an equal grouping IS interpreting the quotient for a free K-3 practice activity); the spec's KEYWORD/SCENE/structural-placement solver suite is deliberately NOT implemented.
**A build lesson recorded (the desktop-sparse / row-stacking tension):** the FIRST layout stacked cookies in 2-3 ROWS per plate → tall+narrow vessels → to pass the resolved-state FITS gate at 1024×900 (the shell Check appears only when resolved, costing ~120px) the scene had to be SHRUNK → the visual-critic then flagged DESKTOP SPARSE ("a tiny picture floating in a big empty card", cookies hard to count). The fix that satisfied BOTH the FITS gate AND the sparse-critic: a **SINGLE wide row of cookies** → short+wide vessels that fill the card width (≤640px) without growing tall, plus much bigger answer cards. **Lesson: when a scene must be SHORT (a resolved-state fold budget) yet NOT sparse on desktop, make it WIDE-and-short (fill width, minimal vertical content) rather than compact — a single-row arrangement beats multi-row stacking on both axes.** Also: a long prompt sentence wraps to extra lines in the shell banner (eating the fold budget) — shortening "Share the 12 cookies equally onto the 3 plates…" → "Share 12 cookies onto 3 plates…" reclaimed the last ~37px. 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-maple-bakery-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100% answer=D÷divisor, answer-derived-not-stored, exact-division, ≥3 distinct choices, wrong rejected, ≥7 distinct, both cogs) + _maple-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, prompt interpolation, EN-only) + visual-qa 60 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #57 — Hopper the frog & the lily-pad stepping stones / the +5/+10/+100 hop arcs (Game #67 "Hopper's Lily Hops", 2.NBT.A.2 skip-counting by 5s/10s/100s) — built CLARITY-FIRST as a lcs-shell FREE activity; RENAMED from the spec's colliding "Pim the otter"
**Hopper the frog** skip-counts across a pond on a row of numbered **lily-pad stepping stones**, the dashed hops between pads carrying a "+5 / +10 / +100" arc label. One pad is **blank (a coral "?")** and the child taps the missing number; a second cog (`whichstep`) hides the labels and the child taps how big each hop is (+5/+10/+100). **Moods/poses (for the eventual CA5 art):** Hopper (idle / happy-wiggle on a correct tap / gentle nudge). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the **lily pads** (flat green ellipses with a cream wedge-notch, the number in dark teal, the blank a coral "?"), drawn in a SINGLE wide row so the scene fills the card width while staying short; the **"+step" hop arcs** (dashed teal quadratics, labelled on `fill` to scaffold the pattern, UNlabelled on `whichstep` so the child must derive the step; backward rounds show "−10" and the prompt reads "Counting down by 10s"). The QUESTION (with the step) flows through the SHELL prompt banner (one prompt, no duplicate); the three answer choices are big bold DOM cards (`.sc-cand`). Correct → the blank pad fills with the answer + glows + Hopper wiggles; wrong → a warm nudge, no advance, no buzzer/score. Numbers ALWAYS real DOM/SVG `<text>` (EN pilot; 11-locale-ready — the spec notes a numerals-only core sidesteps the number-word irregularities).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST — operator-ruled):** the walk after #61 hit defers/owned (#62 RL.K.3 = defer-class authored-story like #58; #63 2.OA.C.3 + #65 owned) → the clear unowned options were #64 affix word-meaning + **#67 skip-counting**; the operator chose **"#67 skip-counting, CLARITY-FIRST"** on an AskUserQuestion. The `skipcount-01.md` spec is a friction-heavy anti-cheat design (a produce place-value number-BUILDER + double-blank + an un-skippable forced wrong-press interstitial, to defeat "builder search" on a no-fail board) — deliberately SET ASIDE for clarity (same trade as #81/#61): a clear visible skip-count row, tap-the-number. The spec's "Pim the otter" character COLLIDES (Pim = char #2 AND #27) → renamed; the clearest skip-count metaphor is hopping (each hop = +step) → re-themed the abstract "stamp mill" to concrete lily-pad stepping stones (like Maple re-themed division to a bakery).
**A build lesson recorded (the desktop card-presence note):** the first pass shipped the answer cards at the Maple sizing (max-width 170, min-height ≤82) — the visual-critic flagged the DESKTOP fill-round cards as too small/thin + sparse (empty cream below), inconsistent with the bolder phone cards + the which-step cards. Fix: bigger desktop cards (max-width 200, min-height clamp to 96px, font clamp to 2.7rem, thicker border) → the taller cards fill the vertical space + read as the primary tappable target; critic PASS on re-review. **Lesson: a wide-short scene that fills the card width leaves the SHORT answer-card row looking sparse on a tall desktop card — size the answer cards generously (tall + bold) so they anchor the lower half; the FITS budget (the resolved-state shell Check) is the only ceiling.** 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-skipcount-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100% answer=start+k*step / |step|, answer-derived-not-stored + not-displayed, within-1000, ≥1 non-multiple-start + ≥1 cross-hundred, both cogs, ≥7) + _hopper-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, prompt interpolation incl. "counting down", EN-only) + visual-qa 66 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #58 — Marigold the mole & the Knowing Machine / the four operator-cogs (Game #64 "Marigold's Knowing Machine", L.2.4.b prefix-meaning + L.2.4.c suffix) — built CLARITY-FIRST as a lcs-shell FREE activity; the #53 morphology companion
**Marigold the mole** runs a brass **Knowing Machine**: a root word rides in, an affix-cog is installed, and the child reads the new word and taps what it means. Four clean single-sense operator-cogs: **un- = NOT · re- = AGAIN · -ful = FULL OF · -less = WITHOUT.** Two cogs: `apply` (the affixed word shows, e.g. "unkind"; tap the MEANING from 3 cards) + `which` (a root + a target meaning; tap the affix-cog that makes it). **Moods/poses (for the eventual CA5 art):** Marigold (idle / happy-wiggle on a correct tap / gentle nudge). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the **brass machine frame** (a tan/teal box + an inner plate + a dashed conveyor belt) with the affixed word (apply) or the root + a "?" cog-slot (which) as real DOM/SVG `<text>`; the **four operator-cog glyphs** (a gear ring + a symbol: un = a circle-with-slash NOT, re = a circular AGAIN arrow, -ful = a FILLED cup, -less = an EMPTY cup [outline + rim — the full/empty contrast reads before the label]); never object-pictures (an affix has no referent). The answer surface: `apply` = a row of 3 wide readable meaning cards (text wraps); `which` = a row of 3 big cog buttons (glyph + affix label + sense word). A correct tap → the answer lights + Marigold wiggles + a brief "what the cog does" line; a wrong tap → a warm nudge, no advance, no buzzer/score. Numbers/words ALWAYS real `<text>` (EN pilot; affixes are heavily language-bound — non-EN is a real per-locale fan-out, deferred).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST — operator-ruled):** the walk after #67 re-derived #64 as the lowest un-built UNOWNED entry (L.2.4.b/c = 0 manifests; the deliberate companion to the shipped #53 "Skip's Word-Welding Yard" compound-meaning game). The `affix-01.md` spec carries a sophisticated anti-cheat layer (a computed semantic-TUPLE + a per-item ≥3-realization `phrasings[]` table + a `PARAPHRASE_TEMPLATE_SOLVER` run against the rendered surface, to stop affix→gloss-keyword matching) — SET ASIDE for clarity (the operator's standing CLARITY-FIRST ruling, same as #81/#61/#67): simple readable meanings + derived-by-affix-match + a root-only distractor + a wrong-affix distractor. The spec's character "Marigold the tinkerer mole" is FREE (no collision) so it was kept.
**Two build lessons recorded:** (1) **the vertical-stack height trap** — a text-meaning game's first instinct is to STACK the meaning cards (readable), but a scene + a 3-card vertical stack is too TALL for the resolved-state FITS budget on a 900-tall desktop (apply hit 1039px). Fix = a ROW of wrapping text cards (one row, like the math number rows) + a shorter scene. (2) **the desktop-sparse re-balance** — the row of short cards then read SPARSE on desktop (empty band below), so the cards were enlarged (taller ~113px + bigger font) + the scene slightly grown to fill the band, bounded by the ~54px resolved-FITS headroom; the critic PASS came on re-review. Also: the **-less cup** was initially a bare "U" outline (ambiguous, read as a letter) → redrawn as a clear empty cup (outline + rim) paralleling the -ful filled cup. 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-affix-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100%, answer derived-by-affix-match not stored, root + wrong-affix distractors, word=root+affix spelling, all 4 families, both cogs, ≥7) + _marigold-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, prompt interpolation, 3-cards/3-cogs render, EN-only) + visual-qa 66 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #59 — Shelly the hermit crab & the tide-line / the measure-bar / the stacked X-marks (Game #71 "Shelly's Tide-Line", 2.MD.D.9 measurement line plot) — built CLARITY-FIRST as a lcs-shell FREE activity
**Shelly the hermit crab** measures shells and plots their lengths on a **tide-line** — a numbered scale (1–8) with **✕**-marks stacked above each length. Two cogs: `plot` (a new shell is drawn as a coral BAR from 0→its length; the child reads the length + taps it to drop its X) + `read` (a complete line plot + a question → tap a number card). **Moods/poses (for the eventual CA5 art):** Shelly (idle / happy-wiggle on a correct tap / gentle nudge). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the **tide-line** (a teal number line, whole-unit ticks 1–8 labeled, ✕-marks stacked above each length as real `<text>` glyphs); a coral **measure-bar** (0→length, with a dashed drop-line to its tick) for `plot`; number cards (`.tl-cand`) for the answer. **Read question types (a mix; the genuine VALUE-reads kept):** how-many-at-N, how-many-longer-than-N, most-common (mode, placed NON-extreme so the tallest stack isn't a free answer), longest-minus-shortest, how-many-more-at-A-than-B. A correct tap → for `plot` the X drops onto the scale (coral), for `read` Shelly wiggles + the answer states; wrong → a warm nudge, no advance, no buzzer/score. Authored shallow distributions (cluster + outlier, ≥1 zero-count interior gap). Numbers ALWAYS real `<text>` (EN pilot; 11-locale-ready).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST — operator-ruled):** the walk after #64 hit defers (#66 RL.1.6 POV + #68 RL.1.9 = abstract RL; #69 1.MD.A.1 = abstract transitive-reasoning core) → on an AskUserQuestion the operator chose **#71 line plot (concrete data)** as the clearest. The `line-plot-01.md` spec carries a deep anti-cheat layer (a `VALUE_PERMUTE_INVARIANCE` read-taxonomy splitting reads into region-reducible Tier-1 vs value-irreducible Tier-2, a session-granularity `VISUAL_REGION_COUNT_SOLVER`) — SET ASIDE for clarity (the operator's standing ruling), but the genuine VALUE-reads (longest-minus-shortest / how-many-more) are KEPT so it stays a real line plot, not a bar graph. Spec character was unnamed ("seaside") → Shelly the hermit crab (measures shells → a shell-length line plot, on-theme).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the plot-round measure-bar is the FITS constraint** — the `plot` scene (number line + the measure-bar below) is taller than the `read` scene, and its RESOLVED state (the shell Check appears) is what bumps the 900-tall-desktop fold; cap the PLOT scene maxWidth tight while letting the READ scene (more headroom) grow — a per-cog scene cap. (2) **`vw` resolves to the IFRAME width (~400px), not the screenshot viewport** — a `clamp(…, Nvw, …)` font sits near its MIN in the activity iframe, so the desktop numbers render small; the fix is a bigger rem FLOOR (vw-independent) + narrower cards so the single digit dominates (the recurring desktop-card-presence lesson, now with the vw-floor twist). (3) the verify mutation-test for derived-not-stored must assert the oracle RECOMPUTES correctly on changed data (a pure function), NOT "the answer must change" (adding a mark at the max changes neither the mode nor max−min → a false failure). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-line-plot-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100% vs an independent truth reimpl, derived-not-stored, ≥3 read types incl. value-reads, interior gap, mode non-extreme, ≥7) + _shelly-probe.js (FITS 320→1366, incl. read-resolved + plot-resolved) + local-test (wrong no-advance, ✕-marks render, prompt interpolation, EN-only) + visual-qa 66 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).
**STRATEGIC FLAG (from the spec):** the Grade 2-3 MATH white-space is now THIN — the near-term remaining INDEX entries skew ELA/RL (#66/#68 RL, #70 L.1.1.e, #72 RI.2.6). Surface at a strategic checkpoint whether to keep mining concrete-math, open the deferred abstract-RL/ELA entries (with a comprehension-careful design), or shift strategy.

## Character #60 — Fable the fox & the picture-story strip (Game #58 "Fable's Picture Stories", RL.K.1 ask & answer about key details in a story) — the CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN of the deferred #58; the FIRST build under the operator's "no-skip / redesign-all / 80-target" policy
**Fable the fox** shows a short **3-panel CAPTIONED picture-story**; the child reads/views it (a "🔊 Hear the story" button reads the captions aloud — pre-reader support), then answers a key-detail question by tapping a text card. One cog (`answer`) over 6 question types = the RL.K.1 facets: **who / what / where / order (sequence) / why / feeling.** **Moods/poses (for the eventual CA5 art):** Fable (idle / happy-wiggle on a correct tap / gentle nudge). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** a horizontal **3-panel strip** — each panel a small framed SVG scene (a ~12-glyph library: bunny / mouse / bird / friend-critter / tree / mitten / carrot / snow / sun / rain-cloud / umbrella / basket) + a ≤6-word **caption** (real DOM text, readable + TTS-able). 3 short stories: "The Lost Mitten", "The Stuck Carrot", "The Rainy Picnic" (~3-4 questions each → 11 rounds). The answer surface = 3 wrapping TEXT cards (`.ps-cand`); correct MATCHED (`response === round.answer`, never a stored index); distractors plausible-but-NOT-in-this-story. Correct → Fable wiggles; wrong → "look at the pictures again", no advance, no buzzer/score. The answer is grounded in the story (a content word ≥4 chars appears in the captions — the verify asserts it).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN — the POLICY CHANGE):** the operator ruled **"don't skip any activity — REDESIGN them; there should be 80 activities in the end."** This is the FIRST build under that policy (the previously-deferred abstract entries #58/#62/#66/#68/#69 are now in scope, redesigned clarity-first; the strict-next un-built non-duplicate was #58). The `story-01.md` spec was the program's most complex (WORDLESS picture-story + an entity-registry + a blind second-annotator + a HUMAN bypass-pilot ship gate + Pip-firefly/lantern theme colliding with Wake-Up-Pip) — I'd deferred it for art-dependence + HITL. The redesign keeps the standard's heart (recall & answer key details about THIS story) but makes it a clear CAPTIONED picture-story the child can read (RL.K.1 is "with prompting and support"); the wordless/entity-registry/blind-annotator/HITL rigor is SET ASIDE; fresh character (Fable, no Pip, no lantern).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the wordless→captioned fix is the model for redesigning deferred RL/comprehension specs** — a comprehension standard's "no-text-support" purity is the very thing that broke comprehension; a captioned strip + a Hear-it button makes it clear AND still instantiates the standard (recall key details). (2) **stub-glyph readability is load-bearing for a picture-story** — the visual-critic correctly rejected the first mitten (a red blob) + bird (a blue blob): for a PRE-READER the art must reinforce the caption, so each scene-glyph must read as its noun (the carrot story's clear glyphs were the model). Fixed by redrawing the mitten (thumb + ribbed cuff) + bird (head/beak/wing/tail/legs) + basket (rim + weave); ≤2 glyphs per panel (3 crowds at 360). (3) the recurring desktop-fill lever (wider strip + bigger card font) applied within the resolved-FITS budget. 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-picture-story-core.js (MEASURED — oracle=round.answer, derived-not-stored/matched, answer-grounded-in-captions, ≥3 qtypes, ≥3 stories, ≥7) + _fable-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, 3-panel strip + captions render, prompt carries the question, EN-only) + visual-qa 66 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design; full native per-locale story-authoring is a heavy future fan-out).

## Character #61 — Dot the dragon & the captioned story-strip (Game #62 "Dot's Story Spine", RL.K.3 story-grammar role-typing: setting/problem/solution) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #62; the 2nd build under the no-skip/redesign policy
**Dot the dragon** shows a short 3-panel CAPTIONED picture-story; each round asks for one narrative JOB ("Which part is THE START / shows THE TROUBLE / is THE FIX?") and the child taps the matching PANEL. The three captioned panels ARE the tap targets (no separate choice row). **Moods/poses (for the eventual CA5 art):** Dot (idle / happy-wiggle on a correct tap / gentle nudge). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** reuses the #58 captioned-strip + the FIXED glyph library (bunny/tree/mitten/bird/mouse/carrot/snow[improved]/sun/rain/umbrella/basket/friend); each panel = a framed scene (glyphs BIGGER than #58 — here the panel IS the answer card, so the glyphs must FILL it for the not-sparse gate) + a SHORT caption + a teal button border. **The panel display order is SHUFFLED per round** (position ≠ role → the child must READ each part, not tap "the middle"; role-typing is order-independent so shuffling is correct). A "🔊 Hear the story" button. Correct tap → the panel glows (gold) + Dot wiggles; wrong → a warm causal nudge ("the trouble is the part where something goes wrong"), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** 3rd RL game (story-01/02/03 = #58/#60/#62). The `story-03.md` spec is heavy anti-cheat (graded-per-role AFFECT_SOLVER, ELIMINATION_SOLVER, affect-matched swing-pairs, a load-bearing decoy, the entity-registry + blind-annotator + HUMAN bypass-pilot, role≠order machinery) — SET ASIDE for clarity; the standard's heart (assign each major part its narrative JOB) kept in a clear captioned-story + tap-the-part form. **Distinct from #58 by MECHANIC** (function-typing of the story PARTS, tapping a panel — vs #58's detail-recall via a text card); reuses the 3 #58 stories (one story → multiple games). RE-TAGGED from the catalog's mis-tagged RL.1.5 → RL.K.3 (also RL.K.5 story structure).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **when the PANELS are the answer cards, the not-sparse gate applies to them** (unlike #58 where panels were decorative + the text cards were the answers) → the scene glyphs must FILL the panel (bigger glyphs + a taller scene viewBox) AND the captions must be SHORT (a long caption stretches all panels tall via `align-items:stretch` → the svg becomes a small fraction → SPARSE; the picnic story's long captions were the culprit). (2) **a picture-story's beats must be VISUALLY DISTINCT for a pre-reader** — the original carrot story (mouse+carrot in all 3 panels) failed the critic (pictures didn't distinguish start/trouble/fix); reworked so WHO is in each panel carries the beat (owner mouse → thief bird → helper friend = setting/problem/solution), a legitimate K story-grammar cue. (3) **shuffle the display order** for a role-typing (order-independent) task so position≠role isn't a free cheat — the child reads each part; the tap maps via a `data-pi` story-index, not the Nth button. 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-story-spine-core.js (MEASURED — oracle=the role-matching panel, role-derived-not-stored, exactly-one-panel-per-role, ≥3 roles, ≥3 stories, ≥7) + _dot-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong-role no-advance, 3 tappable panels render, prompt carries the role, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #62 — Lumen the owl & the lighthouse creature-windows (Game #66 "Lumen's Lighthouse Windows", RL.1.6 point-of-view / who-is-telling) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #66; 3rd build under the no-skip/redesign policy
**Lumen the owl** keeps a lighthouse over the harbor. A first-person line describes a harbor event from one creature's VIEW (high → tiny/far; low → big/close); the child taps the creature (in its height-window) whose viewpoint it is. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Lumen (idle / happy-wiggle / gentle nudge). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** a row of 3 tappable **height-windows** (`.lw-cand`), each a LANDSCAPE SVG with a height-tinted background (**pale sky #E4F1FB = high**, cream = middle, **deep teal water #8FC3CF = low** — clearly distinct), an up/down arrow (↑↑ high / dashes mid / ↓↓ low), a big creature on a white halo (visible on any tint), + a short text label ("Up high" / "Middle" / "Down low"). So the height is shown THREE reinforcing ways (tint + arrow + label). Creatures (bird/mouse/friend/bunny) sit at VARIED heights per round (not a fixed cast → the child must read the position, not memorize). The first-person line flows through the SHELL prompt ("…" Who said it?); the event is a short say-row caption. Correct → the window glows + Lumen happy; wrong → a vantage nudge ("who is way up HIGH? up high, things look tiny and far"), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** RL.1.6 was the 4th RL build. The `pov-01.md` spec is heavy anti-cheat (a gate-consumes-the-render lock, a position-vs-canSee field split, `*_NOT_LEXICALLY_IN_LABEL` keyword lints, a teller-shift rung, a knowledge tier, a HUMAN bypass-pilot) — SET ASIDE for clarity. The grade-1-embodied heart kept: high→far/tiny, low→big/close (vantage on the high/low axis only; occlusion/facing-direction cuts the spec already made). DISTINCT cognition from the other RL builds (viewpoint attribution — reason from position → what they'd see; not detail-recall #58, not part-typing #62).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **a PORTRAIT shape (a lighthouse tower) can't be the answer-card content** — filling a wide card's width makes it too tall (FITS bust), but capping it small makes it a tiny fraction of the card (SPARSE). The fix: a LANDSCAPE card content (a height-TINTED window, wide-short) that fills a width-capped card without going tall — and convey the "height" by TINT + ARROW + LABEL rather than vertical tower position. (2) the recurring narrow-width SPARSE/long-label tension again — short labels ("Up high" not "High on the tower") + a taller window viewBox cleared the 320px sparse. (3) the critic's color-reinforcement note: when a cue is conveyed by COLOR, make the two poles clearly DIFFERENT hues (pale sky vs deep teal water) — two similar blues don't reinforce; + a white halo keeps the creature visible on a deep tint. 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-point-of-view-core.js (MEASURED — oracle=the view-matching creature, derived-not-stored, exactly-one-match-per-view + a high&low each round, arrangements varied [≥2 distinct layouts — not a fixed cast], both views, ≥7) + _lumen-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong-height no-advance, 3 windows + labels render, prompt carries the line, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #63 — Mossbeard the Story-Keeper & the two-moon tales (Game #68 "Two Moons", RL.1.9 compare-two-stories) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #68; completes the RL arc (comprehend #58 → retell #60 → role-type #62 → POV #66 → COMPARE #68); 5th build under the no-skip/redesign policy
**Mossbeard the Story-Keeper** is a calm mossy tortoise who keeps two short tales under two moons over one meadow. A first-person-free pair of tiny stories is shown — a GOLD-moon tale + a SILVER-moon tale — each a small animal-glyph scene + ONE short experience-summary line + a shared "Hear both tales" button. The child compares what happened to the two characters and taps one of THREE short experience-statement cards. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Mossbeard (idle / happy-wiggle on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** two moon-tinted tale panels (`.tt-tale`, gold-dot/"Gold moon" + silver-dot/"Silver moon" headers) side-by-side at ALL widths (one row-height, FITS-safe); a small animal glyph each (fox / bunny / mouse / bird / frog / duck — bunny/mouse/bird reuse the proven #58/#62 drawings, fox/frog/duck new with clear species cues); the experience summary in plain text. The SHELL prompt asks one of two flavors — "What is the SAME in both stories?" (`mode:same`) or "What happened in ONLY ONE story?" (`mode:diff`) — with SAME / ONLY ONE in ALL-CAPS for emphasis. Three `.tt-cand` experience-statement cards (e.g. "Lost it, then found it" / "A friend helped" / "Kept trying") are the tap targets; correct → the card glows + Mossbeard wiggles; wrong → a warm "Look again — read both tales" nudge, no advance, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each story carries 2 tags from a CLOSED experience set (LOST_FOUND / GOT_HELP / TRIED_AGAIN / SHARED / SCARED_SAFE / FIXED_IT). The correct card is computed by the mode predicate over the two tag-sets — SAME → the tag in BOTH (A∩B); ONLY ONE → the tag in EXACTLY ONE (A△B) — with NO stored isCorrect/correctIndex. SAME distractors are each true-of-one (tempting); ONLY-ONE rounds include the shared "true-of-both" tag as the tempting distractor + a "neither" tag. 9 rounds (5 same + 4 diff) over 6 stories in 7 distinct pairings.
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** RL.1.9 was the FRESH cross-text compare-mechanic completing the RL arc. The `compare-01.md` spec is the cohort's MOST anti-cheat-heavy (a per-enum varied-wording SYNONYM BANK, a gate that CONSUMES the rendered text via an EXPERIENCE_WORD_MATCH_SOLVER@chance + lexical-distance + NO_POLARITY_ONLY_DIVERGENCE asserts, PICTURE_VERDICT_MUTUAL_INFORMATION≈0, a HARD mute/art-hidden QA gate, a 2nd which-part-differs cognition, ~2× per-locale bank cost) — all SET ASIDE for clarity. Kept ONE clear compare-and-contrast mechanic (same / only-one across two tales). EN-only pilot (spec FORK-A: the most language-bound game — the entire stimulus is narrative prose; non-EN is a separate ~2× commission). DISTINCT cognition from the other RL builds (cross-text experience comparison, not within-text recall/role-typing/POV).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the tightest FITS budget yet** (two tale panels + say-row + Hear + 3 cards + the resolved shell Check ~+120px). The phone STACK busted hard; the fix was keeping the two tales SIDE-BY-SIDE at all widths (one row-height not two) + shorter card phrases + at the tiniest width (≤340) HIDING the decorative tale glyph (text-first; the glyph is warmth, the summary+Hear carry it) to reclaim the resolved-fold budget. (2) **the spec's central warning made real by the visual-critic: PROSE↔TAG DRIFT.** My first verify gate proved exactly-one-correct over the TAGS, but several summaries didn't visibly evidence both their tags (duck "shared lunch" was tagged helped; frog tagged scared but prose only said "felt safe"; fox tagged "kept trying" but prose didn't show it) → a child reasoning from the VISIBLE TEXT reached a different answer than the tag-oracle, and "true-of-both" distractors read as a 2nd valid answer. **The critic caught this (would have shipped a child-confusing game that passed the tag-gate); the fix = rewrite every summary to visibly show BOTH its experiences AND add a `PROSE_SUPPORTS_TAG` keyword gate to verify (each summary must evidence each of its tags via a regex) so the drift class is STRUCTURALLY caught.** This is the clarity-first, lightweight form of the spec's "gate consumes the render" — no lexical solvers, but the visible prose must agree with the oracle. LESSON: when an answer is a SET-EQUALITY/MEMBERSHIP over closed enums rendered AS TEXT, a tag-only gate is insufficient — you must also gate that the RENDERED TEXT supports each enum, or the oracle and the child diverge. 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-two-tales-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100%, exactly-one-correct, derived-not-stored, both modes, every card a closed-set tag with a phrase, a tempting distractor each round, PROSE_SUPPORTS_TAG, ≥7 distinct + ≥7 distinct pairs) + _mossbeard-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, two panels + 3 cards + Hear render, prompt carries same/only-one, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS (after the prose↔tag fix). EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #64 — Tilly the shop cat & the faraway shelves (Game #69 "The Faraway Shelf", 1.MD.A.1 order-by-length + indirect comparison) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #69; 6th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; a CONCRETE-MATH build (answers the standing Gr1-3 math-white-space flag)
**Tilly the shop cat** (an orange tabby) keeps things on far-apart shelves, so a measuring CORD (a fixed-length reference — the "third object") is the go-between. The child reads each ribbon against the cord, then taps the LONGEST / the SHORTEST / the one AS LONG AS the cord. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Tilly (idle / happy-wiggle on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** a teal STRIPED cord bar at top (labeled "the cord" on a small flag) ending at a dashed vertical CORD LINE; three color ribbons (red/blue/green/yellow/purple/orange), each on its own shelf-track, all from a common LEFT edge, drawn at their HONEST lengths (width = len/maxUnit) so each visibly extends PAST the cord line or stops SHORT of it; the dashed cord-line marker repeats on every shelf at the same track-percentage so "past/short of the cord" reads on each row; a color-name label per ribbon. Answer surface = three `.sr-cand` color CHIPS (swatch + color name) below. Tilly + the live win/nudge line in the say-row. Correct → the chip glows + Tilly wiggles; wrong → a warm per-mode cord nudge ("the longest goes PAST all the others" / "the shortest stops first" / "find the ribbon that ends right at the cord line"), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each ribbon carries a true `len`; the cord a `cordLen`. Correct is computed per mode — `longest`=max len, `shortest`=min len, `samecord`=the ribbon with `len===cordLen` — with NO stored isCorrect/correctIndex. Within a round all 3 lengths are distinct (longest/shortest unambiguous); samecord rounds have exactly one ribbon ==cordLen (others clearly past/short). 9 rounds (3 longest / 3 shortest / 3 samecord) over varied length-sets. The cord is the genuine indirect-comparison device (samecord REQUIRES comparing each ribbon to the cord; the dashed line makes longer/shorter-than-cord readable everywhere) → instantiates 1.MD.A.1's "compare two indirectly via a third"; longest/shortest = "order three by length."
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** 1.MD.A.1 was UNOWNED (cohort owns K.MD.A.2 direct-compare + 1.MD.A.2 unit-iteration + 2.MD.A.1 read-a-scale). The `seriation-01.md` spec is heavily anti-cheat — its centerpiece is **scale-inverted "lying pixels"** (the rendered length deliberately does NOT match the true length so a child can't eyeball), a frozen cord-segment "memory rail," `REMEASURE_IMPOSSIBLE`, `PREMISE_ROLE_FREQUENCY` solvers, and a genuine-40%-transitive ceiling. For a 6-year-old that inversion is the OPPOSITE of clarity, so per the §A.13.62 clarity-first ruling (clarity > anti-cheat purity for a free K-3 activity) ALL of it was SET ASIDE: HONEST pixels (drawn length = real length), the cord as a clear visible reference. The spec's "Pip the shop cat" character COLLIDES (Pip is the firefly of the live Game-60 "Wake-Up-Pip") → renamed **Tilly**. EN-only pilot (comparison-words are language-bound; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the recurring resolved-state FITS fold at 320×640** (the scene's cord row + 3 shelf-tracks + chips + the resolved shell Check ~+120px) — trimmed at ≤380 via shorter track height (24→20px) + tighter scene gap/padding + chip min-height 44 to clear the 24px overflow (the standard narrow-width compaction lever). (2) **honest horizontal bars are FITS-friendly** (wide-short) and dodge the not-sparse gate cleanly when the ANSWER surface is text color-chips (the length-bearing ribbons live in the display scene, not in answer cards) — the chips are text-bearing → not-sparse-exempt, the ribbons are wide divs → not-tiny-fine. (3) the dashed cord-line aligning across the cord bar + all 3 shelves (each track shares left:0, so the same track-percentage aligns vertically without a fragile scene-wide overlay) is what makes the indirect comparison READ — the visual-critic specifically confirmed it aligns in every round. **Visual-critic PASS first try** (no rework). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-seriation-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100% per mode, exactly-one-correct, derived-not-stored [oracle moves under length/cordLen mutation], distinct lengths, samecord-valid, ≥2 modes, ≥7 distinct + ≥7 sets) + _tilly-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, cord+3 shelves+3 chips render, prompt carries the mode question, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #65 — Juniper the robin & the clock tower's three time-windows (Game #70 "The Clock Tower", L.1.1.e verb tense past/present/future) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #70; 7th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; the first build in the unowned L.1.1 grammar family
**Juniper the robin** (brown body, red-orange breast) keeps a clock tower with three time-windows — **Before (🌙 past) · Now (☀ present) · Soon (clock→ future)**. Each round shows a sentence with a clear TIME word + a blank; the matching window lights; the child taps the verb form that fits the time. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Juniper (idle / happy-wiggle on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** three time-window cards (`.tn-win` — moon / sun / clock-with-forward-arrow glyphs + a Before/Now/Soon label; the round's time gets `.on` = teal highlight + glow, the other two dimmed) in a row; Juniper robin SVG + the live win/nudge line; a "Hear it" button (TTS reads the sentence stem + the three options); three `.tn-cand` verb-form chips (text = `verb.forms[tense]`, shuffled). Correct → the chip glows + Juniper wiggles; wrong → a warm per-time nudge ("'Yesterday' already happened — pick the PAST word" / "'Right now' is happening — pick the word for NOW" / "'Tomorrow' has not happened yet — pick the word with 'will'"), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each round = {time, timeWord, subject, verb:{lemma, forms:{present,past,future}, regular}}. The correct chip = the one whose tense key === `round.time` (form text = `verb.forms[time]`); NO stored isCorrect/correctIndex (the answer simply IS round.time; the FORM is derived). 9 rounds (3 past / 3 present / 3 future), 9 distinct verbs, 3 irregular pasts (went/ran→will run/rode). **Present rounds use PLURAL or "and" subjects** ("the kids jump", "Bo and Pat wave") so present = the BARE form — honors the L.1.1.c fence (NO 3rd-singular -s trap); future is always "will + base".
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** L.1.1.e was UNOWNED (the whole L.1.1 grammar family is). The `tense-01.md` spec is heavily anti-cheat — its centerpiece `OPTION_SURFACE_NON_DIAGNOSTIC` BANS the time cue (cue-absent assessment), HIDES the future "will" (a `FUTURE_WILL_SOLVER` must fail), avoids the novelty-outlier irregular, demotes the morph to a post-correct reveal, across 4 modes. For a 6-year-old that is BACKWARDS — recognizing "Yesterday → -ed" and "Tomorrow → will …" IS how L.1.1.e is learned — so per the §A.13.62 clarity-first ruling ALL of it was SET ASIDE: the time cue + the visible "will" are KEPT (good Grade-1 pedagogy, not a cheat). **Two character/theme fixes:** the spec's "Marigold the lighthouse keeper" COLLIDES (Marigold the mole = char #58 Game-64; the lighthouse is Lumen's Game-66) → reframed to a **clock tower** (fits "Before/Now/Soon" far better) with a fresh keeper. "Juniper" was free but "squirrel" was taken (chars #16 Acorn, #51 Skip) → Juniper is a **robin** (hedgehog=Quill #50; robin was only a prose mention). EN-only pilot (HEAVY per-locale: Finnish has no grammatical future, Germanic future is adverb-dependent; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) the recurring resolved-state 320×640 FITS fold (3 windows + say + Hear + 3 chips + the resolved shell Check ~+120px) — trimmed at ≤380 via smaller window glyphs (svg 40→24px) + tighter padding/gaps, leaving ~7px headroom (NOT 1px — a 1px pass is too fragile before the real visual-qa run; give comfortable headroom). (2) **the Hear button must stay ≥44px even in the narrow-width compaction block** — the ≤380 rule fires at 360 too (a visual-qa shot width), and visual-qa's tap≥44 gate measures ALL interactive controls, not just the answer chips; shrinking Hear to 40 at ≤380 would fail the 360 tap gate. (3) the highlighted time-window + the time word + the prompt give the child THREE reinforcing cues for the time, so the form↔time mapping is the only thing left to reason — clear + load-bearing. **Visual-critic PASS first try** (confirmed the -s trap is avoided in every present round + the window highlight matches the time word in all 9). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-tense-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100%, exactly-one-correct, derived-not-stored [oracle+form follow mutated time], distinct forms, future-well-formed "will …", present-bare no-3rd-sing-s, all 3 times balanced, ≥1 irregular, ≥7 distinct + ≥7 verbs) + _juniper-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, 3 windows + correct-highlight + 3 chips + Hear render, prompt carries the sentence+time, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #66 — Marlow the pelican & the harbor post (Game #72 "The Harbor Post", RI.2.6 author's purpose — inform/entertain/instruct) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #72; 8th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; MID-SIZE per spec
**Marlow the pelican** (cream body, long orange bill + a yellow pouch = mailbag) is a harbor postmaster who sorts notes by WHY the author wrote them, into three bins: "To tell facts" (inform) / "To tell a fun story" (entertain) / "To show how to do it" (instruct). The child reads one short note + taps the matching bin. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Marlow (idle / happy-wiggle on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** a "letter/note" card (cream paper, gold left border, the snippet as real DOM `<text>`) + a "Hear it" TTS button; Marlow pelican SVG + the live win/nudge line; the 3 `.ap-cand` bin chips (TEXT-only — the author-MOVE labels). Correct → the bin glows + Marlow wiggles; wrong → a warm per-purpose move-nudge ("This note tells TRUE FACTS…" / "This note is a FUN STORY with a silly surprise…" / "This note shows you HOW — look for 'first… next… then'"), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the answer:** each note carries its `purpose` (the authored ground truth — unavoidable for an authored-judgment standard); the 3 bins are the fixed purpose set, display-shuffled; `isAnswer(round, binPurpose) = binPurpose === note.purpose`; no stored correctIndex. 9 notes (3 inform / 3 entertain / 3 instruct), all CLEARLY single-purpose.
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** RI.2.6 was UNOWNED + a new RI axis (RI.K.2 main-idea=WHAT, 1.RI.5 locate=WHERE; author's-purpose=WHY). The `author-purpose-01.md` spec is dominated by a VALIDITY ceiling — author's purpose is inherently contestable, so the spec mandates a machine swap-invariance gate + a HUMAN BLIND-AGREEMENT panel (≥N native reviewers, tiered unanimity, quarantine-on-disagreement) + same-TOPIC-triads-only as the graded class. The human-panel / machine-adversarial / quarantine rigor is unrunnable in this loop → SET ASIDE per the §A.13.62 clarity-first ruling, but the spec's VALID core was KEPT: clear single-purpose prototypes (not contestable 50/50s — the clarity-first analog of "only defensible golds ship") + author-MOVE bin labels (never reader-effect — "to tell a fun story", NOT "to make you enjoy"). The spec's "Lighthouse Keeper" would be the THIRD lighthouse (Lumen #66 + the renamed #70) → reframed to a harbor POSTMASTER (sorting mail by intent maps perfectly to author's-purpose) — Marlow the pelican (pouch=mailbag). EN-only pilot (the catalog's most language-bound — humor + instruct-register are culture-bound; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the executable validity proxy** — replaced the unrunnable human blind-panel with a lightweight, NON-TAUTOLOGICAL `matchesPurpose` structural gate that reads each note's PROSE against its claimed purpose: instruct = ≥2 sequence markers (first/next/then/last); entertain = <2 markers AND a "!"; inform = <2 markers AND no "!". A mislabeled note FAILS the build. This is the clarity-first form of "the gold is valid" — it reads the actual text, not just the tag. (2) **the text-heaviest activity busted FITS hard at 320×640** (a ~28-word note wraps to ~7 lines on a 290px screen → ~150px over). Fixes: SHORTEN every note to ~15-20 words (still clear 2-3 sentences, still passes the structural gate) + DROP the Hear button only at ≤340 (the note is on-screen text; reclaim the resolved-fold budget — the #68 hide-at-≤340 pattern) + 3 bins in a single row at narrow. (3) **an SVG ICON in an answer card TRIGGERS the visual-qa not-sparse gate** — the first build gave each bin an icon+label; visual-qa flagged SPARSE (area 0.06: a 30px icon in a ~200px-wide bin → the gate measures the svg as the answer-content). A CSS background swatch (#69) does NOT trigger it, but an `<svg>` does. **Fix = TEXT-ONLY bins** (the gate's "text-only buttons SKIPPED" rule; the author-MOVE labels are self-explanatory for a reading activity, like #70's verb chips). LESSON: for the not-sparse gate, an answer card with an `<svg>`/`<img>` child is image-bearing (its content must fill ≥0.32 area / ≥0.45 width); to stay exempt, make answer chips text-only (or use a CSS-painted cue, not an inline svg). **Visual-critic PASS first try** (confirmed all 9 notes validity-clean — no contestable 50/50 — + author-move labels). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-author-purpose-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100%, derived-not-stored, the MATCHES_PURPOSE structural validity gate, 12-40 words, balanced 3/3/3, ≥7) + _marlow-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, note+3 bins+Hear render, prompt carries the question, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #67 — Glim the light-wisp & the describing words (Game #73 "Glim's Describing Words", L.2.1.e adjective vs adverb — choose by what is modified) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #73; 9th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; MID-SIZE per spec
**Glim the light-wisp** (a luminous little teardrop with a glow + face) helps finish sentences in the House of Soft Light. Each round shows a sentence with a blank; the prompt names what the blank describes; the child picks the right describing word — the adjective (for a thing) or the adverb (for an action). **Moods/poses (CA5):** Glim (idle / happy-glow on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the sentence as real DOM `<text>` with a `.wc-blank` underline slot (fills with the chosen word + a coral tint on resolve); Glim wisp SVG + the live win/nudge line; a "Hear it" TTS button (reads the sentence with "blank" + the question); two `.wc-cand` form chips (the same base word's adjective + adverb forms, e.g. quick / quickly, shuffled). Correct → the blank fills + Glim glows; wrong → a warm per-kind function nudge ("'quickly' tells how an ACTION happens; the blank describes the rabbit — a THING" / vice-versa), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each round = {sentence (with "___"), target (the modified word), targetKind:'noun'|'verb', base, forms:{adjective,adverb}, q}. `oracle = targetKind==='noun' ? 'adjective' : 'adverb'`; each chip carries its class; `isAnswer(round, class) = class === oracle` — NO stored correctIndex (the grader reads only the chip's class vs the modified target's kind, never the chip text). 9 rounds (4 adjective / 5 adverb), 2 dual-class bases (quick + loud each used as an adjective in one round + an adverb in another — the spec's signature, kept). The PROMPT names the modified word ("describes the rabbit" / "tells how the rabbit hopped") → the pick is a FUNCTION judgment (thing→adjective, action→adverb), not pure -ly suffix-matching.
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** L.2.1.e was UNOWNED (only generic L.2.1 "Sentence Surgeon" #2). The `wordclass-01.md` spec is dominated by the ADJACENCY cheat — in English a modifier sits glued next to its target ("a fast CAR" / "ran FAST"), so "tap the adjacent content word" passes ~95% WITHOUT judging function; the spec's fix (force every load-bearing round NON-adjacent + ≥3 candidates + a SEMANTIC_FIT solver + dual-class-every-round, point-at-the-word-in-the-sentence) collapses the cheat-proof corpus to ~2 templates (the source of the difficulty). Per the §A.13.62 clarity-first ruling ALL of that SET ASIDE: the standard's heart kept as a clean FORM-PICK (choose adjective vs adverb for a blank, driven by what's modified) — the function-naming prompt makes it a real function judgment, and the -ly contrast (which the spec DISTRUSTS for assessment-purity) is kept as good first-time-learning pedagogy. The spec's "Pip, a thread-of-light ghost" COLLIDES hard (Pip = the firefly of Game-60 + Pim the otter) → renamed the wisp to **Glim** ("ghost" is just prose elsewhere; "wisp" free). EN-only pilot (the anti-cheat lever is English's -ly; Romance/German/Finnish need different designs — agreement / flat adverbs; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** light-content activity (a 1-2 line sentence + 2 text chips) → FITS-comfortable at all widths (no narrow-width trimming needed — contrast the text-heavy #72). The fill-the-blank-on-correct feedback (replace "___" with the chosen word + a coral tint) is a clean, satisfying resolve cue for a sentence-completion activity. **Visual-critic PASS first try** (confirmed prompt-target matches blank position + exactly one answer in all 9; the dual-class recurrence reinforces the contrast). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-wordclass-core.js (MEASURED — oracle 100% [adjective↔noun / adverb↔verb], exactly-one-correct, derived-not-stored [oracle flips under mutated targetKind], 2 distinct forms + q + "___" blank each, both kinds balanced, ≥1 dual-class base, ≥7) + _glim-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, sentence+2 chips+Hear render, blank fills on correct, prompt names the modified word, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #68 — Nib the apostrophe-spirit & the contraction seat (Game #77 "Nib's Apostrophe Seat", L.2.2.c contractions) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #77; 10th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; MID-SIZE per spec; the FIRST entry reached after a run of owned-duplicate gate-skips
**Nib the apostrophe-spirit** (a coral apostrophe/comma-curl with a little face) stands in the empty seat a dropped letter leaves. Each round shows two words (do + not); the child picks the correctly-written contraction. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Nib (idle / happy-glow on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the two source words as real DOM `<text>` (`word1 + "+" + word2`, the "+" coral); Nib coral apostrophe-curl SVG + the live win/nudge line + a "Hear it" TTS button; three `.ct-cand` contraction chips (the correct / a misplaced-apostrophe / a no-apostrophe spelling, shuffled). On correct: the words line grows a "→ <contraction>" reveal + a memorial note ("The apostrophe stands right where the missing letters were!"); Nib glows. Wrong → a warm nudge ("the apostrophe stands EXACTLY where the missing letters were — look for that spot"), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each round stores the OMISSION STRUCTURE {word1, word2, combined (de-apostrophe'd letters, e.g. "dont"), apostropheIndex, wrongIndex, irregular} — NEVER the contraction literal. The core derives the 3 chip strings by inserting "'" into `combined` (correct = at apostropheIndex → "don't", misplaced = at wrongIndex → "do'nt", none = "dont"); `isAnswer(round, str) = str === deriveCorrect(round)`. Apostrophe POSITION is graded (do'nt / dont both wrong). 9 rounds across 5 families (n't / 's / 're / 'll / 'm) + 2 IRREGULARS (won't ← will not, can't ← cannot) authored as flagged wholes (their `combined` stored directly — no clean-drop rule needed).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** L.2.2.c was the FIRST genuinely-UNOWNED entry after a run of OWNED-DUPLICATE gate-skips — #74 (3.MD.A.2 → live `pond-juice`), #75 (3.OA.A.2 → live `maple-bakery` #61), #76 (3.NF.A.1 → live), #78 (2.MD.D.10 → live) are all already-built (count toward 80, like #59/#60; the gate-check at the trigger caught them). The `contraction-01.md` spec is a PROCESS-axis anti-cheat: its build-by-dropping-letters mechanic is brute-forceable (≤5-letter search + free undo + a pre-commit oracle → trial-and-error to green with zero omission knowledge), so it mandates no-pre-commit-oracle + commit-then-grade-with-cost + a TRIAL_AND_ERROR_SOLVER + FAMILY_RULE_SOLVER + cross-family-≥4 + discriminating-≥50%. Per the §A.13.62 clarity-first ruling ALL of that SET ASIDE by choosing a mechanic with NO brute-force surface — a clean 3-way recognition PICK (correct / misplaced / no-apostrophe). The standard's heart (right letters dropped + apostrophe at the omission site) is what the pick tests; the distractors embody the two common mistakes. The spec's "Wisp" COLLIDES (Glim "the light-wisp" = char #67) → renamed to **Nib** (pen-nib → writing/ink; "Tuck" was prose-ambiguous, "Nib" free). EN-ONLY-by-design (the omission-apostrophe contraction is English-specific; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **gate-check forward through owned codes** — when the next INDEX entries' CCSS codes are already in a live manifest, they're owned-duplicates: skip-past (counts toward 80) and land on the first genuinely-unowned entry. Cross-check via `grep "code" mini tools/*-activities.json`. (2) **derived-not-stored for a SYMBOL output** — store the de-apostrophe'd `combined` + the index, derive the contraction by inserting "'"; the verify deep-scans that NO stored string contains an apostrophe (the answer literal is never stored — a strong, clean derived-not-stored proof for a symbolic-output game). (3) **the visual-critic's I/l-bar catch (clarity polish)** — "I'll / Ill / Il'l" renders as near-identical vertical bars in the sans-serif (the I and l are the same glyph shape), making the hardest-to-read chip set on the exact skill being taught; swapped that round for "you + will → you'll" (lowercase, distinct apostrophe positions, no real-word distractor). LESSON: for an apostrophe/letter-position activity, AVOID words whose letters render as identical bars (capital-I + l); the discriminating mark (the apostrophe) must sit among clearly-distinguishable letters. (Kept I'm — the `m` keeps it readable.) **Visual-critic PASS** (all 9 rounds chip-by-chip correct, irregulars right, apostrophe positions distinct; the I'll→you'll polish addressed its one non-blocking note). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-contraction-core.js (MEASURED — derive=one-apostrophe-at-index, 3 distinct chips, oracle 100%, derived-not-stored [no apostrophe literal stored; oracle changes under mutated index], ≥2 families, ≥2 irregulars, ≥7) + _nib-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, words+3 chips+Hear render, contraction shows on resolve, irregular won't correct, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #69 — Pearl the pond-moon & the Doubling Pond (Game #79 "The Doubling Pond", L.2.1.b irregular plural nouns) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #79; 11th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; MID-SIZE per spec
**Pearl the pond-moon** (a round pale moon reflected in the pond) reflects the TRUE plural, never the lazy +s. Each round shows a singular noun; the child taps the correct plural — the special word, not +s. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Pearl (idle / happy-glow on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the singular word as real DOM `<text>` (becomes a "singular → plural" reveal on resolve); Pearl moon SVG (pale disc + craters + face + a pond ripple) + the live win/nudge line + a "Hear it" TTS button; three `.pl-cand` plural chips. Correct → the `foot → feet` reveal + a memorial note ("a special word, not the lazy +s!" / no-change: "<word> stays the same — already many!"); Pearl glows. Wrong → a warm per-rule nudge ("<word> is a special word — it changes, and it does NOT just add an s" / for no-change: "<word> stays the SAME — don't add an s!"), no advance, no answer-reveal, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each round stores {singular, rule: vowel-change|suffix|no-change, ruleSpec} — NEVER the plural literal. `derivePlural`: vowel-change = `singular.replace(ruleSpec.from, ruleSpec.to)` (foot→feet "oo"→"ee", man→men "a"→"e", mouse→mice "ouse"→"ice"); suffix = `singular + ruleSpec.add` (child + "ren" = children); no-change = identity. The 3 chips = {correct (derive) / +s overreg (singular+"s") / unchanged-singular (transform) or +es (no-change)}; `isAnswer(round, str) = str === derivePlural`. 9 rounds: vowel-change oo→ee (foot/tooth/goose), a→e (man/woman), mouse→mice; suffix (child→children); no-change (deer, sheep).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** L.2.1.b was UNOWNED (#78 2.MD.D.10 was an owned-dup, skipped). The `plural-01.md` spec RE-CENTERED away from a menu-select onto a constrained build-from-tiles, because a 2-3 menu has guess-floors: the always-wrong +s is SCHEMA-ELIMINABLE (the game teaches "+s is punished" → drop it → 50%), + an auditory-recognition shortcut + a pre-highlight leak. Per the §A.13.62 clarity-first ruling the constrained-build / MENU_GUESS_SOLVER / CUE_CLASSIFY / pre-highlight / no-pre-commit-oracle rigor SET ASIDE in favor of the clearest Grade-2 form — a 3-way PICK — but **the spec's main concern is addressed in the DISTRACTOR design**: the transform rounds carry the UNCHANGED SINGULAR (a no-s wrong form), so "drop anything with an s" leaves {correct, singular} → the child must know the form actually changes. EN-only-by-design (the irregular-plural SET is language-specific + disjoint — de umlaut Fuß→Füße, fr -aux; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **derived-not-stored for a no-change word needs a singular-exclusion in the leak scan** — for no-change words plural===singular, and the singular STEM is legitimately stored (it's the displayed word), so the verify's "no stored string equals the plural literal" deep-scan must EXCLUDE the singular field (else fish/deer/sheep false-flag). The real invariant: no SEPARATELY-stored plural literal. (2) **the visual-critic's "fishes" catch (correctness, not just clarity)** — "fishes" is a technically-valid English plural (multiple species), so as a no-change distractor it could mark a knowing child wrong. Fix = swap the `fish` round for `deer` (also canonical no-change), where the generic +es distractor "deeres" is unambiguously not a word — keeps the natural +es distractor pattern AND removes the only real-word-collision. LESSON: when a generic distractor rule (singular+"es") can accidentally produce a REAL alternate form for one word, swap the WORD rather than the rule (deer/sheep have no real +es/+s plural; fish does). (3) the unchanged-singular distractor is the clarity-first answer to the spec's eliminate-+s concern — it blocks the cheat WITHOUT the build-from-tiles complexity, and teaches "it does change" alongside "not +s". **Visual-critic PASS** (all 9 forms correct, layout clean; the one optional "fishes" note actioned via the deer swap). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-plural-core.js (MEASURED — derive vs a known-forms table, 3 distinct chips, oracle 100%, derived-not-stored [singular-excluded leak scan + mutate-ruleSpec], blocks-eliminate-s on transform rounds, ≥2 rules, ≥2 no-change, ≥7) + _pearl-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, singular+3 chips+Hear render, reveal on resolve, deer no-change correct, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #70 — Hattie the hare & the Lost-and-Found Hat Shop (Game #84 "The Borrowed Hat", L.1.1.d personal pronouns by case) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #84; 12th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; TIGHT per spec
**Hattie the hare** (tan, long ears, a little green shop-hat) runs the Lost-and-Found Hat Shop. Each round shows a sentence with a blank; the child gives the character the right pronoun "word-hat" for its job. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Hattie (idle / happy on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** the sentence as real DOM `<text>` with a `.pn-blank` underline slot (fills with the chosen pronoun + coral tint on resolve); Hattie hare SVG + the live win/nudge line + a "Hear it" TTS button; two `.pn-cand` pronoun chips. Correct → the blank fills + a per-role note ("It is DOING the action!" / "The action happens TO it!" / "It shows what BELONGS to someone!"); Hattie glows. Wrong → a warm per-role nudge ("Who is DOING it? Use the doing-word" / "happens TO them — the getting-word" / "BELONGS to them — the owning-word"), no advance, no answer-reveal, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each round stores {sentence (with "___"), referent (case-set key i/he/they/we), role, wrongRole, cap, kind} — NEVER the pronoun literal. The core's `CASE_TABLE` (i: I/me/my, he: he/him/his, they: they/them/their, we: we/us/our) + `displayForm(referent, role, cap)` derive the words. The 2 chips = [form(role), form(wrongRole)] of the SAME referent (he/him, I/me, his/him, They/Them, their/them, us/we); `isAnswer(round, str) = str === deriveCorrect`. 9 rounds: subject (he/they), object (him/them/us), possessive form-by-function (his/their), compound-subject (Sam and I), compound-object (Mom called Sam and me).
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST REDESIGN, no-skip policy):** L.1.1.d was the first UNOWNED entry after a run of owned-dup gate-skips (#80 3.MD.C.6 BUILT [mosaic-menders]; #81 3.NBT.A.1, #82 3.OA.A.1, #83 3.MD.D.8 all live). The `pronoun-case-01.md` spec is heavily anti-cheat — it WITHHOLDS the role-cue to post-commit, grades ONLY "ear-resistant" items (compound case + form-by-function), models the rendered screen with a ROLE_AFFORDANCE_READ_SOLVER, mandates hard-discrete-commit. Per the §A.13.62 clarity-first ruling the grade-only-ear-resistant / role-cue-withhold-mechanics / role-affordance-read / hard-discrete-commit / position-heuristic / drop-and-ear rigor SET ASIDE — BUT TWO spec elements KEPT because clarity-aligned: (a) the **TWO-TILE SAME-REFERENT board** (both chips are one pronoun's two case forms → CASE is the only axis, NO gender clever-hans); (b) the **FORM-BY-FUNCTION vein** (his↔him, their↔them — function sets the form). The clean mechanic (sentence + 2-form pick) shows NO role cue, so "withhold the cue" is satisfied for free; and simple subject/object cases KEPT as valid first-learning (the spec excludes them as "ear-solvable" — an assessment-purity stance, not a clarity one). The spec's "Pim the owl" COLLIDES (Pim = otter #2/#27) → renamed **Hattie the hare** (hat-shop pun). EN-only-by-design (case systems diverge — German 4-case, Romance clitics, Finnish suffixes; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the two-tile same-referent board is the clean clarity-first answer to the gender-cheat** — when an answer = role × features, making both chips the SAME referent's two case forms removes the feature (gender/number) axis entirely, so CASE is the only discriminator (no need for the spec's role-cue-withhold machinery; there's nothing to read but the two forms). (2) the no-answer-leak check (the derived pronoun must not be a standalone token in the sentence) is the pronoun analog of the derived-not-stored scan — verified per round. (3) capitalization is not a cue when BOTH chips share the round's casing (He/Him both capitalized sentence-initial; he/him both lowercase mid-sentence; "I" always capital is just orthography, and it's correct in one round / wrong in another so not a reliable pick). **Visual-critic PASS** (all 9 rounds a clean single-pronoun case-contrast, correct answers, layout clean). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-pronoun-core.js (MEASURED — displayForm vs CASE_TABLE, 2 distinct same-referent chips, oracle 100% [role-correct accepted, wrong-case rejected], derived-not-stored [role-mutation crosses case], no-answer-leak, all 3 roles + ≥1 each compound-subj/compound-obj/form-by-function, ≥7) + _hattie-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (wrong no-advance, sentence+2 chips+Hear render, blank fills on correct, EN-only) + visual-qa 54 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #71 — Bram the magpie & the Wobble Museum (Game #85 "The Wobble Museum", W.K.2 informative-writing topic focus) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #85; 13th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; the program's FIRST writing-production game; FIRST text-sentence-tap answer surface
**Bram the magpie** (a black-and-white collector — the perfect museum curator) keeps a museum where every room is about ONE thing, but exhibits keep wandering in. The child reads a room's sign + 4 facts and taps the one that wandered in from another room. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Bram (idle / happy on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** a room-sign header ("This room is all about frogs.") + 4 tappable `.wm-line` sentence rows (real DOM `<text>`, shuffled order) + a compact footer row [Bram magpie SVG + the live win/nudge line + a "Hear" TTS button, all in ONE row]. Tap a line → if it's the drift (about !== topic) → the line glows `.lit` + a "for-later shelf" note ("That is true — but it is a <about> fact. It wandered in!"); Bram happy. Wrong (an on-topic line) → a warm nudge ("That one IS about frogs — it belongs here! Find the label that wandered in."), no advance, no answer-reveal, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each room stores {topic, topicWord, sentences:[{text, about}]} — every sentence carries an `about` label; the drift is the sentence where `about !== topic` (exactly one). `oracle(room)` = that index; NO stored correctIndex. Re-pointable: change room.topic → the drift moves (relevance is RELATIVE to the committed topic, not absolute). 8 rooms (frogs/moon/fire-trucks/rain/dogs/bees/trees/the-body), drift at 4 distinct positions, display shuffled per round.
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST, no-skip policy):** W.K.2 was UNOWNED — the program's FIRST writing-production family. Unusually, the `informative-writing-01.md` spec's CORE mechanic (CATCH-THE-DRIFT) is GENUINELY CLEAR + age-appropriate (the documented K "knowledge-telling drift"), NOT an anti-cheat contortion — so it was KEPT as the spine + the KEYWORD-DEFEAT (≥1 on-topic fact lacks the topic word; the drift shares vocab → it's about TOPIC not word-matching). SET ASIDE only the heavy multi-phase rigor: the authorial commit-topic-BEFORE-facts flow, the separate graded re-home phase, the hard-lure-only grading, and the CATEGORIZATION_BIN/TOPIC_AS_CLUSTER/OBVIOUS_LURE solver suite. The spec's "Curator Pip the hedgehog" COLLIDES (Pip=otter; hedgehog=Quill #50) → renamed **Bram the magpie**. EN-only-by-design (topic/fact content + relevance relations + keyword-defeat foils are language+culture-specific — a per-locale native-authored fork, not a fan-out; non-EN 404-by-design).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the shell PROMPT banner is a real fold-budget cost on desktop** — a long prompt ("One label wandered in from another room. Which one?") wrapped to 2 lines at 1024 (146px), and combined with this activity's tall 4-row content it CUT OFF at 1024×900 (passed at 1366 only because the wider prompt fit 1 line). Fix = a SHORT prompt ("What wandered in?", 1 line ≈73px). LESSON: for a tall (4+ row) activity, keep the shell prompt SHORT enough to be 1 line at 1024 — the prompt height is a shared budget the content competes with; a 2-line prompt + tall content tips the resolved fold. (2) **a 4-stacked-tappable-rows answer surface is the tallest yet** — merge the Bram + live-msg + Hear into ONE footer row (saves a full ~44px row) + keep rows at the 44px tap floor + tight gaps; measure section heights (a throwaway puppeteer boundingRect dump) to find the true tall culprit rather than trimming blindly (the culprit was the PROMPT, not the rows). (3) **the visual-critic's VALIDITY catch (the real save)** — two "shared-word" drifts were arguably ON-topic: "Water puts out fires" (a fire truck's core FUNCTION) + "Honey is sweet" (a bee PRODUCT, with an on-topic "They make honey"). RULE: a shared-word drift must be a genuinely SEPARATE object/organism/place (birds/plants/stars/cats/ants/stop-signs), NOT a product/function/sub-part of the topic — else a child reasonably reads it as belonging. Fixed → "Stop signs are red." (shares "red", separate object) + "Ants live in the dirt." (clean separate organism). **Visual-critic PASS after the 2-room fix.** 5 protected cores + lcs-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-wobble-museum-core.js (MEASURED — exactly-one-drift, oracle 100%, derived-not-stored [re-pointing the topic moves the drift], keyword-defeat, drift-positions-varied, ≥6 topics, ≥7) + _bram-probe.js (FITS 320→1366) + local-test (on-topic no-advance, sign+4 rows+Hear render, drift resolves + for-later note, EN-only) + visual-qa 48 renders + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #72 — Crumb the mouse & the Same-Amount Bakery (Game #86 "Crumb's Same-Amount Bakery", 3.NF.A.3 equivalent fractions) — CLARITY-FIRST redesign of #86; 14th build under the no-skip/redesign policy; the LAST designed INDEX entry
**Crumb the mouse** (a tiny round grey bakery mouse, big pink-lined ears, pink nose + whiskers) runs a bakery where a treat cut into littler pieces stays the same size. Each round shows a REFERENCE fraction (stacked numerals + a proportion-shaded bar) and 3 candidate fractions (shaded bars + numerals); the child taps the candidate showing the SAME amount — the equivalent fraction. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Crumb (idle / happy-eyes on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** a `.fe-ref` reference card (one wide SVG = stacked num/line/den + a segmented proportion bar, coral=shaded / pale-mint=unshaded, teal outline) + a row of 3 tappable `.fe-cand` cards (each ONE card-filling SVG = a segmented bar + stacked numerals; the SVG is the visual-qa "answer content" so not-sparse/not-tiny pass) + a compact footer row [Crumb mouse SVG + the live win/nudge line + a "Hear" TTS button]. Tap a candidate → equivalent → card glows `.lit` + "1/2 and 3/6 — the same amount, just more pieces!"; Crumb happy. Wrong (a non-equivalent bar) → warm nudge ("that bar is a little more/less — find the one just as full as 1/2"), no advance, no answer-reveal, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** each round stores {ref:{num,den}, candidates:[{id,num,den}]} — NO correctIndex/answer/equivalent field. Equivalence is derived by INTEGER CROSS-MULTIPLY: `isEquivalent(a,b)=a.num*b.den===a.den*b.num`; `oracle` = the candidate index equivalent to ref. Re-pointable: set ref to any candidate's value → the oracle follows it (equivalence is relative to the committed ref). 8 rounds across grade-3 denominators {2,3,4,6,8}, mixed direction (fewer→more pieces AND more→fewer: 1/2→2/4/3/6/4/8, 1/3→2/6, 2/3→4/6, 3/4→6/8, 1/4→2/8, 2/4→1/2), equiv authored at 3 distinct indices, display order shuffled per round. Distractors STRADDLE (one a little less, one a little more) so the equal one is "not too much, not too little".
**Build context (CLARITY-FIRST, no-skip policy):** 3.NF.A.3 was UNOWNED (3.NF.A.1 = the fraction-forge; A.3 equivalence is the distinct sub-standard). The `fraction-equivalence-01.md` spec re-centered onto a heavy comb-rake → child-glaze-proportional → "did the dough move?" motion-judgment + an 8-solver suite (KXK_PROCEDURE / ENGINE_AUTOSHADE / COMB_VOCABULARY_BRUTE_FORCE / …). SET ASIDE per CLARITY-FIRST — the standard's OWN model ("recognize/generate equivalent fractions … using a VISUAL FRACTION MODEL") is the clear mechanic: proportion-shaded bars + the symbolic fraction, tap the same amount. Proportion is SCALE-INVARIANT (a half-full bar looks half-full at any width) so the ref bar (wide) and candidate bars (small) compare by fullness, and the numerals tie the visual amount to the symbol → genuine equivalence, not eyeball-only. Clean sibling `fraction-equiv-core` (window-shim, seriation-core idiom), distinct from the live `fractions-core` PARTITION engine (which CUTS one shape into equal parts; this RECOGNIZES equal amounts across two fractions). EN-only-by-design (non-EN 404; the math is locale-neutral, a fan-out is a low-cost future task).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the RESOLVED state is a fold cost visual-qa never measures** — visual-qa never clicks to resolve, so the shell `.lcs-activity-check` (hidden until `.crumb-resolved`) is absent from its sweep; the _crumb-probe (which DOES resolve) caught a desktop cut-off at 1024/1366 h=900 (resolved ctrlBottom 927 > 896) that visual-qa passed. FIX = cap the desktop card/ref sizes (`.fe-cand` max-width 160→118, `.fe-ref` 380→300) so the resolved stack fits (→866). LESSON: always run the probe's RESOLVED state at the 900-tall desktop viewports — the Check appearing is ~120px that the layout must still fit; visual-qa alone is blind to it. (2) **proportion is the scale-free equivalence signal** — bars of different widths still compare correctly because "how full" is a ratio; this let the wide ref + small candidates coexist without forcing equal widths. (3) **straddle the distractors** (one less, one more) rather than both same-side — the visual-critic flagged both-same-side rounds as acceptable-but-weaker; one-less/one-more reads clearer for "find the SAME amount." 5 protected cores + lcs-shell + game-shell 0-line.
