Gates: verify-fraction-equiv-core.js (MEASURED — exactly-one-equivalent, oracle 100%, integer cross-multiply, derived-not-stored [ref-mutation follows each candidate; reorder grade-stable], proper fractions + denoms⊆{2,3,4,6,8}, distractors non-equivalent, equiv-positions-varied, ECHO/RANDOM/fixed-position solvers FAIL, ≥7) + _crumb-probe.js (FITS 320→1366 incl. resolved) + local-test (non-equivalent no-advance, ref+3 cands+Hear render, EN-only, reshuffle, 280-768) + visual-qa 48/48 + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #73 — Tuck the hamster & the Ten Bundles (Activity "Tuck's Ten Bundles", 1.NBT.C.4 add-with-regrouping / compose-a-ten) — the FIRST DEFERRED PRODUCTION-CORE after the game-designs catalog-walk (operator chose option 3 "deferred production-cores" → place-value regrouping); a NEW base-ten manipulative engine
**Tuck the hamster** (a round golden hamster with full cheek-pouches — the perfect "tuck ten ones into one bundle" metaphor) helps a child add a two-digit and a one-digit number when the ones make more than ten. Each round (e.g. 27 + 5) shows base-ten blocks; the child taps "Make a ten" to bundle 10 ones into one ten-rod, then reads the regrouped blocks and types the total. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Tuck (idle / happy-eyes on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS; pure geometry — NO image-library → no 404):** a two-column place-value MAT — a TENS column (teal border) of notched ten-rods + an ONES column (coral border) of unit-cubes (the b ones tinted "just arrived") with live "tens (N)" / "ones (N)" labels — + a compact action row [tiny Tuck + the coral "🔁 Make a ten" button | the result caption]. The shell renders the number-keypad (`answerType:'number'`) + Check below. Tap "Make a ten" → 10 cubes bundle into 1 rod (onesCount−=10, tensCount+=1, button hides); read the blocks; type the total; Check.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** a round stores ONLY {a, b}. The sum, decomposition (tens/ones), start-state counts, and "needs a compose" are ALL derived (`a+b`, `Math.floor`/`%`, `ones(a)+b≥10`); NO stored sum/answer/total. The grade = `Core.gradeAnswer(round, answer, onesCount)` = `answer===a+b && onesCount<10` → typing the right total WITHOUT bundling is rejected (a hint "First tap Make a ten") → **the compose-a-ten action is load-bearing AND the predicate is testable without DOM**. 8 rounds, every round composes exactly one ten (ones(a)+b∈[10,18], sum≤99), distinct sums; per-pass `nextTask` order-only reshuffle (the array-activity contract). Re-pointable: mutate a/b → sum changes.
**Build context (deferred-production-core direction):** the game-designs INDEX catalog-walk hit depletion at #86; the operator chose "deferred production-cores." Gate-check of the disciplined-set deferred-core backlog: K.CC.C.6 (compare-groups) + 2.MD.A.1 (drag-measure) are OWNED codes → dropped; **1.NBT.C.4 + 2.NBT.B.7 (regrouping) UNOWNED** → operator chose place-value regrouping. This fills the explicit OPERATE gap in the live E12 place-value family (`place-value-core` only REPRESENTS N; never operates). Activity #1 = 2-digit + 1-digit ADD compose-a-ten (1.NBT.C.4, Grade 1). NEW clean-sibling `place-value-regroup-core.js` (the array-core/place-value-core public contract: strings/init/setupTask/render/paint/reset/injectCSS + pure helpers; node-safe at load) + `place-value-regroup-activity.js` (Object.assign over the core + nextTask + keypad task wiring), **0 lines to the protected `place-value-core` / any of the 5 protected cores / lcs-shell.* / game-shell.***. **CONFIG-READY** (`operation:'add'|'subtract'`, `places:2|3`) so the 2.NBT.B.7 fast-follow (subtraction/decompose-a-ten borrow + 3-digit) rides this same core. EN-only pilot (math + numerals locale-neutral; only "Make a ten" + title localize → a trivial 11-locale fan-out is a cheap follow-on, like E12). The build target is a lcs-shell FREE `/activities/` build (NOT a premium game).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **a block-manipulative + the shell keypad is the TALLEST surface yet** — the keypad is a fixed ~190px below the stage, so the stage must be brutally compact: collapse the mascot/caption into the ONE action row (no separate say-row), shrink rods/cubes, tighten gaps. (2) **the ones column wrapping to a 3rd row is a hidden +15px fold cost** — startOnes 12 = 2 rows, 13-14 = 3 rows → widen the ones-block row (7 cubes/row at ≤380) so 14 ones stay 2 rows; tens column set `flex-wrap:nowrap` so 7 thin rods stay one row. (3) **the shell `.lcs-activity-answer-display` BALLOONS ~70px once it shows the typed number** — a shell-keypad characteristic shared by array/ten-frame, only visible in the transient post-success state; the official visual-qa gate never resolves, so FITS is checked on the INTERACTIVE states (the child reads/bundles/keypads) + the resolve is asserted FUNCTIONALLY (the post-success display-balloon + Next-below-fold is shell-inherent, not this activity's layout — do NOT chase it by gutting the manipulative). (4) **`answerType:'number'` has no answer cards** → visual-qa's not-sparse/tap-card checks are skipped; FITS + no-overflow + not-tiny are the live gates; the `_tuck-probe` (which bundles → resolves) owns the functional + interactive-fold check. (5) visual-critic: the ten-rod notches must be pronounced enough at phone sizes to read "rod = ten units" (bumped notch stroke 0.9→1.3 / opacity 0.7→0.95). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell + game-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-place-value-regroup-core.js (MEASURED — needs-compose every round, sum derived ≤99, bundle arithmetic exact, regroup load-bearing via gradeAnswer, derived-not-stored, ≥7) + _tuck-probe.js (regroup-required + resolve-functions + button-hides + interactive-FITS 320→1366) + local-test (the keypad path: type-without-bundle→no-resolve, bundle→3 tens+2 ones+button-gone, type→resolve+caption, EN-only, reshuffle, 280-768) + visual-qa 48/48 + visual-critic PASS. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

### Tuck #73 — addendum: 2.NBT.B.7 fast-follow BUILT ("Tuck Breaks a Ten", 3-digit subtraction, decompose-a-ten)
The operator's chosen fast-follow on the place-value-regroup core. Activity #2 = **"Tuck Breaks a Ten"** (`place-value-regroup.subtract-decompose.2-nbt-b-7`, Grade 2) — a 3-COLUMN base-ten mat (hundreds-FLATS | ten-rods | unit-cubes) showing a 3-digit minuend (e.g. **342 − 5**). Because there aren't enough ones to take away, the child taps **"🔁 Break a ten"** → 1 ten-rod becomes 10 ones (tens 4→3, ones 2→12); the last `b` ones render with a dimmed **✕ "take away"** mark (5 marked → 7 remain), and the child reads 3 hundreds + 3 tens + 7 ones = **337** and types it on the keypad. One clean cognition: **decompose a ten** (the inverse of #1's compose-a-ten). The hundreds column is shown (3-digit / within 1000) but is NOT decomposed — **decompose-a-HUNDRED** (double-borrow, e.g. 302−5) + 3-digit-ADD-compose are further follow-ons on this same core.
**Same Tuck, same engine** (no new character) — the core gained the op-aware `subtract`/`places:3` paths (the config-ready design paid off): pure helpers `diffOf`/`needsBorrow`/`gradeSubtract(round,answer,decomposed)=answer===a−b && decomposed` (borrow load-bearing + DOM-free testable), a `breakTen()` action, a `_flat()` (hundred = gridded square), op-aware `setupTask`/`render`/`lockAndCaption`, and a synchronous per-activity title swap (array-activity pattern) so the subtract iframe header reads "Tuck Breaks a Ten." The ADD activity (#1) stayed byte-compatible (regression green). **Zero new tracked edits** — the subtract row went into the already-registered `place-value-regroup-activities.json`; 0 lines to any of the 5 protected cores / `lcs-shell.*` / `game-shell.*`.
**Build lessons:** (1) a 3rd column adds WIDTH not height (height = tallest column) → no-overflow at 280→768 is the binding gate; ones is the widest column (`flex:1.4`) since it holds 12 post-break; tens/hundreds capped (tens(a)≤5, hundreds(a)≤4) so the 3-column mat fits 320. (2) **the action bar's `min-height:44` over-reserves in the button-ABSENT (broken/resolved) states** — the button keeps its OWN `min-height:44` (tap), so dropping the bar reserve to 36 reclaims ~8px exactly where the post-break 12-ones needs it (the broken-state 320 fold fix). (3) the visual-critic's base-ten magnitude note (a hundred should read bigger than a ten) is fold-constrained at 320 with 3 columns — addressed within budget (flat 28px desktop with a stronger grid, bolder ✕) rather than full-rod-height (which busts the 320 width); labels + color + grid carry the rest (critic PASS). Gates: verify-place-value-regroup-core.js (extended — BOTH rows) + _tuck-subtract-probe.js (borrow-required + resolve-functional + 3-col interactive-fold) + local-test-place-value-regroup-subtract.js (break→12 ones/5 marked/caption) + visual-qa 48/48 (both activities) + visual-critic PASS + personal Read pre+post-break 360/768/1024. EN-only pilot. NO deploy.

### Tuck #73 — addendum 2: full 2.NBT.B.7 hundred-level coverage BUILT (compose + decompose a hundred)
Operator: "both." Two more activities on the place-value-regroup core (now 4 total): **"Tuck Makes a Hundred"** (`place-value-regroup.add-compose-hundred.2-nbt-b-7`) — 3-digit addition where the tens reach ten (e.g. 160+50: 11 tens → "🔁 Make a hundred" → 1 flat + 1 ten = 410); and **"Tuck Breaks a Hundred"** (`place-value-regroup.subtract-decompose-hundred.2-nbt-b-7`) — borrow ACROSS ZERO (e.g. 302−5: 0 tens → "🔁 Break a hundred" [1 hundred→10 tens] → "🔁 Break a ten" [1 ten→10 ones] → 5 ✕-marked take-away → 297). Both Grade 2, EN-only, same Tuck/engine.
**Core deltas:** `makeHundred()` (tens≥10 → −10 tens/+1 hundred) + `breakHundred()` (ones short & tens=0 & hundreds≥1 → −1 hundred/+10 tens); op+state-aware button dispatch (Make a hundred / Make a ten / Break a hundred / Break a ten); `gradeAddHundred` (tens<10 load-bearing) + reuse `gradeSubtract` for B (the final break-ten sets `_decomposed`, so the FULL cascade is required — one break alone won't resolve); a `_newTens` tint; a **per-activity "fat column" root class** (`fat-tens` for compose-a-hundred — the tens column reaches ≤11 rods). The other two activities stayed regression-green; 0 lines to any protected core / `lcs-shell.*` / `game-shell.*`; ZERO new tracked edits (rows in the already-registered manifest).
**Build lessons:** (1) **3 columns + a block-heavy column is the program's tightest fold** — fixed by the fat-column class (busy column gets `flex:1.9`) + **thin rods** (≤380 width 7px, desktop height 36) so ≤11 tens (compose) and 9 tens (decompose-post-cascade) stay ONE row; (2) **the dump proved 11 rods fit / 13 wrap** at 320 → capped compose-a-hundred rounds to tens(a)+b/10 ≤ 11 (still composes a hundred) rather than chasing 13-in-a-row; (3) **decompose-a-hundred post-cascade ones must be ≤13** → capped ones(a) ≤ 3 (post-cascade ones = ones(a)+10 ≤ 13, 2 rows); (4) desktop rod height (was 46px) made the 11-tens column overflow 900 at 1024 → 36px. Gates: verify (4 rows, both hundred branches MEASURED) + `_tuck-hundred-probe` (make-a-hundred required + the break-hundred→break-ten cascade required, both BUSY interactive states fit 320→1366) + `local-test-place-value-regroup-hundred` (cascade + titles + captions) + visual-qa 48×4 + visual-critic PASS (both) + personal Read pre+post-regroup 360/768/1024. NO deploy.

## Character #74 — Coco the koala & the Sound Boxes (Activity "Coco's Sound Boxes", RF.K.2.d phoneme isolation) — the deferred PHONEME/ELKONIN production-core (operator-chosen after the place-value-regroup core)
**Coco the koala** (round grey koala, big round listening ears = a good listener, big soft nose) helps a child isolate the FIRST / MIDDLE-vowel / LAST sound in a three-sound (CVC) word, using the iconic Elkonin "sound boxes." Each round: a target CVC word as a picture + a row of 3 connected boxes with ONE highlighted (the target position) + a "🔊 Hear it" (whole word); the child taps one of 3 candidate picture tiles whose word shares that position's sound. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Coco (idle / happy-eyes on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS + REAL image-library pictures):** the target tile (a `@2x.webp` from the image library) + the 3 `.sb-box` Elkonin boxes (the target-position box coral-highlighted) + the "Hear it" button (inline beside the boxes — fold-critical) + a row of 3 tappable `.sb-choice` picture tiles (image-library `@2x.webp`, alt='', aria-label=noun) + Coco + the live win/nudge line. Tap a tile → if its position-phoneme matches the target's → glow `.lit` + a "cat and cap have the same first sound" note + Coco happy (resolve). Wrong → warm position-specific nudge ("that middle sound is different…"), no advance, no reveal, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** RF.K.2.d's gradeable, whole-word-audio form (a child's spoken sound can't be graded; isolated-phoneme TTS is unreliable — the §20.7/E7-E9 mute-per-tile lesson) → MATCH the position-phoneme across CVC-word PICTURES, the Elkonin boxes the segmentation scaffold. A round stores `{position:'b'|'m'|'e', target:{noun,themeDir,b,m,e}, options:[…b,m,e]}` — each word carries its 3 phonemes as SOUND keys (k for c/k so grapheme≠phoneme never mis-matches). `isAnswer(round,i)=options[i][position]===target[position]`; oracle = that index; NO stored correctIndex. Re-pointable: change position → a different option matches (the verify mutation: breaking the oracle phoneme→0 matches, making a distractor share it→2). 9 rounds, 3 each first/middle/last (medial-vowel + final are the unowned RF.K.2.d core vs the owned RF.K.3.a initial-sound), 7 distinct targets, per-pass `nextTask` reshuffle.
**Build context (deferred production-core, EN-only):** the place-value-regroup core done, the operator chose the next backlog core = phoneme/Elkonin (RF.K.2.d UNOWNED). Explored data: phonemes derive from `scripts/worksheet-gen/data/literacy/{beginning,middle,ending}-sounds.json` + `scripts/v2-data/vocabulary-phonics.json` (`pat:CVC`, `vs:short_a`); CVC pictures at `/image-library-webp/themes/<theme>/<noun>@2x.webp`. NEW clean-sibling `sound-boxes-core` (window-shim, pure cognition) + `sound-boxes-activity` (skin); 0 lines to any of the 5 protected cores / `lcs-shell.*` / `game-shell.*`; EN-only-by-design (phonemes language-specific, like E7/E8/E9 — non-EN 404). The verify gate cross-checks every word is a clean 3-phoneme CVC + in `approved-words-en.json` (§20.5 gate) + has a COLOR `@2x.webp` (§20.5 color-only).
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **picture RECOGNIZABILITY is a blocking gate for a pre-reader picture-match activity** — the visual-critic caught 4 unrecognizable image-library assets (net=racket, mop=broom, fan=pinwheel, bun=bread-loaf) landing on TARGETS/CORRECT-tiles → fatal (a child can't name them); rebuilt the rounds with only obviously-recognizable concrete nouns (cat/cap/sun/dog/bat/bag/hat/pig/pot/van/jug/bed/can) + DROPPED the scarce/ambiguous medial-/i/ vowel entirely (used /a/,/u/,/o/ medials). LESSON: for any picture-stimulus activity, run the visual-critic recognizability pass + prefer concrete everyday nouns; the image-library label ≠ a recognizable picture (net→racket). (2) **the shell prompt is ~200px tall at desktop AND wraps to 2 lines when long** (the #85 lesson again) — combined with a picture stimulus (target pic + boxes + Hear) + picture-option tiles, the stage overran the desktop fold; fixed by SHORT prompts ("Same MIDDLE sound as Cat?") + a compact stimulus (Hear-it inline with the boxes, target pic 56px, tiles ≤104px). (3) whole-word audio only (no isolated-phoneme TTS in the grade path). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell + game-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-sound-boxes-core.js (MEASURED — exactly-one-match, oracle, live-derived, positions varied b/m/e, every word clean-CVC+approved+color-imaged, ≥7) + _coco-probe.js (FITS 320→1366, picture-tile tap≥44) + local-test-sound-boxes.js (render + images-load-no-404 + non-match-no-advance + resolve + EN-only + reshuffle) + visual-qa 54 + visual-critic PASS (after the recognizability rebuild). EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design).

## Character #75 — Wiggles the caterpillar & the Sentence Builder (Activity "Wiggles' Sentence Builder", L.1.1.j + L.K.1.b) — the LAST deferred production-core (the productive-language/grammar engine); the deferred-core backlog is now exhausted
**Wiggles the caterpillar** (a green, multi-segment caterpillar — a line of body segments = words in order = a sentence; tiny antennae, friendly face) helps a child build a complete declarative sentence by putting scrambled word-tiles in order. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Wiggles (idle / happy-eyes on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS + a REAL image-library subject picture):** a subject-noun picture (`@2x.webp`, the sentence's noun) + a row of empty `.snt-slot`s (one per word, dashed→solid when filled) + a scrambled `.snt-palette` of word-tiles (`.snt-tile`, capital-determiner + period-verb baked on) + a "🔊 Hear it" (reads the CURRENT built arrangement via `type:'ui'` — self-monitoring, not the answer) + Wiggles. Tap a tile → it fills the next empty slot (and hides from the palette); tap a filled slot → returns it. Then the shell **Check** grades. Correct → slots lock + the sentence reads aloud + Wiggles happy + the shell celebrates. Wrong → the shell hint (incomplete → "use all the words"; wrong order → "start with the capital word, end with the period, make it make sense"), no advance, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** "produce a sentence" is open-ended → the clarity-first gradeable form = arrange scrambled tiles into ONE canonical order, curated to a UNIQUE grammatical order (Determiner/number/possessive + Adjective + Noun + intransitive Verb) so exact-order grading is unambiguous. A round stores `{canonical:[words…], subject, seed}` — the canonical sentence is the content (like cvc-builder's target word), NOT a stored correctIndex. `gradeOrder(placed, canonical)` = same words in the same order; the scramble is `Core.scramble(canonical, seed)` (deterministic, guaranteed ≠ canonical). Re-pointable: change the canonical order → the old arrangement fails. 9 sentences (The big dog runs / A little cat naps / The hot sun shines / Six brown hens peck / The fast bus stops / My green frog hops / The wet pig digs / Two black bats fly / The old van honks), 9 distinct subjects, ≥4 words each, per-pass `nextTask` reshuffle.
**Build context (last deferred-core, EN-only):** sound-boxes done, the operator chose the final backlog core = sentence-building (L.1.1.j + L.K.1.b UNOWNED). NEW clean-sibling `sentence-builder-core` (window-shim pure cognition) + `sentence-builder-activity` (skin) reusing the word-builder tap-to-place tile→slot pattern with 0 lines to it / any of the 5 protected cores / `lcs-shell.*` / `game-shell.*`; EN-only-by-design (word order is language-specific — non-EN 404); answerType:'state' with the shell Check VISIBLE (arrange-then-check, unlike the auto-resolve tap-card activities). No grammar data existed → the 9 sentences authored here.
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **the determinism guard = baking the capital onto the determiner tile + the period onto the verb tile + an adj-noun middle** makes a SECOND valid sentence structurally impossible (the capital can only start, the period can only end, "big dog" never "dog big") → exact-order grading is safe. The visual-critic's #1 gate ("could the tiles form another valid sentence?") confirmed all 9 rigid. LESSON: for an arrange-into-one-order activity, design the tile set so only ONE grammatical order exists (intransitive verbs, no linking-verb adj/predicate swaps, no two-noun SVO), and bake the capital/period cues onto the boundary tiles. (2) variable-width WORD tiles in two rows (slots + palette) must `flex-wrap` + compact font so a 4-5-word sentence fits 320 without overflow. (3) Hear-it reads the CURRENT arrangement (productive self-monitoring), not the target (avoids audio-transcription). 5 protected cores + lcs-shell + game-shell 0-line. Gates: verify-sentence-builder-core.js (MEASURED — ≥4 words, capital+period, no-dup, ordered-grade strict [every swap rejected], scramble-differs, derived [order-mutation], subjects color-imaged, ≥7) + _wiggles-probe.js (empty/built/resolved FITS 320→1366, slots+tiles tap≥44, wide-row no-overflow) + local-test-sentence-builder.js (build-in-order resolves + wrong/incomplete don't + tap-to-return + image-loads + EN-only + reshuffle) + visual-qa 54 + visual-critic PASS (second-valid-order gate clean). EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design). **With this, the deferred production-core backlog is EXHAUSTED** (place-value-regroup ×4 + sound-boxes + sentence-builder).

## Character #76 — Sprocket the rooster & Sprocket's Clock (Activity "Sprocket's Clock — Read the Clock to the Hour", 1.MD.B.3) — the TIME-EXPANSION commission, activity #1; the `clock-digital` engine (read analog → match the digital time)
**Sprocket the rooster** (a warm round rooster — red comb + wattle, golden-brown body, a clock/time mascot who "crows the hours") helps a child read an analog clock at o'clock times and tap the matching digital time. **Moods/poses (CA5):** Sprocket (idle / happy-crow on correct). ~512², transparent.
**Props / scene (code-drawn DOM/SVG/CSS, no image-library):** an analog clock SVG (`.cd-clock`, viewBox 0 0 100 100 — face circle + 12 upright numerals + 12 ticks + a long thin coral MINUTE hand + a short thick teal HOUR hand, both via `handAngles(h,m)` rotate-transforms) on top + a row of 3 `.cd-choice` DIGITAL-time cards ("3:00") + a "🔊 Say it" speaker + Sprocket + the live win/nudge line. Tap a card → if its time matches the clock → glow `.lit` + a "Yes! The clock says 3 o'clock — 3:00." note + Sprocket happy (resolve, `.sprocket-resolved`; shell Check hidden until resolved). Wrong → warm nudge ("Look where the SHORT hand points — that's the hour."), no advance, no reveal, no buzzer/score.
**The cognition + the derived oracle:** read-the-analog-clock's gradeable form = read the face → tap the matching DIGITAL time of 3 (the analog↔digital connection, the whole point of 1.MD.B.3). A round stores `{id, target:{h,m}, options:[{h,m}]}` — the digital card text is rendered from `{h,m}` ("h:mm"), the analog face from `handAngles(h,m)` (hour 30h+0.5m, minute 6m, COUPLED). `isAnswer(round,i)=options[i].h===target.h && options[i].m===target.m`; oracle = that index; NO stored correctIndex. Re-pointable: change `target` → a different option matches (the verify mutation: target→a distractor's time → the oracle follows). 9 rounds across distinct hours, m=0 (granularity "hour"), 3 options each (exactly one match, 2 distractors = other o'clock hours), 7+ distinct targets, per-pass `nextTask` reshuffle.
**Build context (TIME-EXPANSION commission, EN-only pilot):** the operator commissioned ≥10 new time activities covering the screenshot spec (reading granularities Hour/Half/Quarter/Five-min/Minute + operations Elapsed/12↔24-Convert/Add-Subtract + Analog/Digital), **one per plan-mode session**. Gate-check confirmed NO existing activity does analog→DIGITAL reading/matching or any operation (the protected `clock-core` SETS the hands; `clock-read-core` reads-to-EVENT) — code-duplication across time standards is EXPECTED here (the breadth IS the product). NEW clean-sibling `clock-digital-core` (window-shim pure cognition — `handAngles` re-implemented, NOT imported from the protected `clock-core`) + `clock-digital-activity` (skin); a SINGLE engine designed to serve the reading + matching granularity/direction variants #1-7,11 as config (the operations #8-10 get their own small engines). 0 lines to any of the 5 protected cores / `clock-core` / `lcs-shell.*` / `game-shell.*`; EN-only-by-design (digital times are locale-neutral; the prompt localizes — an 11-locale fan-out is a cheap follow-on). The ONLY tracked edit = the `activities.ts MANIFEST_FILES` row.
**Build lessons recorded:** (1) **a small analog clock reads as "rotated numerals" / an ambiguous hour hand only at thumbnail size** — at the actual desktop render (180px) and full-res the numerals are upright + the hands clearly distinct; my first-pass misread (1:00-vs-5:00 at the 150px desktop thumbnail) was a small-image antialiasing artifact, NOT a render bug (a full-res zoom proved ch-5 = hour hand exactly on 5). LESSON: judge analog-clock legibility from a HIGH-RES crop of the clock element, not the small full-page desktop thumbnail — and size the clock generously (bumped `min(46vw,150px)`→`min(52vw,180px)`; the probe showed ~250px of fold headroom at desktop). (2) **digital-time cards are TEXT-only answer cards → exempt from the visual-qa not-sparse/not-tiny image measure** (they fill the card with bold time text; tap≥44 + FITS still apply) — the analog clock is the non-card STIMULUS (not measured). (3) 12:00 / near-12 hours overlap the two hands (both ~straight up) — inherent to clocks, acceptable for to-the-hour, and the distractor set never offers a confusable o'clock so a plausible-but-wrong tap is impossible. 5 protected cores + `clock-core` + `lcs-shell` + `game-shell` 0-line. Gates: verify-clock-digital-core.js (MEASURED — exactly-one-match, oracle, COUPLED handAngles correct [3:00→90°/0°, 9:30→285°/180°], granularity-in-scope [m=0], derived-not-stored [oracle follows a mutated target], ≥6 targets/≥7 rounds) + _sprocket-probe.js (roundA/roundB/wrong/resolved FITS 320→1366, digital cards tap≥44, no overlap/overflow) + local-test-clock-digital.js (clock face + 12 numerals + ticks + 2 hands + 3 cards render, wrong-no-advance, correct-resolves + note, EN-only, ≥7 + reshuffle, no overflow 280→768) + visual-qa 54/54 + visual-critic CLEAN + personal Read 360/768/1024. EN-only pilot (slug.en → 404 non-EN by design). NO deploy. **The first of the ≥10 time activities; the engine is reused by the reading/match granularity variants (#2 Half-Hour next).**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum: TIME-EXPANSION activity #2 "Read to the Half-Hour" BUILT (1.MD.B.3, the first `clock-digital` granularity variant)
The second time activity reuses the `clock-digital` engine + Sprocket (#76 — no new character) as a granularity variant: `clock-digital.read-half-hour.1-md-b-3`, granularity `"half"` (o'clock + half-past). **Genuinely distinct from #1:** in #1 the minute hand is always on 12 so only the HOUR hand is read; in #2 the **minute hand is load-bearing** — targets mix `:00`/`:30` and every round carries a **same-hour minute-trap** distractor (3:30 target with a 3:00 distractor) so a child who reads only the short hand fails. The half-past hour hand correctly sits BETWEEN two numbers (the key half-past visual cue) via the coupled `handAngles` (3:30 → hour 105°, minute 180°).
**Files — ZERO new git-tracked edits:** added a 2nd row to `clock-digital-activities.json` (slug `read-clock-to-the-half-hour`, 9 rounds, each = target + same-hour-minute-trap + same-minute-other-hour-trap); `clock-digital-activities.json` was already in `MANIFEST_FILES` from #1 → `activities.ts` UNCHANGED. The core needed NO change (`GRAN.half={m:[0,30]}`, `spoken()` "half past 3" already shipped). Two small activity-layer tweaks (untracked, 0 protected-core/clock-core/shell): a **granularity-aware hint** (`hint` for hour = short-hand line; `hintMin` for half/quarter/… = "Read both hands — the long hand tells the minutes." — the #1 short-hand hint is wrong when the discriminator is the minute hand) + **enlarged the desktop clock `min(52vw,180px)→min(58vw,230px)`** (the visual-critic's recommendation — the entire half-hour skill is reading the subtle ~15° hour-hand dip, so it must be legible at desktop; the probe's fold headroom absorbed +28% — resolved @1024×900 = 890 ≤ 896). Generalized `_sprocket-probe.js` + `local-test-clock-digital.js` to accept `--activity=<id>` and force rounds by POOL INDEX (not hardcoded `ch-*` ids) so #3-7,11 reuse them with no per-variant clone.
**Build lessons:** (1) **a granularity variant on a config-driven clock engine is a manifest row + tiny copy** — no new core, no new character, ZERO tracked edits (the manifest was registered in #1); the verify gate auto-covers the new row (it loops every manifest row reading `params.granularity`). (2) **enlarge the analog clock on desktop when the skill IS the subtle hand position** (half-past dip) — the small-render legibility note recurs (#1 + #2); fix the layout (bigger clock, fold headroom permitting), never the gate. (3) the minute-trap (same-hour, different-minute distractor) is the design lever that makes "to the half-hour" a genuinely harder/distinct skill than "to the hour" while reusing the identical engine. Gates: verify-clock-digital-core.js (18 rounds, both deck rows, MEASURED) + _sprocket-probe.js + local-test-clock-digital.js (both `--activity` ids + #1 regression) + visual-qa 54/54 both ids + visual-critic CLEAN + personal Read 360/768/1024 (half-past dip clear at desktop after the enlargement). EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #3 "Read to the Quarter-Hour" (2.MD.C.7; granularity:"quarter" — `spoken()` already handles m=15/45).**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 2: TIME-EXPANSION activity #3 "Read to the Quarter-Hour" BUILT (2.MD.C.7, the cleanest `clock-digital` variant — manifest row only)
Third time activity, the leanest variant yet: `clock-digital.read-quarter-hour.2-md-c-7`, granularity `"quarter"`, slug `read-clock-to-the-quarter-hour`, Sprocket #76 reused. **ZERO code changes — a single manifest row.** The engine already supported quarter (`GRAN.quarter={m:[0,15,30,45]}`); `spoken()` already returns "quarter past 3" (m=15) / "quarter to 4" (m=45); the granularity-aware `hintMin` (added in #2) already serves it; the enlarged desktop clock (`min(58vw,230px)`, from #2) carries over; the verify gate auto-loops the new row; the probe/local-test (generalized to `--activity` in #2) reuse as-is. **Distinct from #2:** #2 = :00/:30; #3 = the NEW quarter past (:15, minute hand on **3**) and quarter to (:45, minute hand on **9**) — 5×:15 + 4×:45 targets. Two load-bearing reads: (1) the minute-POSITION (3 vs 9), via every round's same-hour minute-trap (3:15↔3:45); (2) the quarter-TO hour read — at 3:45 the hour hand is nearly on 4, so "3:45" (quarter to 4, still the 3 o'clock hour) must be told apart from the next-hour hour-trap "4:45" (present on all 4 quarter-to rounds). The win note connects the two representations: card "3:45" + note "Yes! quarter to 4."
**Gate gotcha resolved:** the visual-critic flagged 11:15's hour hand as "drifted toward 12"; full-res zoom + `handAngles(11,15)={hour:337.5}` confirmed it is exactly 7.5° past 11 (a quarter of the gap), clearly left of vertical — geometrically correct and unambiguous ("hasn't reached 12 → still 11"). **Left unchanged: NEVER fudge correct clock geometry to satisfy a small-render perception — that would teach a wrong clock.** (Restates the #1 lesson: judge analog legibility from a high-res crop, not the thumbnail.)
**DoD:** verify 27 rounds (3 rows) + visual-qa 54/54 + probe/local-test (quarter id + #1 + #2 regression) + variety (all 3 reshuffle) + preflight (153 coords, slug unique) + personal Read 360/768/1024 (quarter-past minute-on-3, quarter-to minute-on-9, the hard 2:45 + 11:15 full-res confirmed) + visual-critic CLEAN + git-diff 0 new tracked / 0 protected-core. EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #4 "Read to the Five-Minute" (2.MD.C.7; granularity:"five" — all :00..:55 in 5-min steps; `spoken()` falls back to bare "h:mm" for non-quarter minutes → VERIFY the win-note reads OK for e.g. 3:20, consider a "twenty past"/plain-digital tweak).**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 3: TIME-EXPANSION activity #4 "Read to the Five-Minute" BUILT (2.MD.C.7) + a shared hour-hand legibility fix (#1-4)
Fourth time activity: `clock-digital.read-five-minute.2-md-c-7`, granularity `"five"`, slug `read-clock-to-the-five-minute`, Sprocket #76 reused. **Distinct from #3** (quarter marks :15/:45): #4 targets the NEW non-quarter 5-min values (:05/:10/:20/:25/:35/:40/:50/:55) — the child **counts by 5s** around the face; the load-bearing trap is **adjacent ±5 min** (3:20 with 3:15/3:25 cards, a couple crossing the hour like 4:55 vs 5:00). The engine already supported `"five"` (`GRAN.five=null` → `inScope` = `m%5===0`); `spoken()`'s bare "3:20" fallback reads fine in the win note. **Files:** 1 manifest row + ONE small copy tweak — added `hintFive` ("Count by fives around the clock — the long hand tells the minutes.") selected for `granularity==='five'` (the counting cue beats the generic `hintMin` for this skill). Zero git-tracked edits.
**Shared fix (applies to ALL of #1-4):** the visual-critic flagged the teal hour hand as nearly as LONG as the coral minute hand — the canonical "short hand = hour" cue was weak (separation rested only on color/thickness). Fixed in the shared `clockSVG`: hour hand shortened (`y2:28→30`, length 22→20 ≈ 55% of the minute hand) + minute hand lengthened (`y2:16→14`, length 34→36) + hour stroke 4.4→4.8. Now the short/long distinction is unmistakable at phone + desktop (re-critic VERDICT CLEAN; all four activities re-ran visual-qa GREEN). **Lesson: the "short hand = hour, long hand = minute" cue must be visually OBVIOUS (≈55% length ratio), not merely present — color+thickness alone don't carry it; the fix lives in the one shared clockSVG so every clock activity benefits.**
**DoD:** verify 36 rounds (4 rows) + visual-qa 54/54 ALL FOUR ids + probe/local-test (five id + #1/#2/#3 regression incl. the `_nudge` hint-selection: #1→hint, #2/#3→hintMin, #4→hintFive) + variety (all 4 reshuffle) + preflight 154 coords + personal Read 360/768/1024 (3:20 minute-on-4, 7:05 minute-on-1, 2:25 post-fix desktop) + visual-critic CLEAN (after the hour-hand fix) + git-diff 0 new tracked / 0 protected-core. EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #5 "Read to the Minute" (3.MD.A.1, Grade 3; granularity:"minute" — ANY minute; distractors = near-minute ±1..±3 traps; the hardest read; consider a minute-specific hint).**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 4: TIME-EXPANSION activity #5 "Read to the Minute" BUILT (3.MD.A.1) — the reading ladder is COMPLETE; + a minute-tick face for the shared clockSVG
Fifth time activity, the LAST rung of the reading ladder (hour→half→quarter→five→**minute**): `clock-digital.read-minute.3-md-a-1`, granularity `"minute"`, slug `read-clock-to-the-minute`, Sprocket #76 reused. **3.MD.A.1 was UNOWNED** (clean new Grade-3 code). **Distinct from #4** (5-min marks): #5 targets ANY non-multiple-of-5 minute (:06/:08/:17/:23/:28/:34/:41/:47/:52) — the child reads the EXACT minute, counting from the nearest 5-mark; distractors are **±2 and ±3 min** (12–18°, distinguishable WITH ticks, not the unfair ±1=6°).
**The one real design addition — a conditional MINUTE-TICK RING on the shared `clockSVG`:** the face had only 12 hour ticks, so a fair to-the-minute read was impossible. `clockSVG(h,m,opts)` now draws 48 fine minute ticks (the non-5 positions, r43.5→r46, stroke 0.7) when `opts.minuteTicks`; `render()` passes `{minuteTicks: granularity==='minute'}` → **#1-4 faces are untouched** (opts defaults off). Added `hintMinute` ("Count the little marks — each one is a minute.") selected for granularity==='minute'. The ring is clean (not cluttered) at phone 360 + desktop, confirmed by the critic + my Read.
**Build lessons:** (1) **a "to-the-minute" analog activity NEEDS minute tick marks** — without them the read is guesswork; the conditional ring is the right scope (only the activity that needs it pays the clutter cost). (2) **desktop clock size is FOLD-constrained, not free** — the clock is a healthy 230px (`min(58vw,230px)`) but LOOKS small against the 720px shell card; the critic's "enlarge it" advisory is non-blocking AND cannot be acted on (resolved @1024×900 = 890/896, 6px headroom → +30px clock would bust FITS). Kept 230px; never move the FITS gate for a cosmetic preference. (3) ±2-minute distractor is the hardest fair gap on a static face; ±1 (6°) would be unfair.
**DoD:** verify 45 rounds (5 rows) + visual-qa 54/54 + probe/local-test (minute id + #1-4 regression — the new `clockSVG` opts arg defaults to NO ticks for them; `_nudge` selection now hour→hint/five→hintFive/minute→hintMinute/else→hintMin) + variety (all 5 reshuffle) + preflight 155 + personal Read 360/768/1024 (3:23 zoom + 7:47 desktop + 2:34 phone, ticks countable + clean) + visual-critic CLEAN + git-diff 0 new tracked / 0 protected-core. EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **The reading-granularity ladder is COMPLETE (5 activities). NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #6 "Match the Clocks (Analog ↔ Digital)" — the first MATCH variant; needs a small render branch (show one clock + 3 digital, or one digital + 3 analog faces) + a `direction`/`match` config, more than a manifest row.**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 5: TIME-EXPANSION activity #6 "Match the Clocks" BUILT (2.MD.C.7) — the first MATCH variant; a direction render-branch (digital → analog)
Sixth time activity, the first NON-reading variant: `clock-digital.match-clocks.2-md-c-7`, `params.direction:"digital-to-analog"`, granularity `"five"` (mixed o'clock/half/quarter/5-min), slug `match-analog-and-digital-clocks`, Sprocket #76 reused. **The REVERSE direction** of #1-5: a big **DIGITAL** readout is the stimulus + **3 ANALOG-clock-face cards** are the choices; the child taps the clock whose hands show the time. Genuinely distinct (the child reads each ANALOG choice now, not one). Prompt "Which clock shows this time?" (`qMatch`); wrong → `hintMatch` "Find the clock whose hands show this time." Each round = target + same-hour-different-minute + different-hour (every face must be read).
**The engine stays DIRECTION-AGNOSTIC — the branch is pure RENDER:** `clock-digital-core` is unchanged (a round is still `{target, options}` of `{h,m}`; `isAnswer`/`oracle` compare times). `render()` reads `params.direction` (default `'analog-to-digital'`): for `digital-to-analog` it emits a `.cd-readout` digital stimulus + `.cd-choice.cd-clockcard` buttons each holding a `clockSVG`; `_makeTask`/`_nudge`/`_srMirror` are direction-aware; `verify` auto-covered it (the data shape is identical). **Lesson: when an activity flips the stimulus/choice representation, keep the cognition core direction-agnostic and branch ONLY the render — the verify gate, the derived oracle, and the data shape all carry over for free.**
**Build lessons:** (1) **analog-clock CHOICE cards pass the visual-qa not-sparse/not-tiny gate naturally** (they have svg children that fill the card) — unlike the digital-TEXT cards (#1-5) which are exempt. (2) The visual-critic's "clocks small with empty surround on desktop" advisory was acted on (the match activity has fold room — the digital readout is much shorter than the #1-5 stimulus clock): added a **`.cd-match` root modifier** (digital-to-analog only) widening the row `460px→560px` + clock cards `128px→165px`; FITS held (resolved @1024×900 = 877/896). Reading activities (#1-5) untouched. (3) the local-test was made **direction-aware** (assert `.cd-readout` + 3 `.cd-clockcard` for match; digital-text cards for reading) so one harness covers both directions.
**DoD:** verify 54 rounds (6 rows) + visual-qa 54/54 (analog cards pass not-sparse/tap) + probe/local-test (match id + #1-forward probe + #1-forward local-test + #5 regression) + variety (all 6 reshuffle) + preflight 156 + personal Read 360/768/1024 (digital readout stimulus + 3 legible analog cards, exactly one matches, enlarged desktop clocks) + visual-critic CLEAN (advisory acted on) + git-diff 0 new tracked / 0 protected-core. EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #7 — REASSESS: #7 as queued (digital→pick analog) is the SAME direction as #6 → make #7 a MIXED-direction match (each round randomly analog→digital OR digital→analog) OR fold #7 and jump to the OPERATION engines (#8 elapsed / #9 12↔24 convert / #10 add-subtract / #11 AM-PM). Flag to the operator at #7's plan.**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 6: TIME-EXPANSION activity #7 "Elapsed Time" BUILT (3.MD.A.1) — the first OPERATION; a NEW `clock-elapsed` engine
Seventh time activity, the first NON-matching skill: `clock-elapsed.what-time.3-md-a-1`, slug `elapsed-time-what-time-will-it-be`, Sprocket #76 reused (the cohesive time mascot — no new character). **Operator (2026-06-27) DROPPED the redundant reverse-#7** (same direction as #6) and chose to jump to Elapsed Time. A child reads a **START analog clock**, the prompt asks "What time will it be in N minutes?", and they tap the **END time** among 3 digital cards. **NEW engine `clock-elapsed`** (genuinely new cognition — time ARITHMETIC, not a `clock-digital` config). **3.MD.A.1 is multi-clause** — #5 owns the read-to-minute clause, #7 the elapsed-word-problem clause (the time-expansion explicitly allows code-dup across the breadth; §A.13.59 multi-clause precedent).
**Derived-not-stored:** a round stores `{start, deltaMin, options}`; the END is **COMPUTED** `endTime(start, delta)` with a 12-hour wrap (`total=((h%12)*60+m+delta) mod 720; endH=floor/60 %12 (0→12)`) — never stored. Distractors = plausible errors (added-to-hour, no-rollover). The verify mutates `deltaMin` to re-point the oracle (derived proof). Rollover cases proven: 3:45+30=4:15, 2:15+45=3:00, 10:50+20=11:10, 11:40+30=12:10.
**Build lessons:** (1) **the resolved-state fold caught a real cut-off the visual-qa missed** (the §A.13.62 lesson — visual-qa never clicks Check): the probe flagged resolved @1024×900 = 946>896. ROOT CAUSE = a `.ce-duration` line that **DUPLICATED the shell prompt** (both showed "What time will it be in N minutes?") — removing the redundant line (the shell prompt carries the duration; the start time is read from the clock) + a compact one-line win note ("Yes! 3:00 + 30 min = 3:30.") + the clock at 175px cleared it (890/896). **Lesson: don't restate the shell prompt inside the stage — it's redundant AND eats the fold.** (2) the desktop clock is FOLD-constrained at 175px (the resolved Check sits at 890/896 — 6px headroom; the visual-critic's enlarge-the-dial advisory can't be acted on without busting FITS; kept it — never move the gate). (3) re-implemented a compact `clockSVG` + `sprocketSVG` + `handAngles` in the new engine (0 lines to clock-digital — the time-expansion re-implement doctrine).
**DoD:** verify-clock-elapsed-core (endTime + rollover ground-truth + exactly-one-match + derived [delta-mutation] + ≥7) + visual-qa 54/54 + _elapsed-probe (resolved fold cleared) + local-test-clock-elapsed + preflight 87 manifests/157 coords (the §21.5 MANIFEST_FILES gate — `clock-elapsed-activities.json` registered) + variety reshuffle + clock-digital regression (verify + #1 + #6 intact) + personal Read 360/768/1024 (3:45+30→4:15, 5:10+40→5:50) + visual-critic CLEAN (independently verified all 9 rounds' arithmetic). The ONE tracked edit = `activities.ts` (new-engine registration). EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #8 "Time Addition & Subtraction" — extend `clock-elapsed` with a SUBTRACT mode ("what time was it N minutes ago?" — `endTime` already handles negative delta via the mod-720 wrap; a manifest row + small render/copy tweak, reusing the engine → minimal tracked edits). Then #9 12↔24 convert (NEW clock-convert), #10 AM/PM.**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 7: TIME-EXPANSION activity #8 "Time Addition & Subtraction" BUILT (3.MD.A.1) — the `clock-elapsed` SUBTRACT mode (mixed add + subtract)
Eighth time activity: `clock-elapsed.add-subtract.3-md-a-1`, slug `time-addition-and-subtraction`, Sprocket #76 reused. **Distinct from #7** (add-only): #8 is MIXED add + subtract — the new skill is **SUBTRACTION** ("what time was it N minutes ago?") plus reading the operation direction each round (4 add + 5 subtract, back-rollover in 12:10−20→11:50 and 1:05−10→12:55). **Reuses the `clock-elapsed` engine with NO core change** — `endTime(start, delta)` already handled NEGATIVE delta via its `((total%720)+720)%720` wrap (the mod-720 was written in #7 to be sign-safe; #8 just feeds negative `deltaMin`). 3.MD.A.1 (the add-AND-subtract clause; same multi-clause standard as #5/#7, different facet).
**Files — ZERO new git-tracked edits:** a 2nd row in the already-registered `clock-elapsed-activities.json` (deltaMin positive=add / negative=subtract) → `activities.ts` UNCHANGED since #7. The core was UNTOUCHED. Sign-aware copy in the (untracked) activity: `qBack`/`winBack`/`hintBack` added; `_makeTask` prompt + `_resolve` note + `_nudge` hint + `_srMirror` all branch on `deltaMin<0` (the `{n}` placeholder always gets `Math.abs`); the win note uses the typographic minus `−` (U+2212). #7 (all-positive deltas) is provably unaffected (regression-green).
**Build lessons:** (1) **a sign-safe engine pays off** — writing `endTime`'s wrap as `((total%720)+720)%720` in #7 (rather than a forward-only `total%720`) meant the entire subtraction activity (incl. back-past-12 rollover) needed ZERO core change — only manifest data + display copy. Design the cognition core to handle the general case (signed delta) even when the first activity only uses the positive half. (2) the `deltaMin` sign is the single source of truth for add-vs-subtract across prompt/win/hint/sr-mirror — one field drives all the copy branches. (3) the verify gate's per-round loop auto-covered the negative rounds (oracle = `endTime` which already handled them); only added 4 negative ground-truth `expectEnd` assertions for explicit proof.
**DoD:** verify 18 rounds (2 rows) + 4 negative ground-truth cases (3:30−30, 12:10−20 back-rollover, 1:05−10 back-rollover, 9:05−25) + visual-qa 54/54 + _elapsed-probe + local-test (--activity add-subtract) + #7 add-path regression (probe + local-test green) + preflight 87/158 + variety (both reshuffle) + personal Read 360/768/1024 (subtract r3 12:10−20→11:50 back-rollover "was it…ago?" + r1 + add r4) + visual-critic CLEAN (independently verified all 9 rounds incl. both rollovers + the "ago" prompt). EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #9 "12-Hour ↔ 24-Hour Conversion" — a NEW `clock-convert` engine (12h+am/pm ↔ 24h, an intl/European skill; align 2.MD.C.7). Then #10 AM/PM. 8 built → #9/#10 reach 10.**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 8: TIME-EXPANSION activity #9 "12-Hour ↔ 24-Hour Conversion" BUILT (2.MD.C.7) — a NEW digital-only `clock-convert` engine
Ninth time activity: `clock-convert.12-24.2-md-c-7`, slug `convert-12-hour-and-24-hour-time`, Sprocket #76 reused. **A NEW engine `clock-convert`** — genuinely new cognition (NOTATION conversion 12h↔24h, not arithmetic-on-a-clock); **digital-only — NO analog clock SVG** (it's a notation skill). MIXED both directions (5 `12to24` / 4 `24to12`), all PM times (13-23) where the +12 matters; the key distractor is the "forgot +12 / wrong-meridiem" error (3:00 PM → a "03:00" card; 20:00 → an "8:00 AM" card).
**Standards-honesty (operator-approved 2026-06-27):** 12↔24 conversion isn't a clean US-CCSS skill (24h time is European/intl). Per §20.8 I FLAGGED it via AskUserQuestion (3 options: build@2.MD.C.7 / do AM-PM first / skip). Operator chose **build, aligned 2.MD.C.7** — that standard explicitly owns "...using a.m. and p.m.", which the conversion is built on (extended to 24h notation for the platform's European K-3 audience, who use 24h time). A defensible reasonable-fit, NOT an invented code. The page-intro says "Builds on Common Core 2.MD.C.7 …, extended to 24-hour time." **Lesson: when a genuinely-useful-for-the-audience skill has no clean CCSS code, FLAG it (don't silently force-fit or invent) — the operator rules; here the a.m./p.m. clause of 2.MD.C.7 was the honest anchor.**
**Derived-not-stored:** a round stores `{dir, h24, m, options:[h24 ints]}`; the true time is `h24`; the answer is **the option whose value === h24** (`isAnswer = options[i]===round.h24`). The core RENDERS each value in the right notation per `dir` (the SAME options integers render as 24h "15:00" for 12to24, or 12h "3:00 PM" for 24to12). Verify mutates `h24` → oracle follows. **Lesson: store the format-agnostic value (h24) + a `dir` flag; render per-direction — one data shape serves both conversion directions, and the derived answer (option===h24) is direction-free.**
**Files — ONE git-tracked edit** (`activities.ts` registers `clock-convert-activities.json`, the §21.5 gate). New `clock-convert-core.js` (`to12`/`to24str`/`to12str`/`givenStr`/`optionStr`/derived oracle) + `clock-convert-activity.js` (digital readout + 3 text cards + Sprocket; dir-aware prompt/win note; re-impl `sprocketSVG`; NO clockSVG) + html + manifest. Digital-only → LOTS of fold room (no 900-tall risk). The 12h cards ("11:45 PM" ~8 chars ×3) fit at 320 (font 1.2rem, shrink to 1.02rem @≤380).
**DoD:** verify-clock-convert-core (notation ground-truth incl. noon/midnight + exactly-one-match + derived [h24-mutation] + ≥7) + visual-qa 54/54 (12h cards no overflow) + _convert-probe + local-test-clock-convert + preflight 88 manifests/159 coords (the §21.5 gate registered the engine) + variety reshuffle + clock-elapsed + clock-digital regression intact + personal Read 360/768/1024 (12→24 "3:00 PM"→15:00 + 24→12 "20:00"→8:00 PM) + visual-critic CLEAN (independently verified all 9 conversions + distractors). EN-only pilot. NO deploy. **NEXT (on "switch to plan mode + continue") = #10 (final) "A.M. / P.M." — cleanly 2.MD.C.7 ("using a.m. and p.m."); completes the commission (≥10 time activities).**

### Sprocket #76 — addendum 9: TIME-EXPANSION activity #10 "A.M. / P.M." BUILT (2.MD.C.7) — a NEW 2-choice `clock-ampm` engine — and the COMMISSION COMPLETES (10 activities)
Tenth + FINAL time activity: `clock-ampm.morning-or-night.2-md-c-7`, slug `am-or-pm-morning-or-night`, Sprocket #76 reused. **NEW 2-choice engine `clock-ampm`** — distinct from the 3-card pattern. **Cleanly 2.MD.C.7** ("...using a.m. and p.m." — no stretch, unlike #9). **Key design insight: a.m./p.m. CANNOT be read from a clock alone** (3:00 is ambiguous) → the load-bearing cue is an everyday ACTIVITY (breakfast→morning→a.m.; dinner→evening→p.m.). NO analog clock (a clock would mislead). The child reads "You eat breakfast. 7:00" + taps the SUN ("a.m.") or MOON ("p.m.") button. 9 unambiguous K-3 activities (4 a.m. / 5 p.m.); the 8:00/6:00/9:00 reuse across periods make the ACTIVITY load-bearing, not the number.
**Derived (content-label):** round stores `{activity, time, ampm}`; the 2 choices are the FIXED pair ['AM','PM']; `isAnswer(choice)=choice===round.ampm`. `ampm` is the pedagogical CONTENT (breakfast→AM, like a target word — NOT a redundant correctIndex); verify flips `ampm` → oracle flips. ONE tracked edit (`activities.ts`). Re-impl `sprocketSVG` + a code-drawn `sunSVG` (8 rays + disc) + `moonSVG` (crescent + stars) — NO emoji (controlled, locale-neutral, avoids the §sound-boxes recognizability/emoji-variance trap).
**Build lesson — the sparse-vs-fold tension on icon-buttons:** the 2 buttons have a SVG icon + a text label; the visual-qa not-sparse gate measures only the svg-union area, so a small icon next to text reads SPARSE ("tiny content in a big button"). Enlarging the icon fixes sparse but a TALLER button busts the 320×640 resolved fold. Resolution = the icon must be ≥~70px (sparse needs ≥0.45 width / ≥0.32 area at the 140px button) AND the fold is reclaimed by compacting the SCENE (activity/time fonts + root gap at ≤380), NOT by shrinking the icon. Landed at icon 78px (sparse 0.35 area / 0.55 width) + a compact small-screen scene (320 resolved 626/636). **Lesson: for an icon+label answer button, size the icon to satisfy not-sparse FIRST, then win back the fold from the surrounding scene/gaps — never shrink the icon to fit the fold (it re-fails sparse).**
**DoD:** verify-clock-ampm-core (ampm∈{AM,PM}, isAnswer correct + other-false, derived [ampm-flip], ≥7 distinct, ≥2 each AM/PM) + visual-qa 54/54 (the 2 sun/moon buttons pass not-sparse + tap≥44 after the icon-size/scene-compact fix) + _ampm-probe (resolved fold cleared 320→1366) + local-test-clock-ampm (2 buttons, sun+moon icons, wrong-no-advance, ampm win note) + preflight 89 manifests/160 coords (the §21.5 gate registered the engine) + variety reshuffle + clock-convert + clock-digital regression + personal Read 360/768/1024 (a.m. "wake up" + p.m. "go to sleep") + visual-critic CLEAN (all 9 unambiguous, sun/moon distinct). EN-only pilot. NO deploy.
**🏁 THE TIME-EXPANSION COMMISSION IS COMPLETE — 10 new time activities (all Sprocket #76, EN-only pilots, NOT deployed):** #1-5 reading ladder (hour→half→quarter→five→minute, `clock-digital`), #6 analog↔digital match (`clock-digital` reverse), #7 elapsed + #8 add/subtract (`clock-elapsed`), #9 12↔24 conversion (`clock-convert`), #10 a.m./p.m. (`clock-ampm`). The screenshot spec is fully covered. **NEXT is the operator's call (do NOT auto-start): (a) deploy the batch [one `git pull → cp → deploy.sh` chain — built + gated, not live], (b) 11-locale fan-out of the EN pilots [cheap — digital times locale-neutral; the prompts/copy localize], or (c) a fresh commission.**

### BUGFIX 2026-06-27 — "Add the Magic e" gave away the answer (gild removed; AUTHORIZED protected-core CSS touch)
Operator bug: `cvc-builder.build-magic-e.rf-1-3-silent-e` ("Add the Magic e — Long Vowel Words", RF.1.3, LIVE en) **showed the correct option already highlighted — no challenge.** Root cause = a single CSS rule in the PROTECTED `mini tools/cvc-builder-core.js` that GILDED the magic-e tile (`.cvc-wrap.cvc-prefill .cvc-letter[data-letter="e"]{gold gradient + glow}`). Every magic-e word ends in `e` and the distractors are `a/o/y/i`, so the gold tile was ALWAYS the answer — the child just tapped the gold one. **Fix (operator-AUTHORIZED protected-core touch, CSS-only, 0 logic): removed that rule.** Kept the `.cvc-prefill` class + the locked-slot dim (`.cvc-slot--filled{opacity:0.92}` — points at WHERE to add, not WHICH tile). Now all four palette tiles render identically; the child must find the magic e (the RF.1.3 skill restored). Cache-buster `cvc-builder-core.js?v=3→4`. Added a **no-gild regression guard** to `local-test-cvc-builder-magice.js` (the `e` tile's computed bg/color/shadow MUST equal a distractor tile's — locks the fix in). **Verified 3 ways:** programmatic no-gild guard PASS + personal Read (all 4 tiles identical) + visual-critic "GILD: CLEAN". verify-magice + blend/vowel-team/endings regressions + preflight all PASS (the shared core CSS edit didn't break siblings). EN-only; **NOT deployed (operator batched — joins the un-deployed batch with the 10 time activities).**
**Lesson (§A.13.60 challenge bar): a "scaffold" that visually marks the correct option DEFEATS the activity — never gild/highlight the answer tile; the child must actually choose. Add a computed-style-equality guard so it can't creep back.**
**⚠️ SURFACED (separate, pre-existing, NOT this fix): the whole cvc-builder family has a desktop-fold gate-fail** — `visual-qa-activity.js` flags CUT-OFF at the 900-px-tall desktop viewports (1024×900 / 1366×900; content ~997px) on magic-e AND the untouched build-cvc / build-blend siblings (these E7 builds predate the §A.13.62 visual-qa gate). It's marginal (content fits at 768×1000 and on taller windows / the auto-resizing iframe; the critic saw no cut-off at 768×1000) and family-wide, NOT caused by the paint-only gild removal. A separate fix (the html `@media(min-width:768px)` upscaling overflows a 900-tall window) — flag for an operator decision; do NOT bundle into the gild fix.

### FOLLOW-UP 2026-06-27 — cvc-builder family desktop+phone fold FIXED (html @media only; NO protected-core touch)
The magic-e gild fix surfaced a pre-existing family-wide fold: the cvc-builder activities ran taller than a 900-px window → §A.13.62 visual-qa CUT-OFF at 1024×900/1366×900 (build-cvc worst 1031>900) + build-cvc 674>640 @320. **Root cause (measured):** the stack is `shell-prompt(202px, protected) + chrome(~295) + subject-img + slots + palette + Check`; `.cvc-slot`/`.cvc-letter` are `aspect-ratio:1`, so their HEIGHT is driven by container-width÷N, and the **palette WRAPPED to 2 rows** for build-cvc's 7 tiles (130px) — the dominant content hog — while the v7.4 `@media(min-width:768px)` upscaled subject-img to 180px on top. **Fix — `mini tools/cvc-builder-activity.html` `@media` ONLY (editable inline `<style>`, the established `!important`-override pattern; `cvc-builder-core.js` UNTOUCHED by this task):** desktop — subject-img 180→clamp(64,9vmin,84), `.cvc-slots` max-width 360→240, `.cvc-palette` capped to ONE ROW via `grid-template-columns:repeat(auto-fit,minmax(46px,56px))!important; justify-content:center; max-width:472px` (7 tiles fit one row ≈ 56px tall, was 130 wrapped), gap→10; phone `@media(max-width:599px)` — subject-img→clamp(46,11vmin,64), slots max-width→208, gap→7 to clear the 640 floor. Dialed against visual-qa. **All 3 cvc-builder activities (build-cvc / build-blend / build-magic-e) now visual-qa GREEN at all 6 viewports;** magic-e gild fix + verify/local-tests all still PASS; the picture/slots/tiles stay legible+tappable (visual-critic FIT: CLEAN). EN-only; NOT deployed (joins the un-deployed batch).
**Lesson: `aspect-ratio:1` tiles in a width-driven grid balloon their HEIGHT at desktop, and a palette that wraps to 2 rows is the silent fold hog — cap the TILE size (`minmax(min,max)` not `1fr`) + center + a max-width that fits N-tiles in ONE row, rather than just scaling fonts. The ceiling is the gate's 900-tall desktop FITS check (shell prompt+chrome already eat ~500px).**
**⚠️ SURFACED (separate follow-up, NOT fixed): the word-builder / `syllable-builder-activity.html` family ALSO folds — WORSE** (`syllable-builder.build-vowel-teams.rf-1-3` 1131>900 @1024, 1055>1000 @768; `build-endings.rf-1-3-f` 1021>900 @1024; + the es/it/pt/fr/fi syllable + de/nl/sv/da/no sound-chunk activities share that html). Different engine + html → a separate same-shape fix (same levers: cap tiles to one row + trim subject/gaps in `syllable-builder-activity.html`). Awaiting an operator decision.

### FOLLOW-UP 2 2026-06-27 — word-builder / syllable-builder family desktop+phone fold FIXED (html @media only; covers all 13 activities)
The cvc-builder fold fix surfaced that the word-builder family (`mini tools/syllable-builder-activity.html`, engine `word-builder-core.js`, protected) folded WORSE (build-vowel-teams 1131>900 @1024, 1055>1000 even @768). **Root causes (measured):** (1) `.wb-palette{flex-wrap:wrap}` with wide multi-char chunk tiles (`.wb-tile min-width 128` @desktop) → wrapped to 2 rows (the hog); (2) `.wb-subject-text` (the word-only endings subject) font was `clamp(40,10vmin,72)` = **72px** → long bases (jumping/painted) wrapped → +56px on those rounds; (3) the **verbose-locale prompts** (es/de/it/sv wrap to 3-4 lines in the protected shell prompt block → ~55px more chrome above the content). **Fix — `syllable-builder-activity.html` `@media` ONLY (no protected-core touch; `word-builder-core.js` + `syllable-builder-activity.js` UNTOUCHED):** desktop — subject-img→clamp(52,6.5vmin,60), wb-subject-text→clamp(30,4.6vmin,42), wb-slot min-height→clamp(44,6vmin,52), wb-tile min-width→clamp(48,6.5vmin,56)+min-height clamp(44,6vmin,48)+small font+padding (so up to ~8 chunk tiles stay ONE row in the 520px palette, unclipped), gap→8; phone block trimmed (subject-img + slot/tile min-height + subject-text + gap) for the 640 floor. Dialed against visual-qa. **All 13 activities GREEN at all 6 viewports** — EN vowel-teams/endings/build-words + es/fi/pt/fr/it/de/nl/sv/no/da syllable+sound-chunk; word-builder verify/local-tests (vowelteam+endings) intact; cvc-builder unaffected (different html); chunk tiles unclipped (visual-critic CLEAN). Batched-not-deployed.
**Lessons (add to the cvc fold lesson): (1) a flex-wrap palette of WIDE multi-char tiles is the same 2-row fold hog — cap tile min-width small + a max-width that fits the max tile count in ONE row. (2) a word-only TEXT subject at a huge font (72px) wraps long words — cap it in the @media. (3) VERBOSE-LOCALE prompts wrap to 3-4 lines in the protected shell prompt block (~55px more chrome) — the content must be trimmed enough to fit a 900-tall window UNDER the longest locale prompt (drive the dial-in off a verbose locale like es/de, not just EN).**
**Both phonics families (cvc-builder + word-builder/syllable-builder) are now §A.13.62-fold-clean across all viewports + locales.**

## 2026-06-27 — BATCH FOLD FIX (5 activities + 1 geometry) + an AUTHORIZED shell trim

Operator bug report: 5 LIVE activities "cut off at the bottom, Check button not visible in some exercises," + one geometry bug. Root cause was TWO-layered:

**(1) Oversized manipulatives (per-activity, html @media fix — no protected-core touch).**
- **array** (`array-activity.html`; build-array + equal-groups): the `aspect-ratio:1` grid cells were sized by the container width ÷ cols → a 5-row array was ~875px of grid alone. Capped cells to fixed squares (`.arr-grid .arr-cell` width+height + `aspect-ratio:auto`, centred) so grid height tracks the ROW COUNT; also capped the hero prompt + compacted the repeated-addition strip (5 addends → one line). 320px Galaxy-Fold needed 15px cells (cells have no imgs → no TINY risk).
- **fractions grid-count** (`fractions-activity.html`): the partition figure is a min(82vw,360px) SQUARE `.frac-svg`; capped via `.frac-wrap .frac-svg` (HIGHER specificity needed — bare `.frac-svg` ties the core's runtime `!important` and the later/core wins, §A.13.47 rule 6) + capped the 4-line prompt. **The grid-count figure is a WIDE rectangle in a SQUARE viewBox → ~half the svg box is empty top/bottom → it read small + floating (critic SPARSE).** Fix: scope a BIGGER svg + a **negative `margin-block`** to grid-count ONLY via `:has(.lcs-activity-keypad)` (grid-count is the only keypad activity; partition siblings are tap-to-cut) — the negative margin absorbs the svg's own internal whitespace so the rectangle is prominent without pushing Check off. Partition siblings keep the smaller cap.
- **choice-board fraction-noncongruent** (`choice-board-activity.js` injectFractionCSS — activity layer): capped the 4 fraction tiles (fixed squares not aspect-of-column) + the anchor + scoped a prompt cap to fraction boards via `.lcs-app.activity:has(.cb-frac-board) .lcs-activity-prompt` (other 20 choice-board activities untouched).
- **ten-frame solve-the-story** + **number-bond story-problem**: the WORD PROBLEM rides the SHELL PROMPT (48px → 6-9 lines = 312-478px!). Capped the prompt (desktop) + the manipulative (ten-frame cells via `.tf-wrap .tf-cell`; bond via `.nb-wrap .nb-svg`). **Ten-frame cells CANNOT shrink much — the counter IMAGES scale with the cell and go <14px = TINY** (so cells aren't a phone fold lever; only the prompt/readout are). Bond numbers are SVG text (safe to shrink).

**(2) Systemic keypad-family floor (operator-AUTHORIZED `lcs-shell.css` trim).** EVERY keypad activity (incl. shipped ten-frame how-many 933, place-value 1002, write-numeral 1017) overshot the strict 1024×900/1366×900 gate by ~30-50px — chrome + answer-display + 6×2 keypad + Check + the universal breathing-room gaps. No manipulative shrink can clear it. **Operator chose "trim the shell."** Trimmed the DEAD outer frame `padding-block: 10vh` (=90px top+bottom at vh=900) → `clamp(16px,4vh,44px)` (the §A.13.62-gate render has no parent iframe so 10vh = full-window; the real desktop iframe is 66.67vh where 10vh is smaller) + the universal gap `clamp(16,3vh,28)` → `clamp(14,2.4vh,22)` + the mobile padding/gap. Recovered ~80px desktop / ~30px phone — these are DEAD margins (NOT the inter-section breathing-room gaps that fixed the v7.3 flush-children complaint), so zero flush-children risk. `lcs-shell.css?v=17`→`?v=18` bumped across all 92 htmls. Broad regression sample (choice-board state, match-pairs, cvc, magic-e, clock, place-value) confirmed the trim only REDUCES dead space — no regressions; it IMPROVED the whole keypad family (place-value 1002→926, ten-frame how-many now passes all 6).

**Geometry (F):** `FRACTION_FIGURES` corner-to-corner diagonals (`rect-halves-diag`, `sq-halves-diag`) drew to the SHARP corners of `rx=6` rounded rects → the line tip poked past the rounded outline. Inset the endpoints (rect `_frLn(13,31,87,69)`, sq `_frLn(15,15,85,85)`) + matching share-path apexes. `circle-halves-diag` unchanged (endpoints on the circle). Confirmed inside-the-shape by high-res render + critic.

**Outcome:** A/B/C GREEN all 6 viewports + visual-critic CLEAN; D/E GREEN at 768/1024/1366/360/412 + critic CLEAN — they fail ONLY 320×640 (Galaxy-Fold cover) where a multi-line word problem + manipulative + keypad physically exceeds 640 at the legibility floor (even with ZERO manipulative, story+keypad > 640); real phones scroll (the iframe auto-grows, Prong D.1 — no actual cut-off). All verify/local-test/preflight PASS (cores untouched). 0 protected-CORE lines (choice-board-core, ten-frame-core, etc. untouched; only the activity-layer choice-board-activity.js + the authorized lcs-shell.css). Batched — NOT deployed.

**New lessons:** (1) a manipulative built from IMAGES (ten-frame counters) can't be shrunk for fold — the imgs hit the 14px TINY floor; only text/figure-line manipulatives shrink freely (array dots, fraction svg lines, bond numbers). (2) A WIDE figure in a SQUARE viewBox reads small+sparse — use a negative `margin-block` to pull in the empty bands (scope it to the right activity via `:has()`). (3) The shell's MOBILE prompt rule (`.lcs-app.activity .lcs-activity-prompt{...!important}`, 0,2,0) shadows a wrapper's `.lcs-activity-prompt{...!important}` (0,1,0) — phone prompt caps need ≥0,2,0 specificity (desktop works because the shell's desktop prompt rule has NO !important). (4) A story-in-the-prompt is the tallest verbose case — cap the prompt + drive the dial-in off the longest-story locale (de). (5) A word-problem + keypad is unfittable on a 320px phone — that's a real wall, document it (real phones scroll).

## 2026-06-27/28 — FULL-LIBRARY keypad/manipulative fold SWEEP (operator: "sweep those too")

After the 5-activity batch + shell trim, ran the §A.13.62 gate across ALL 160 activity coordinates (one-time driver). **13 failers** (147 already green). Fixed every desktop + 360/412 failure with the proven html-`@media` pattern (0 protected-core; only the 5 wrapper htmls — place-value / match-pairs / choice-board / sort-bins / ten-frame). **8 now fully green (all 6 viewports):** choice-board which-more + flat-solid, match-pairs make-the-number + addition-and-subtraction-pairs + find-the-missing-number, place-value tens-and-ones + expanded-form, sort-bins defining-attributes. **5 are green at desktop+360/412 with a documented 320×640 (Galaxy-Fold cover) wall:** match-pairs three-ways (6 number-word cards), ten-frame write-numeral (20-counter density) + solve-the-story (word problem), number-bond story-problem (word problem), sort-bins sort-by-sides (multi-bin sort). All 5 walls are physical (content + keypad/manipulative > 640 at the legibility floor); real phones scroll (growing iframe).

**Fixes by engine (all html @media, cores untouched):**
- **place-value** (PROTECTED core): desktop prompt cap + `.pv-wrap .pv-tray--*` height cap (0,2,0 beats core runtime). The 768-1023 tray cap didn't cover ≥1024 → tall base trays. Fixed tens-and-ones (926) + expanded-form (1002).
- **match-pairs** (PROTECTED core): desktop prompt cap (fixed find-missing 969) + phone prompt cap (0,2,0 to beat the shell mobile rule) + a targeted shrink of ONLY the tall mp-word/mp-expanded cards (0,4,0; spelled-out number words wrapped to 169px). Generic phone card rules BACKFIRED on three-ways' heterogeneous cards (grew them) → reverted; the word-card-only shrink is safe.
- **choice-board** (PROTECTED core): desktop prompt cap + subject-img trim (fixed flat-solid 970) + `:has(.cb-group)` group-tile enlarge (fixed which-more — its 76px uniform tiles shrank a 9-item group to 10px TINY images). All 23 choice-board re-verified.
- **sort-bins**: phone prompt cap (0,2,0) + pile/drop-zone min-height trim. defining-attributes fixed; sort-by-sides 320-wall.
- **ten-frame** (PROTECTED core): phone `.tf-double .tf-cell` floor 28→32 (counter ≥14px, fixed write-numeral 360 TINY). write-numeral DESKTOP counters are FOLD-BOUND at ~40px (24px counters, above the 14px floor): chrome+prompt+keypad+Check ≈ 828px fixed cost leaves only ~80px of 2-row frame height at vh=900; bigger cells fold or overflow→wrap→fold. A visual-critic flags them as small, but enlarging needs a protected-shell keypad change — left at the gate-passing physical max + flagged.

**New lessons:**
1. **Always keep `</style>` when replacing an inline `<style>` block** — dropped it 3× this sweep; the swallowed `<script>` tags → ".lcs-app never mounts" → gate ERROR "Waiting failed 15000ms" (looks like a render bug, is actually a broken html). Re-read the file tail after such edits.
2. **`.tf-double` is ON the `.tf-wrap` element (compound), not a descendant** — `.tf-wrap .tf-double .tf-cell` (descendant) matches nothing; use `.tf-wrap.tf-double .tf-cell` (compound, 0,3,0).
3. **The shell's MOBILE prompt rule has `!important` + 0,2,0** — a phone prompt cap must be `.lcs-app.activity .lcs-activity-prompt` (≥0,2,0) to bite (desktop works at 0,1,0 because the shell's desktop prompt rule has no `!important`).
4. **Generic phone card rules backfire on heterogeneous-card activities** (three-ways) — scope shrink rules to the specific tall card type (mp-word/mp-expanded), not all `.mp-card`.
5. **`:has()` is the clean shared-html scoper** — `:has(.cb-group)` (group tiles vs digit tiles), `:has(.lcs-activity-keypad)` (keypad coord vs tap-to-cut sibling) — distinguishes coords that share one wrapper html without a JS marker class.
6. **A keypad activity's manipulative is fold-bound by the keypad** — the ~828px chrome+keypad+Check fixed cost at vh=900 caps the manipulative height; a content-heavy manipulative (20 counters) can't be both big AND fit. The measured gate (not-tiny ≥14) is the bar; a critic's "comfortably large" can exceed physics → document, don't force.

0 protected-core lines (place-value-core / match-pairs-core / choice-board-core / ten-frame-core / cvc-builder-core untouched; only the wrapper htmls). Batched — NOT deployed.

## 2026-06-28 — Bundle Bot: make the "ones" controllable (one-by-one + tap-to-remove)

Operator bug: "It is not possible to control the ones — it neither increases one by one nor is it possible to delete added ones." Root cause: the "Drop ones" feeder added a FORCED cycling clump (`Core.nextClump` → clumpSizes[_feedIdx++%len] = 1,2,3,1…) so the child couldn't add exactly one, and there was NO remove (only tapping a whole ten-bar `unbundle`s → +10). Building a target needing exact loose ones (23 → 3 loose) was uncontrollable.

**Fix (operator-chosen: one-by-one + tap-to-remove):**
- `bundle-machine-core.js` (NON-protected game core): added `removeOne(s)` (s.ones>0 → s.ones--, floors at 0; can never create a bundle → no anti-cheat impact) + exported it. `feed`/`pullLever`/`unbundle`/`isSolved`/SOLVERS unchanged.
- `bundle-bot-activity.js`: feeder → one-per-tap (`_feed` calls `Core.feed(cstate,1)`; label "Drop a one" + single pip; dropped the cycling clump). Each loose `.bb-cube` is now a `<button>` → `_removeOne()` (which checks `isSolved`→`_win` because removing an over-shoot can land the target, e.g. 24→23). Cubes enlarged to clamp(22,5vw,28) for tappability + a hover/focus coral **×** badge (visible "removable" affordance) + active/hover brightness. Instruction updated ("Drop ones one at a time (tap a one to take it back)…").
- `bundle-bot-activity.html`: `bundle-machine-core.js?v=1→2`, `bundle-bot-activity.js?v=2→3`.
- `local-test-bundle-bot.js`: added one-per-tap (feeder click → +1 exactly), tap-to-remove (tap cube → −1), and remove-to-solve (overshoot 24 → tap → 23 solves) assertions.

**The clump-feed was NOT a gate-enforced anti-cheat** — `verify-bundle-machine-core.js` never checks `feedIsClumpNotBlindTap` (it's in `facts()` only), and its own solver uses `feed(s,1)`. The core cognition (count→judge "this is ten"→pull lever; lever refuses <10; no auto-bundle) is untouched → all gates pass.

**Verified:** verify-bundle-machine-core PASS (anti-cheats intact) · local-test-bundle-bot PASS (incl. the 3 new assertions) · visual-qa 54/54 (9 rounds × 6 viewports) · preflight PASS · personal Read 768+360 (Drop-a-one adds one, cubes tappable with × badge, lever bundles/refuses, Check visible) · visual-critic CLEAN. 0 protected-core. Batched — NOT deployed.

**Lessons:** (1) a "manipulative item" tap target (≤12 tray cubes) can't meet the 44px answer-card standard — the visual-qa gate correctly exempts non-card content; enlarge to a practical ~22-28px + add a visible affordance (hover/focus ×) rather than chase 44px. (2) `removeOne` must re-check `isSolved`→win (removing an over-shoot can complete the target). (3) `filter: … scale()` is invalid — `scale` is a transform function, not a filter; use `filter:brightness()` for press feedback when a transform slot is already taken (the cube jitter uses transform:translate).

## 2026-06-28 — Make Thirds: a dotted cut-line drawn OUTSIDE the rectangle

Operator bug (screenshot): on the RECTANGLE round of "Make Thirds" (`fractions.partition-thirds.2-g-a-3`) one dashed ghost cut-line sat above the top edge — outside the shape. Root cause in `fractions-core.js _lines` `n===3 && cut==='h'`: the rect box is y 28..72 (h=44); the distractor was `_lineH(box, h1-16)` = 26.67 = **1.33u above the top (28)**. A parallel horizontal distractor CAN'T fit inside a 44u-tall rect ≥14u from BOTH correct thirds-lines (42.67/57.33) — the verify gate's tap-separation rule makes it impossible → it fell outside.

**Fix:** the rect-h-thirds distractor is now an ORTHOGONAL vertical centre cut (`_lineV(box, midX)`, x=50 y28..72 — fully inside). It passes the gate (foil: a horizontal+vertical cross = 4 unequal pieces ratio ~2.0 ≥1.12; separation: orthogonal pair is exempt; correct set untouched → 3 equal strips) and is a plausible "wrong cut" for horizontal thirds. Only this one round used rect+h; the other 6 thirds rounds (circle/triangle/hexagon/square-v/diamond/ellipse) were already inside.

**Gate hardened:** `verify-fractions-core.js` now asserts IN-BOUNDS — every candidate cut-line's interior samples (t∈{0.25,0.5,0.75}) must be `pointInShape`. The gate previously checked equal-area + foil + tap-separation but NOT in-bounds, which is why a line-outside-the-shape shipped. The new check would have caught it (the old line's midpoint (50,26.67) fails rect y≥28) and makes the class a HARD gate failure forever.

`fractions-core.js?v=7→8`. Verified: verify-fractions-core PASS (15 rounds, incl. the new in-bounds check) · verify-fractions-gridcount PASS · visual-qa partition-thirds + partition-equal-shares GREEN · preflight PASS · personal Read of the forced rect round (2 horizontal + 1 vertical dashed, all inside) · visual-critic CLEAN. 0 protected-core (fractions-core is non-protected). Batched — NOT deployed.

**Lesson:** a content/geometry gate that proves equal-area + foil + tap-separation can still miss "the line is drawn outside the figure" — add an explicit in-bounds (point-in-shape) assertion. And a too-short shape can't host a parallel distractor with adequate tap-separation → use an orthogonal foil.

## 2026-06-28 — Mama's Roll Call: crude trace numerals → smooth professional print forms

Operator: the tracing numerals "are not professional / not traceable." Root cause: `numeral-trace-core.js GLYPHS` were coarse ordered-checkpoint POLYLINES rendered RAW as SVG `<polyline>` (`mamas-roll-call-activity.js` sign stage) — 0 was a 6-point hexagon, 8 a 9-point diamond-stack knot, 3/5/6/9 angular zigzags. The same array doubled as the trace-validation checkpoints.

**Fix (two parts, non-protected numeral-trace files):**
1. Re-authored `GLYPHS` 0-9 to professional K-print numeral forms — ordered `{x,y}` points lying ON the smooth numeral curve, correct stroke order/direction (0 oval CCW-from-top, 4 two-stroke, 8 a true figure-eight crossing centre, 6/9 closed loops). Structure preserved (array-of-strokes-of-`{x,y}`, ≥2 pts/stroke, 4=2 strokes) so the gate + solvers are unaffected.
2. Render each stroke as a Catmull-Rom→Bézier SPLINE (`<path d>`) that PASSES THROUGH the checkpoints, instead of the raw polyline. Smooth professional guide; the same points stay the ordered trace checkpoints (validation/TOL=20 unchanged). Kept the dashed-guide/solid-formed styling + coral start-dot + pointer capture.

`numeral-trace-core.js?v=1→2`, `mamas-roll-call-activity.js?v=3→4`.

**Why it's gate-safe:** `verify-numeral-trace-core.js` pins GLYPHS[d]=array-of-arrays(len≥2) + 4=2 strokes + the solvers (`idealPath`=the checkpoints, `traceScore` strict-order, TOL=20). Re-authored checkpoints pass by construction; a real rough trace passes via TOL=20.

Verified: verify-numeral-trace-core PASS · local-test-mamas-roll-call PASS · visual-qa 54/54 · preflight PASS · before/after glyph sheets + LIVE sign-stage render (8 = a smooth figure-eight with dashed trace-guide + start dot) personally Read · visual-critic CLEAN (all 0-9 recognizable, smooth, traceable). 0 protected-core (numeral-trace-core non-protected). Batched — NOT deployed.

**Lesson:** when ordered checkpoints double as BOTH the trace-validation path AND the visible guide, render them as a spline-through-the-points (Catmull-Rom interpolates → the smooth curve still hits every checkpoint) — a raw polyline of sparse checkpoints reads as a crude polygon. Keep the data structure the gate/solvers expect; change positions + render, not shape. Per-locale 1/4/7/9 variants remain a fast-follow.
