## 2026-06-28 — Mama's Roll Call: numerals look right but WON'T TRACE (follow-up to the glyph fix)

Operator: "numbers look much better but I still can't trace them." Root cause was the trace-ACCEPTANCE, not the glyphs. `numeral-trace-core.js traceScore` advanced the checkpoint index by **≤1 per pointer sample** (point-to-POINT, TOL=20). The prior glyph rewrite raised each digit to 11-13 checkpoints, so a real finger/mouse drag — which emits FEWER pointer samples than there are checkpoints, or skips a checkpoint between two samples — could never advance through all of them → always `'off-path'` → `_formStroke` silently rejected. (`idealPath` passed only because it feeds one exact-on-checkpoint point per checkpoint.)

**Fix:** rewrote `traceScore` to point-to-SEGMENT — a checkpoint counts if ANY drawn segment passes within TOL of it, and one segment may cover several checkpoints (inner `while`), so a coarse/fast drag completes. Added a `segDist(a,b,p)` helper. Order/direction stay strict (forward-only `cp`; per-segment "near the NEXT needed checkpoint"); the origin-garbage scribble still fails (≥30u away). `numeral-trace-core.js?v=2→3` (activity js unchanged — it already passes the path).

**Gate hardened (`verify-numeral-trace-core.js` #11):** for every digit/stroke, build a coarse ON-CURVE path with FEWER points than checkpoints (sample the same Catmull-Rom curve at ~⌈checkpoints·0.6⌉ pts) and assert `traceScore===true`, plus an off-shape (+45u) path asserts `false`. The OLD point-to-point code FAILS this; the new passes — locking "real drags trace."

**Proven:** verify-core PASS (incl #11, all 10 digits' coarse traces accepted) · local-test PASS · visual-qa 54/54 · preflight PASS · LIVE real-`PointerEvent` coarse drag (8 samples on an 11-checkpoint digit) advanced `strokesDone 0→1`; full count→trace→seal end-to-end SEALED + solved; sealed render personally Read. 0 protected-core (numeral-trace-core non-protected). Batched — NOT deployed.

**Lessons:** (1) a trace/path-following validator must use point-to-SEGMENT (cover-along-the-drawn-line), never point-to-point-per-sample — pointer-sample count is variable + usually far below the checkpoint count; a point-to-point checker silently breaks the moment the guide has more checkpoints than the drag has samples. (2) When proving a pointer-drag interaction in puppeteer, `page.mouse` may not fire the page's `pointer*` listeners reliably in headless — dispatch real `PointerEvent`s in-page (that's what a real user generates) for a faithful test. (3) A "looks fixed" report can hide a still-broken INTERACTION — always prove the actual gesture, not just the render.

## 2026-06-28 — Mama's Roll Call trace: thorough re-analysis vs the WORKING snippy (alphabet trace)

Operator: trace STILL fails; compare to Snippy's Letter Trace (`snippy.letter-formation.l-k-1-a`) which WORKS (terrible letters but traceable). Findings from exhaustive A/B diagnostics:
- **My prior "fixes" addressed the wrong layer.** Snippy's `traceScore` is the IDENTICAL point-to-point algorithm and works → point-to-point vs point-to-segment was a red herring. Snippy glyphs are SPARSE (2-5 checkpoints/stroke); mama's are dense (8-13) — I hypothesized that made validation too strict.
- **But the hypothesis was WRONG too.** Instrumented real-input A/B (CDP mouse + CDP touch, realistic ±14-16 wobble path, correct count): mama's trace `_traceDown` fires, `_formStroke` receives the path, `traceScore` returns TRUE, and the round SEALS — on BOTH mouse and touch, even digit 8 (13 checkpoints). The trace genuinely WORKS in headless. The only "failures" in my probes were count artifacts (over-sending ducks → commit-mismatch → state reset → strokesDone back to 0), NOT trace failures.
- **The ONE real code difference from working snippy:** snippy calls `ev.preventDefault()` on pointerdown; mama did not. Real browsers can hijack a pointerdown-drag over an element (native selection / image-drag / touch-callout / scroll) — which headless does not exercise — so this is the most likely real-device cause.

**Applied (snippy-parity input robustness, the evidence-based difference):** `ev.preventDefault()` in `_traceDown` + `_traceMove` (guarded by `e.cancelable`); `-webkit-user-select:none;user-select:none;-webkit-touch-callout:none` on `.rc-glyph`. `mamas-roll-call-activity.js?v=4→5`. Verified: mouse seal + touch seal still true; verify-core + local-test + visual-qa + preflight all PASS; render unchanged.

**HONEST STATUS:** I could NOT reproduce the operator's failure in any automated test (mouse/touch/wobble/seal all pass on the current code). The preventDefault parity is correct + zero-risk but UNCONFIRMED as the cause. Need the operator's environment (preview vs live — NB everything is batched-NOT-deployed, so testing the live site = OLD code; device/touch-vs-mouse; exact symptom) to pin a device-specific cause. Snippy's terrible-letters fix remains deferred. Batched — NOT deployed.

**Lesson:** when automated tests pass but the operator reports failure, the bug is likely in the un-tested layer (real-browser input hijack, device, or STALENESS/deploy state) — gather reproduction details instead of guessing a 4th code change. Test probes that drive count programmatically can mask trace success behind commit-mismatch resets — always isolate the trace from the commit when diagnosing.

## 2026-06-28 — Mama's Roll Call trace: REAL CAUSE FOUND = no live-ink feedback (resolved)
Operator clues that cracked it: testing LOCAL PREVIEW (so my fixes ARE present) + MOUSE + symptom "**nothing draws at all**." → Not validation (the trace validated + SEALED in every headless test, mouse+touch). The bug: `_traceMove` only accumulated points and NEVER painted — so dragging showed NO ink → "tracing doesn't work." The working snippy paints the current stroke on every pointermove (`_paintCur`); Mama's lacked it.
FIX: added `_paintCur(svg)` to Mama's (append/replace one coral `.rc-curink` path of the in-drag points; no full re-render so pointer capture survives) + call it from `_traceMove`. Kept the snippy-parity guards added this session (`ev.preventDefault()` in `_traceDown`/`_traceMove`, `user-select/touch-callout:none` on `.rc-glyph`). `mamas-roll-call-activity.js?v=5`. numeral-trace-core.js NOT changed this session (the approved plan's sparse-checkpoint/TOL/gate-#11 changes were correctly NOT applied — the diagnostic proved validation was never the problem).
PROVEN: mid-drag a coral ink trail renders along the guide (screenshot `_live-ink-middrag.png`, personally Read — the "6" traces in coral) + the stroke still seals (mouse digit-8 + digit-6); verify-core + local-test + visual-qa + preflight all PASS; render unchanged. 0 protected-core. Batched — NOT deployed.
**Meta-lesson (3 wrong diagnoses before this): when "tests pass but operator fails," STOP guessing code and get the operator's exact symptom + environment FIRST. "Nothing draws" = a FEEDBACK/render bug, not a validation/logic bug. My headless seal-tests masked it because they never needed to SEE ink. The single most useful action was the AskUserQuestion (where/device/symptom), which pointed straight at the missing live-paint.**

## 2026-06-28 — Mim's Glow Workshop (shapeforge): "can't fill any shape" = a var-closure-in-loop bug (FIXED)
Operator: shapes place/rotate but you can't fill ANY shape completely (local preview = on-disk). The core logic (legalAnchors/isComplete/solve/_checkComplete/_forged) was all CORRECT — empirical UI-path probe confirmed every round IS solvable via the offered anchors+rotate (so NOT a reachability/oracle gap). REAL CAUSE (reproduced via the DOM): the anchor-dot click handler closed over the loop `var o` in `for (var o…){ if(o!==this.orient)continue; legalAnchors(...).forEach(a=>{ dot.onclick=()=>_placeAt(o,a.dr,a.dc) }) }`. By click time the loop had exited so `o === orientCount` (an INVALID orient index) → `_placeAt(orientCount,…)` → `cellsOf` reads `orients[invalid]` → the placement covers nothing/throws → `isComplete` never true → "can't fill anything." Hit EVERY piece on EVERY round (matches "all shapes"). FIX: capture `var oFix = o;` per-dot inside the forEach + use `oFix` in the click/keydown handlers (and the cells/legalAnchors call). `shapeforge-activity.js?v=2→3`; shapeforge-core UNCHANGED. PROVEN: real DOM dot click now places orient 0 → onramp-rhombus SOLVES; all 11 rounds complete via the real place flow; verify-shapeforge-core + local-test + visual-qa + preflight PASS; solved render personally Read ("You forged it!" hexagon flares). 0 protected-core. Batched — NOT deployed.
LESSON: the classic `var`-in-loop closure (handlers capturing a loop var that's mutated past valid range). The SOLVABILITY GATE tested the `solve` oracle, not the UI's dot-click → it stayed green while every click was broken. ALWAYS exercise the real DOM interaction (click the actual dot, assert the resulting state), not just the model/oracle — same lesson as the trace saga. The empirical UI-path probe + a real dot-click test caught it in one pass this time.

## 2026-06-28 — Winter Piles (winter-piles.draw-partition.2-oa-c-4): "grid corners almost invisible" (FIXED)
Operator (screenshot): the acorn-grid panel's four rounded corners were almost invisible — they melted into the cream card. ROOT CAUSE: `.wp-field` is an SVG with `background:#5A4632` (dark brown) + CSS `border-radius:18px`. The CSS border-radius CLIPS the SVG's brown background at each corner to transparent → the corner shows the cream `.wp-root` card (`#FBF3E4→#F1E5CC`) behind it. Straight edges kept a visible 4-6px brown frame, but the corners had only `inset 0 0 0 4px rgba(0,0,0,.12)` (12% black) defining them = invisible cream-on-cream. The pile-band rect's oversized `rx:26` (vs the 18px frame, inset only 4-6px) widened the dead zone. FIX (2 edits, `winter-piles-activity.js` only): (1) `.wp-field` box-shadow → `0 0 0 3px #5A4632, inset 0 0 0 4px rgba(0,0,0,.18), 0 4px 10px rgba(90,70,50,.18)` — an OUTER brown ring that follows border-radius, so all 4 corners get a continuous visible edge OUTSIDE the clipped box; (2) band rect `rx:26→14` so the band corners sit concentric with the 18px frame (uniform frame width). Logic untouched. `winter-piles-activity.js?v=8→9`; draw-partition-core UNCHANGED. PROVEN: verify-draw-partition-core + local-test-winter-piles + preflight PASS; visual-qa GREEN 54/54 (6 viewports × 9 rounds); personally Read 360/768/1024 un-cut + multi-band — all 4 corners crisp brown frame vs the cream card; visual-critic CORNERS CLEAN / OVERALL CLEAN. 0 protected-core. Batched — NOT deployed.
LESSON: a rounded SVG/box whose CORNER fill is its `background` (CSS) will lose its corners when `border-radius` clips that background to transparent against a same-family background — an inset shadow + the bg alone vanish at the clipped corner. Define the corner with an OUTER ring/border (outer box-shadow follows border-radius), not an inset shadow. Also keep an inner colored rect's `rx` concentric with the frame radius so the frame width is uniform around the corner.

## 2026-06-28 — Tildy's Tailor Shop (tildy.measure-ruler.2-md-a-1): "can't slide the ruler" (FIXED)
Operator: the draggable ruler wouldn't slide. ROOT CAUSE (the classic drag bug — full re-render destroys the pointer-captured element mid-gesture): `tildy-activity.js` wires the drag on the ruler `<g>` (pointerdown → `rg.setPointerCapture()`; pointermove → `_setRulerZero`), but `_setRulerZero`'s drag branch called `_repaintRuler()` → full `this.render()` → `stage.innerHTML=''` detaches the captured `rg` → pointer capture released → no further pointermove reaches the ruler → it moves ONE tick then sticks. (Secondary: the pointermove closure's `sv` is also detached → `getBoundingClientRect()` → zeros → `toCm` garbage.) FIX (tildy-activity.js only): during drag, store `_rulerG`+`_rulerBaseZero` at render time and SLIDE the existing group via `transform: translate((rulerZero-base)*CMW, 0)` — NO re-render mid-drag (so capture survives); render only to settle on pointerup/pointercancel. The winter-piles "no full render during drag" pattern. `tildy-activity.js?v=5→6`; tildy-core UNCHANGED. PROVEN: verify-tildy-core + local-test-tildy + _tildy-probe (fold/overflow) + visual-qa GREEN 54/54 + preflight PASS; NEW `scripts/_tildy-drag-probe.js` dispatches a REAL mouse/pointer drag and asserts rulerZero TRACKS across ≥3 moves INCLUDING a vertical drift off the ruler band (the capture-survival discriminator) + clamps at 0 and the right edge — AND it was proven to FAIL on the buggy full-render build (track=[2,2,2,2,2,2,2,2], stuck after one tick) before passing on the fix (track=[2,1,1,0,0,0,0,0]). Personally Read 360/768/1024 + visual-critic RULER/STRIP + OVERALL CLEAN. 0 protected-core. Batched — NOT deployed. (Non-blocking polish note: ruler tick numerals are small at 360px — legible, pre-existing, the not-tiny gate exempts SVG sub-nodes; out of scope for this drag fix.)
LESSON: any DRAG/SLIDE gesture must NOT full-re-render mid-gesture — it detaches the pointer-captured element (capture lost → gesture dies) AND the move-closure's element refs (getBoundingClientRect → zeros). Mutate the existing element's attributes/transform during the drag; re-render only on release. And verify a drag with a REAL multi-move pointer sequence (with off-target drift to test capture), never just logic/visual gates — the existing _tildy-probe drove `_setRulerZero` directly and never exercised the gesture, so it never caught this. Same root lesson as the trace/shapeforge sagas: exercise the real DOM interaction.

## 2026-06-28 — Tuck Makes a Hundred (place-value-regroup.add-compose-hundred.2-nbt-b-7): hundred-flats overflow the column (FIXED)
Operator: when the hundreds are more than three, the units (hundred-flats) overflow the container. ROOT CAUSE (CSS — nowrap in a squeezed column): compose-a-hundred rounds reach 4-5 hundreds (r-450-60 → 4h+5t, +6t=11t → Make a hundred → 5 hundreds; r-410-90 → 5). The hundreds column rendered flats with `.pvr-col-hundreds .pvr-blocks{flex-wrap:nowrap}` (place-value-regroup-core.js injectCSS), all on ONE row — but compose-hundred runs in `fat-tens` mode (tens flex:1.9 for ≤13 rods) so the hundreds column is squeezed to ~90-113px; 4 flats (~121px) / 5 flats (~152px) at 28px each spilled OUTSIDE the column border. A horizontal IN-COLUMN spill that page-overflow + fold gates (visual-qa, _tuck-hundred-probe) never caught (it doesn't exceed page width) → it shipped "green." FIX (place-value-regroup-core.js only, 1 CSS rule): `.pvr-col-hundreds .pvr-blocks` nowrap→WRAP → flats wrap to centered rows inside the box (desktop 5=3+2, phone 5=4+1); fold unaffected (pre-bundle the ≤11-rod tens column drives mat height; post-bundle hundreds 2 rows ~59px < that). `place-value-regroup-core.js?v=16→17`; activity.js + protected place-value-core.js UNTOUCHED. PROVEN: verify-place-value-regroup-core + local-test (hundred/ten/subtract) + _tuck-hundred-probe + visual-qa GREEN 48/48 ×3 hundreds activities + preflight PASS; NEW `scripts/_tuck-hundred-containment-probe.js` asserts every `.pvr-flat` bbox ⊆ its `.pvr-col-hundreds` box (4 pre-bundle / 5 post-bundle / subtract 4-flat) across 320→1366 — PROVEN to FAIL on the buggy nowrap (spills 2-4 every viewport) and PASS on the wrap fix. Personally Read 5-flat post-bundle (360/768/1024) + 4-flat pre-bundle + visual-critic CONTAINMENT + OVERALL CLEAN. 0 protected-core. Batched — NOT deployed.
LESSON: a `flex-wrap:nowrap` row of FIXED-size blocks inside a FLEX-sized (variable-width) column WILL overflow once the block count exceeds the column width — wrap it. And add an in-CONTAINER containment assertion (child bbox ⊆ parent bbox) to the probe suite: page-overflow/fold gates do NOT catch a child spilling its OWN box while staying within the page. Same root lesson as the §A.13.62 desktop-cut-off gap — verify what's actually measured, and measure containment, not just page bounds.

## 2026-06-28 — Expanded "Sunny-Side Diner" into a Kindergarten language set (+2 activities, EN-only pilots, batched/not deployed)

Mechanic: a question with "___" ("___ would you like to eat?"), tap the wh-word that fits among the 6 FIXED chips (who/what/where/when/why/how). Derived from QWORD_TABLE (person→who, thing→what, place→where, time→when, reason→why, manner→how); the round stores {sentence, asks} (the answer-TYPE enum) never the wh-word literal; no-answer-leak (the wh-word not already in the sentence); chips are the fixed wh-set. 8 questions, all 6 answer-types present. **#2 Daisy the duck — "Daisy's Plate Stack" (L.K.1.c, regular plural nouns).** NEW char: Daisy, a yellow duck plating up orders. Mechanic: a request for more-than-one ("Wash all six ___."), tap the correctly-spelled plural among 3 chips. Derived from the /s/-or-/es/ RULE (pluralize: +es after s/x/z/ch/sh, else +s); the round stores {noun, sentence} never the plural; the 3 chips are DERIVED [plural, singular, wrongPlural] where wrongPlural uses the OTHER suffix (cup→"cupes", dish→"dishs") so the /s/-vs-/es/ choice is load-bearing; no-answer-leak. 9 nouns, 3 of the /es/ class (dish/box/peach). NEW clean-sibling `plural-noun-core.js` (DISTINCT from the existing `plural` engine, which is L.2.1.b IRREGULAR plurals). id `daisy-plate-stack.plurals.l-k-1-c`, slug `daisy-plate-stack`.

For Daisy the gate ALSO asserts pluralize(noun) matches a HARDCODED ground-truth plural for every noun incl. all /es/ cases (proves the rule), and the ground truth lives in the gate file (not the round) so the round stays answer-free. LENGTH-BALANCING (Daisy): singular is ALWAYS the shortest chip (base form, never the answer → shortest-bot 0%, clean); the +es plural is always the LONGEST, so the /es/-class share was capped at 3/9 (33%) to keep the longest-bot ≤ chance. Verify gates MEASURE oracle 100% + the keypad bots ≤ chance (Skip: copy-the-start 0% + fixed-guess 11.1%; Tally: copy-largest-addend 0% + copy-first-addend 0% + fixed-guess 11.1%) + the standard-specific invariants (Skip: ones-digit-unchanged for every round + all four deltas + start & result in [100,900]; Tally: every addend two-digit + ≥1 four-addend + ≥1 three-addend + total ≤140) + ≥9 rounds.

## 2026-06-28 — CORRECTION to the Story-Problems expansion (findability + collision fix)
The two activities above shipped UNFINDABLE in the preview index + colliding with existing content. Real cause (operator couldn't find them; I was wrong twice blaming browser cache):
- **page_title must LEAD with the character name.** Every sibling does (`Wren's Question Window…`); mine were "Ten More, Ten Less — …" and "Add the Baskets — …", so a search for the character names found nothing.
- **Collisions:** my 2.NBT.B.8 title "Ten More, Ten Less" duplicated the EXISTING 1.NBT.C.5 "Ten More, Ten Less — Mental Math With Two-Digit Numbers" (choice-board). Character names "Skip" (Skip's Word-Welding Yard, L.2.4.d) and "Squirrel" (Squirrel's Fair Winter Piles, 2.OA.C.4) were already taken; Hopper's Lily Hops owns the hop/skip-count theme.

Fix (operator chose "keep 2.NBT.B.8, differentiate"): re-charactered #1 **Skip the Kangaroo → Comet the Kangaroo** ("big leaps" not "hops"); files/id/tool/slug renamed skip-kangaroo→comet-kangaroo; id `comet-kangaroo.tens-hundreds.2-nbt-b-8`; **page_title "Comet's Big Leaps — 10 & 100 More or Less (Grade 2)"** + intro emphasizes 100-more/less + three-digit (the Grade-2 EXTENSION of the Grade-1 ±10 two-digit 1.NBT.C.5 — no longer a title dup). #2 stayed "Tally the Squirrel" (the name "Tally" is unused; only the label needed fixing) → **page_title "Tally's Acorn Baskets — Add Up to Four Two-Digit Numbers (Grade 2)"** (leads with Tally; distinct from Mim's Memory Baskets + Squirrel's Fair Winter Piles). `jump-tens-core.js`/`add-stack-core.js` byte-unchanged. All gates re-green; index confirmed (curl) to lead with "Comet's…"/"Tally's…", no skip-kangaroo orphan, only one "Ten More, Ten Less" label remains (the existing one).
LESSON (design-time gate for EVERY future activity, beyond the CCSS-code owned-check): (1) page_title.en MUST lead with the character name (findability in the preview index); (2) grep all existing `*-activities.json` page_titles for character-NAME and TITLE/skill collisions before locking the design — Skip/Squirrel/"Ten More Ten Less" were all already present and I missed them.

Operator: "analyze Rosa Raccoon's Rhyme Wagon (RF.K.2.a rhyme) and build two more with original design + context." The named is a PRE-READER LISTENING game (tap an image-library picture to HEAR it, match by sound). COLLISION CHECK up-front: RF.K.2 siblings .b (syllables)/.c (onset-rime)/.d (phonemes, Coco) + RF.K.3.a/.c are ALL owned → only RF.K.2.e free there; operator-confirmed pair = RF.K.2.e + RF.1.2.a (both unowned, collision-clear). DEPARTURE from the text-stub pilots: these MATCH the named — image-library pictures + tap-to-hear TTS (text-only would defeat phonemic awareness). Reuse the rhyme-shop/Coco helpers: `imgUrl = /image-library-webp/themes/<themeDir>/<noun>@2x.webp` (onerror-hide) + `LCSAudio.speak({type:'word'})` whole-word (NO isolated-phoneme audio); inert word caption; verified CVC pool encoded `{noun,themeDir,b,m,e}`.

**#1 Pepper the parrot — "Pepper's Sound Swap" (RF.K.2.e, phoneme substitution).** Hear the target picture-word, see the new LETTER cue ("Change the FIRST sound to H"), tap the picture of the new word (cat→hat). 3 picture cards, answerType:'state' tap-a-picture + shell Check. NEW clean-sibling `phoneme-swap-core.js`. id `pepper-sound-swap.substitute.rf-k-2-e`. ANTI-CHEAT (DERIVED phoneme match): round {target{b,m,e}, swap{position,phoneme,letter}, choices×3}; oracle = the choice whose {b,m,e} == target with [position]→phoneme; foils = a 2-of-3-phoneme NEAR-MISS (same-rime different-onset) + an unrelated. The letter is a DISPLAY cue (grapheme), not the answer. Gate MEASURES exactly-one-match + near-miss-present + target-not-in-choices + position/longest/shortest/fixed-guess ≤ chance + IMAGE-EXISTENCE (every `<themeDir>/<noun>@2x.webp` exists). 8 rounds (6 begin-swap _at/_an families, 2 last-swap cap/ham).

**#2 Stretch the giraffe — "Stretch the Giraffe" (RF.1.2.a, long vs short vowel).** Hear picture-words, tap the one with the long (or short) vowel sound. The ask ALTERNATES long/short. NEW clean-sibling `vowel-length-core.js`. id `stretch-giraffe.long-short-vowel.rf-1-2-a`. ANTI-CHEAT (DERIVED vowel tag): round {ask, choices×3 each vowel-tagged} exactly one matches ask; oracle = that choice. The long/short axis is the SKILL (like Booker's alphabetical axis), NOT a gated form-bot; mitigated by ASK-BALANCE (|#long−#short|≤1) so a prompt-ignoring reader can't beat reading the prompt. Gate MEASURES the 4 FORM bots (position/longest/shortest/fixed-guess) ≤ chance + ask-balance + image-existence. KEY DESIGN FIX: long-vowel words skew 4-letter / short skew 3-letter → a shortest-word bot could exploit it; balanced letter-lengths (long 3-letter answers bee/pie + short 4-letter foils fish/duck) so shortest-bot 37.5% ≤ chance. 8 rounds (4 long-ask / 4 short-ask).

Both: image-library pictures + TTS, ≥8 rounds + per-pass reshuffle, cards shuffle, diffuse no-shame feedback, EN-only. The listening cluster: RF.K.2.a rhyme (Rosa) → RF.K.2.e sound-swap (Pepper) → RF.1.2.a vowel length (Stretch).

The plural-possessive foil (dogs') is the SAME length as the answer (dog's) → correct never unique-longest; plain plural (dogs) is unique shortest = foil → longest/shortest bots 0%. Per-round `slot` (balanced 0/1/2) places the answer so canonical position varies (the Posy canonical-order trap fix); activity shuffles display too. 8 rounds (regular nouns, clean 's).

Both: text + SVG char stubs (no image-library / no audio dependency), ≥8 rounds + per-pass reshuffle, cards shuffle, diffuse no-shame feedback, EN-only, 0 protected-core/lcs-shell lines, strand "Language". The noun-grammar cluster: L.2.1.b irregular plurals (Doubling Pond) → L.1.1.c subject-verb agreement (Vera) → L.1.1.b possessive nouns (Hattie).
LESSONS APPLIED: collision-grep before locking codes; both page_title LEAD with the character name (curl-confirmed); fresh names Vera/Hattie verified absent. BINARY-SKILL RESOLUTION (Vera): when a concept is fundamentally two-form (50% floor) AND has an odd-one-out leak, recast it as a genuine 3-way with three real correct categories (am/is/are) — the cleanest fix, beats gating-around-the-binary. Both visual-qa 48/48 first pass (text-card layouts needed no fold rescue).

## 2026-06-28 — Fixed Snippy's letter glyphs + new full-alphabet trace (Penny), batched/not deployed
Operator: "Snippy's Letter Trace letters are not good/neat — fix them like you fixed the Mama's Roll Call tracing NUMBERS. Also build one activity that teaches tracing the COMPLETE alphabet (capitals + lowercase)."

**ROOT CAUSE of the ugly letters:** `snippy-activity.js` rendered the guide as STRAIGHT line segments (`'M'/'L'` polyline) over only 6–7 sparse points per glyph → jagged. The numbers were fixed (Mama) by storing DENSE points ON a smooth print-curve and rendering a **Catmull-Rom → cubic-Bézier spline through every point** (smooth professional glyph). Applied the same to letters.

**Part 1 — Snippy fix:** redrew all 12 glyphs in `snippy-core.js` as dense on-curve points (KEEPING each stroke's start coord + order + count so the homer/dot-follower anti-cheat skew is unchanged), and switched `snippy-activity.js`'s guide render to the `splinePath` Catmull-Rom helper. `verify-snippy-core.js` stayed GREEN (oracle 100%; homer 40% defeated-on-60%; dot-follower/scribbler 0%). Letters now smooth (eyeballed c/s/a/j/i). Cache-bust snippy-core?v=2 / snippy-activity?v=4.

**Part 2 — "Penny's Alphabet Trace" (L.K.1.a, Penny the pencil):** NEW `alphabet-trace-core.js` — all **52** glyphs (A–Z + a–z), built from **true ellipse arcs (`arc`) + sampled segments (`line`)** so round letters are guaranteed-smooth, rendered via the spline; strict-order on-path trace (`attemptStroke`/`traceScore` point-to-segment) + SOLVER gauntlet (oracle 52/52, scribble + out-of-order fail). `penny-alphabet-trace-activity.{js,html}`: always-guided spline-trace with baseline/midline writing guides + a CAPITAL/lowercase badge + next-stroke start dot; drag each stroke in order → letter inks → advance. 52-round manifest (A–Z band1, a–z band2). A 2nd L.K.1.a activity is operator-directed (Snippy = 12-letter formation-certify game; Penny = full A–Z+a–z handwriting practice).
KEY TECHNIQUE: build curved glyphs from parametric arcs/lines (not hand-eyeballed points) → professional, consistent curves. GLYPH-AESTHETICS reviewed via a 52-glyph contact sheet (`_penny-glyph-sheet` probe, removed) — all 52 neat. Verified: verify-alphabet-trace-core 52/52, local-test, visual-qa 240/240, preflight 132 manifests, findability (curl-confirmed both titles), eyeball phone 360 + desktop 768/1024. 0 protected-core/lcs-shell lines; footprint = snippy 3-file edit + new alphabet-trace-core/penny ×3 / 2 scripts + 1 activities.ts row.

Operator: "analyze Mama's Roll Call (count out 0–9) and build ONE more activity teaching tracing numbers, covering 0 to 9." The digit parallel of Penny's Alphabet Trace.

**Digby's Number Trace (K.CC.A.3, Digby the dog):** always-guided spline-trace of all 10 numerals 0–9 — smooth dashed guide numeral (Catmull-Rom spline) + baseline writing guide + next-stroke start dot; drag each stroke in order → the numeral inks in → advance. ## STORYBOOK APPENDIX (2026-07-01) — character + scene art for the interactive storybooks

The storybook player (`mini tools/storybook*.js`, PixiJS) ingests **packed
WebP atlases in the standard PixiJS spritesheet JSON format**. Deliver raw
PNGs per the layout below; `scripts/storybook/pack-atlas.js` packs them
(TexturePacker with the PixiJS exporter + WebP + max 2048 + trim is accepted
interchangeably — the JSON format is the contract). All CA5 rules above
(consistent registration, transparent background, 12fps clips) carry over.

### Deltas vs the activity-mascot spec
| Setting | Storybook value |
|---|---|
| Frame canvas | **512×640 portrait** (full-body; feet at bottom-center) |
| Poses | `pose_<name>.png` single frames — 2–5 per character; `pose_neutral` REQUIRED, `pose_talk` REQUIRED for narrators |
| Idle | `idle_0001..000N.png` — ONE loop, 6–10 frames, played at ~7fps |
| Clips | `clip_<name>_0001..00NN.png` — optional, 24–48 frames @12fps, ≤2 clips/character, ≤2 characters per story get clips (hero + celebration beats). Every clip must have a declared fallback pose in story.json (weak tablets + reduced-motion show it instead). |
| Registration | ONE source canvas size for ALL of a character's frames — the packer HARD-FAILS otherwise |
| Scenes | pre-composed **1600×1000** opaque PNG per page (packed → WebP q80, ≤200KB target / 300KB cap) |

### Delivery drop per story
```
art-drop/<story-id>/
  scenes/page-01.png … page-NN.png
  cast/<char>/pose_*.png, idle_####.png, clip_*_####.png
  audio/<locale>/<lineId>.mp3          (line IDs = strings.json keys)
```
Pack: `node scripts/storybook/pack-atlas.js --in art-drop/<id>/cast/<char> --name <char> --out "mini tools/stories/<id>/cast/<char>"`
and `--scene art-drop/<id>/scenes/page-01.png --out "mini tools/stories/<id>/scenes"`.
Placeholder art (`scripts/storybook/gen-placeholder-art.js`) is live until
real art lands — swapping is a pure asset replacement, zero code. Per-story
asset worklists: `docs/storybook/asset-worklist-<story>.md`.


## Character #77 — Pip the rabbit ("Number Line", tool #1)

**Where she appears:** the free-play teacher instrument `number-line` (TOOL #1), not an
activity — she is the marker, and she is the only thing on the line that moves. Set a start
and a hop length; every hop is then exactly that length, and she rides each drawn arc.

**Why a rabbit and not Hopper.** The frog was the operator's first instinct and it was
overruled on evidence: three frogs already hop lines here — Hopper in `skipcount-activity`
(2.NBT.A.2, lily pads, +5/+10 arc labels) and again in `hoppers-number-line-activity`
(2.MD.B.6, a ticked line), plus Lily in `ten-stones-activity` (0..20, coral +N arcs). Line
943 of this file records "Hopper's Lily Hops owns the hop/skip-count theme", and
`open-number-line.js:26` states in shipped code that "Hopper's frog is that activity's
character and is deliberately NOT reused here." No rabbit hops a line anywhere in the repo.

⭐ **GENDER: UNMARKED. OPERATOR-RULED 2026-08-03, AND IT IS A HARD ART CONSTRAINT, NOT A
PREFERENCE.** Pip is drawn with no gender cue of any kind, so each of the eleven locales can
take the grammatical gender its own language forces — which they already do, and which is
why this ruling costs zero string changes:

| locale | the rabbit | why |
|---|---|---|
| es / pt | `la coneja` / `a coelha` — feminine | natural, and the panels wrote every sentence to it |
| de | `der Hase` — masculine | `die Häsin` reads as biology, not classroom |
| it | `il coniglio` — masculine | no natural feminine for a story rabbit; `la coniglia` is not said |
| nl | `het konijn` — neuter | so the Dutch is written pronoun-light throughout |
| sv / da / no | feminine | and da/no must never write `hoppen`/`hoppene` — see the mare note below |
| fi | `kani` | Finnish has no grammatical gender |
| en | "she" | English's choice is free, so it takes one |

**What "unmarked" forbids, concretely, so the brief is checkable at delivery:** no bow, no
ribbon, no hair, no eyelashes, no blush or rouge, no skirt, apron or dress, no flower, no
bonnet, no colour-coding toward pink or blue. The face carries **an eye and a mouth, and
nothing else**. The placeholder already satisfies this — verified against a
poison-tested name ban over its seven drawn groups.

⚠ **AND NO CODE GATE CAN ENFORCE THIS ON THE DELIVERED ART.** A source scan cannot see a
PNG's gender presentation, so the control is this brief plus a human look at the sequence
before it is built. Do not let a later pass add a gate that claims to check it — a gate that
cannot fail is worse than no gate, because it certifies.

**Poses — the LEAN set plus one, because this character travels.**

| pose | behavior | loops? | frames @12fps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `idle` | gentle breathe + occasional blink, ears up | **LOOPS** | 12 (~1.0 s) |
| `crouch` | compresses, ears up, about to go | plays once, holds | 6 (~0.5 s) |
| `hop` | airborne, hind legs extended back, ears swept back | plays once, holds | 8 (~0.7 s) |
| `land` | absorbs, slight squash, ears up | plays once | 6 (~0.5 s) |
| `happy` | delighted wiggle, eyes happy-closed, ears up | plays once | 10 (~0.8 s) |

⚠ **Three instructions that decide whether the sprite works at all:**
1. **Draw her HOPPING IN PLACE.** The tool owns x and y — it samples the real arc path and
   places her on it every frame. Travel baked into the art moves her twice.
2. **ONE BASELINE ACROSS EVERY POSE**, at y=470 of 512. `crouch` and `land` may dip a pixel
   or two below it (they compress); `hop` rises. Nothing else may drift, or her feet leave
   the number line.
3. **Draw her FACING RIGHT.** Backward hops are a `scaleX(-1)` on a nested element.

**`happy` is used ONLY when the hops divided the line exactly.** At the wall *with a space
left over* she poses `land`, never `happy` — a delighted rabbit beside a leftover gap reads
to a six-year-old as "well done", which is a verdict, in a tool whose refuse #4 forbids
them. The Italian panel put it plainly: *se resta uno spazio, il coniglio non deve essere
contento — deve semplicemente essersi fermato.*

**Palette:** body `#A8CFC8`, outline `#146B5E`, inner ear + nose `#F2784B`, ink `#2A2A35`,
tail `#FFFDF7`. Warm K-3 register, no gradients on the character.

**Props / scene:** all code-drawn, no image-library dependency — the axis, ticks, the coral
hop arcs, the here-tick under her feet, the caliper. She is the only art asset.

**Today:** an inline SVG placeholder behind the `_setPose` / `[data-pose]` seam
(`number-line.js`, `_bunnySVG` + `_setPose`), following `echo-grove-activity.js:307`. The
sprite drops in at that seam with no other change.

**Build:** `node scripts/games/build-sprites.js --in <folder> --sheet pip --out "mini tools/sprites" --fps 12`
